WEB SCRAPER — CHROME EXTENSION
Roadmap / version history (kept up to date so future versions are never
planned from scratch again). Last updated: V1.13.2.

Architecture at a glance:
  manifest.json          MV3 manifest — permissions, version
  background/background.js   Service worker: Bulk Download queue (V1.5,
                              REUSED UNCHANGED by V1.12's Research Bundle
                              asset downloads), Scheduled Monitoring
                              (V1.8), Notifications on monitored-run
                              changes/errors (V1.9), per-run history log
                              entries (V1.10), the lastRunHasChanges flag
                              the V1.11 dashboard reads (V1.11) — V1.12
                              made ZERO changes to this file
  content/*.js            Scraper engine — selector.js, scraper.js,
                           content.js, domwait.js, pagination.js,
                           autodetect.js (untouched since V1.4 except one
                           tiny V1.3 addition to content.js)
  popup/popup.{html,css,js}   The whole UI
  utils/*.js               Pure, chrome-API-light logic modules shared by
                            popup and/or background: storage, runstate,
                            recipes (Saved Scrapers, incl. the V1.10
                            monitoring history log, the V1.11
                            lastRunHasChanges field, and the V1.12
                            research prefs), results (filter/sort/numeric
                            parsing), csv, xlsx, downloads, changes
                            (V1.6), snapshots (V1.6), transforms (V1.7),
                            research (V1.12, popup-only — Dataset ID
                            assignment, manifest shaping, dataset-info.json;
                            see the V1.12 section below)

No AI/LLM anywhere in this project. Every "smart" feature (Auto Detect,
image resolver, numeric parsing, change detection) is classical,
rule-based DOM/string logic, by explicit design choice.

============================================================
V1.0 — Base scraper
============================================================
- No-code element picker (click an element on the page to add a column)
- Manifest V3, vanilla JS/HTML/CSS, no frameworks, no bundler
- Container/row alignment model: pick a repeating "card", pick fields
  relative to it, extraction re-runs the container+relative selectors
  live against the page
- Selector engine priority: stable id > stable class > safe data-*/
  semantic attrs > parent-child > nth-child fallback
- CSV export

============================================================
V1.1 — Real-site hardening
============================================================
Fixed real bugs found testing on live sites:
- Shadow DOM support (Reddit's shreddit-* web components): shadow-crossing
  relative selectors (array-of-hops form), event.composedPath() for
  un-retargeted click resolution, closestAcrossShadow/queryFromScope
- findRepeatingContainer's "contiguous climb, keep outermost" fix (a
  class-less <td> pair no longer beats the real repeating <tr>)
- eBay anomaly handling: flag structurally-odd rows, never silently drop
  them (_wsAnomaly, simple threshold rules, no ML)
- CSV Excel-compatibility (BOM, CRLF, RFC4180 escaping)
- Real XLSX export (hand-rolled ZIP+OOXML, no external library)
- Image extraction v1: currentSrc/src/data-src fallback chain, basic
  placeholder detection
- URL normalization (relative -> absolute via document.baseURI)
- Text/Link/Image UX polish, preview pagination, missing-value display

============================================================
V1.2 — Saved Scrapers & results productivity
============================================================
- Saved Scrapers / Recipes (utils/recipes.js): CRUD, name/hostname/
  pathname matching, column config persisted to chrome.storage.local
  under `ws_saved_scrapers` (separate key from the V1.1 per-hostname
  `ws_state::<hostname>` working config)
- Remove Duplicates (entire-row or a chosen column)
- JSON export, Copy to Clipboard (TSV)
- Column management: rename/delete/reorder
- Filter (contains/equals/gt/lt/empty/... ) and Sort, with a locale-aware
  numeric parser (utils/results.js: parseNumeric) added specifically to
  make gt/lt and numeric sort work on "$19.99"-style values
- Results toolbar UX (chip buttons, inline panels)

============================================================
V1.3 — Auto Scroll / Multi-page / run state
============================================================
- Run modes: Current Page (default), Auto Scroll, Multi-page
- utils/runstate.js: pure run-state machine (status transitions, stop-
  condition evaluation, cross-page/cross-pass dedup, page-signature loop
  detection) — no chrome APIs, shared by content/pagination.js
- content/domwait.js: MutationObserver-based "has the page settled"
  detection (used by both scroll and page-navigation waits)
- content/pagination.js: drives the actual scroll/click-Next loop,
  persists live progress to chrome.storage.session under
  `ws_run::<hostname>` so the popup can close/reopen mid-run safely
- Multi-page needs to survive real page navigation, which requires a
  registered (not just on-demand-injected) content script for that
  origin — resolved via optional_host_permissions + a runtime
  chrome.permissions.request() prompt at Start (explicit user decision,
  not silently granted)
- Stop / Resume, live progress badge in the popup
- Saved Scraper gained mode/nextButtonConfig/dedupeKey/limits fields
  (backward-compatible additions)

============================================================
V1.4 — Smart Auto Detect
============================================================
- content/autodetect.js: classical-heuristics-only repeating-structure
  detection — NOT AI. Candidate container scoring (nav/footer penalty),
  field detection + naming (price/date/rating/count regex heuristics),
  confidence levels, preview-before-apply
- Sits ALONGSIDE manual Add Column, never replaces it; once applied, an
  Auto Detect column is a completely ordinary column (same rename/
  delete/reorder/filter/sort/export code path)

============================================================
V1.5 — Bulk Image/File Download
============================================================
- New permission: "downloads" (install-time, the only genuinely required
  new permission)
- utils/downloads.js: URL validation (rejects javascript:/chrome:/file:/
  vbscript:, blob: explicitly unsupported rather than half-worked-around),
  dedup, file-type detection, filename templating ({ColumnName}/{index}),
  sanitization, collision resolution (name_2.ext)
- background/background.js gained the whole download orchestration:
  concurrency-limited queue (default 4), chrome.downloads.onChanged
  tracking, Stop (cancels ONLY this run's own downloadIds, never touches
  other downloads or completed files), Retry Failed
- Real production bug found & fixed here: unserialized read-modify-write
  on chrome.storage.session under concurrent onChanged events — fixed
  with a promise-chain operation queue (serializeDownloadOp), the same
  pattern V1.8 reuses for monitoring (serializeMonitoringOp)
- Filtered-vs-entire-dataset download scope choice
- Saved Scraper gained downloadColumn/filenameTemplate/folderName/
  skipDuplicates fields

============================================================
V1.5.1 — Image extraction robustness (unversioned hotfix)
============================================================
Real eBay test found only 4/62 rows had an image (root cause: a single
relativeSelector doesn't generalize across structurally-varying cards,
plus a too-narrow lazy-load-attribute list). Fixed generically (NOT
eBay-specific hostname checks):
- srcset parsing (best-of by w/x descriptor), <picture>/<source> support
- Wider lazy-load attribute fallback chain, 1x1-transparent-GIF and
  generic placeholder-keyword detection
- Card-relative fallback: if the exact relativeSelector doesn't match in
  a given row, score every <img> actually present in THAT row's own
  container (size, visibility, generic icon/avatar keyword penalties) —
  never borrows another row's image, row alignment always preserved
- Reused (not duplicated) via utils/downloads.js's URL-fragment dedup
  normalization, later reused again by V1.6's changes.js

============================================================
V1.6 — Snapshots & Change Detection
============================================================
- utils/snapshots.js: chrome.storage.local CRUD for dataset snapshots
  (`ws_snapshots` key), name-keyed rows (resilient to column-id churn
  across re-scrapes), default retention of 10 snapshots per Saved
  Scraper (or per hostname+pathname for an ad-hoc snapshot), storage-
  size warning as it approaches chrome.storage.local's quota
- utils/changes.js: pure O(n) comparison via key->row Maps (never
  O(n^2)). Unique row key priority: user-chosen column > a Link/URL-like
  column > Entire Row fallback (Entire Row's own limitation — any field
  change looks like remove+new — is intentional and documented, no fuzzy
  matching). Duplicate-key detection warns rather than silently
  mismatching (deterministic last-row-wins, never random)
- Field-level diffs, price-change detection (reuses utils/results.js's
  parseNumeric, no new number parser), PRICE DROPPED/INCREASED
  categorization with delta + percent
- Comparison UI: New/Removed/Changed/Price Changes tabs, click-to-expand
  field detail, Export Changes (CSV/XLSX/JSON, row-per-change schema)
- Saved Scraper gained compareKey (a column NAME, not id — see
  changes.js's header comment for why)

============================================================
V1.7 — Data Cleaning / Transform pipeline
============================================================
- utils/transforms.js: non-destructive pipeline. rawRows is NEVER
  mutated; only an ordered transform CONFIG list is stored, replayed
  fresh from raw every time (applyTransforms). Pipeline order:
  RAW -> TRANSFORMS -> FILTER -> DEDUPE -> SORT -> display/export
- Operations: Trim, Collapse Whitespace, Remove Currency, Extract Number,
  Normalize Number, Find & Replace, Regex Replace, Regex Extract, Change
  Case, Prefix/Suffix, Fill Empty, Normalize URL, Remove Tracking
  Parameters, Strip HTML (DOMParser, no script execution), Substring,
  Split Column, Combine Columns
- Split/Combine are STRUCTURAL (change the column list, not just values)
  — introduced the "effectiveColumns()" concept: everything downstream
  (Filter/Sort/Dedupe/Export/Download/Snapshot) reads the post-transform
  column list, not the scraper's original one
- utils/results.js refactored (behavior-preserving) to expose
  normalizeNumericString so Normalize Number reuses the EXACT SAME
  locale-disambiguation rules Filter/Sort already had, instead of a
  second parser
- Preview-before-apply on every operation; Undo Last / Reset Transforms
  / per-step Remove
- Saved Scraper gained transforms[]/autoApplyTransforms ("Apply saved
  transforms automatically", default on, always user-controlled).
  "Current filtered rows"-scoped transforms are deliberately NOT
  persisted to a Saved Scraper (row-position-dependent, not portable)
- Snapshot/Compare policy decision (V1.6 behavior change, done openly,
  not silently): snapshots now capture the TRANSFORMED dataset, not raw
  — cuts down "$19.99" vs "19.99" format-only false positives

============================================================
V1.8 — Scheduled / Background Monitoring
============================================================
Goal: let a Saved Scraper re-run itself automatically on a schedule and
auto-snapshot + compare, using chrome.alarms + the background service
worker. No notifications yet (reserved for a later version). No cloud
sync/accounts/payments/AI — explicitly out of scope for this version.

New permission: "alarms" (the only new one; chrome.tabs.create/remove/
onUpdated need no separate "tabs" permission since the tab URL is always
supplied by the extension itself, never read back cross-origin).

- Saved Scraper gained:
    lastKnownUrl   — full URL WITH query string (captured at save/update
                     time), used only to reopen the right page for a
                     scheduled run — hostname/pathname alone (used for
                     interactive page-match warnings) deliberately ignore
                     the query string, which would break e.g. an eBay
                     search scraper's specific query on revisit
    monitoring     — {enabled, intervalMinutes, lastRunAt, nextRunAt,
                     lastRunStatus, lastRunSummary, lastError}
  via two new recipes.js functions: setMonitoringConfig (config changes,
  bumps updatedAt) and updateMonitoringStatus (run OUTCOME only, never
  touches columns/containerSelector/transforms/snapshots — this is
  structurally why a failed run can never destroy prior state: the
  snapshot-writing step simply never runs on a failure path)
- Practical intervals only, matching chrome.alarms' real granularity:
  Hourly / Every 6 hours / Every 12 hours / Daily (60/360/720/1440 min)
- background.js: chrome.alarms.onAlarm (name `ws_monitor::<scraperId>`)
  drives runScheduledScrape(), which:
    1. opens the scraper's lastKnownUrl in a new, INACTIVE tab (never
       steals focus, never touches the user's other tabs)
    2. waits for load, injects the same content scripts the popup would
    3. reuses the EXACT SAME messages the popup sends interactively —
       RUN_EXTRACTION for current-page; START_AUTO_SCROLL/
       START_MULTI_PAGE + watching the same ws_run::<hostname> session
       key for the other two modes. content/*.js needed ZERO changes —
       it has never cared whether a popup or the background worker asked
    4. replays the scraper's saved transforms if autoApplyTransforms is
       on (same utils/transforms.js the popup uses)
    5. saves a snapshot and compares it against the previous one (same
       utils/snapshots.js + utils/changes.js, same policies)
    6. records ONLY the outcome; always closes the tab it opened
  A background-created tab is NOT covered by activeTab (that only ever
  follows a direct user gesture on the CURRENTLY ACTIVE tab), so a real,
  persistent host permission is a hard platform requirement here — it's
  requested via chrome.permissions.request() at the moment the user
  clicks "Enable" in the popup (a genuine user gesture); background.js
  itself only ever calls the gesture-free chrome.permissions.contains()
  and fails a run cleanly (never silently) if it's missing/revoked
- Manual "Run Now" (RUN_MONITORED_NOW message) runs the identical code
  path immediately, fire-and-forget from the popup's point of view —
  progress reflects live via chrome.storage.onChanged on
  `ws_saved_scrapers`, same pattern as V1.3 run progress / V1.5 downloads
- A monitoring op queue (serializeMonitoringOp, same pattern as V1.5's
  serializeDownloadOp) prevents concurrent scheduled runs for different
  scrapers from clobbering each other's status writes to the shared
  ws_saved_scrapers blob
- Stale-alarm self-heal (a deleted scraper's lingering alarm clears
  itself on next fire) and stale-"running"-status self-heal (a status
  stuck at "running" because the browser/extension restarted mid-run
  resets to a clear error on next startup, rather than lying forever)
- Popup: new "📡 Monitoring" section listing every Saved Scraper with
  Enable(+interval)/Disable/Run Now and a compact status line (last run,
  next run, +new/-removed/~changed summary or the last error)

Known V1.8 limitations (by design, not oversight):
- Multi-minute Auto Scroll/Multi-page monitored runs rely on
  chrome.storage.onChanged (event-driven, wakes a suspended service
  worker) plus a setTimeout timeout guard; an MV3 service worker can in
  principle be killed mid-wait on an extremely long run — mitigated by
  the stale-"running"-status self-heal on next browser/extension start,
  not eliminated (a known, general MV3 platform constraint, not specific
  to this feature)
- Disabling monitoring does not revoke the host permission it was
  granted under (avoids a repeat prompt if re-enabled soon after) —
  deliberate choice, not a bug

V1.8 real-Chrome bug found & fixed post-release (two rounds):
- Round 1: WSRecipes.persist() never checked chrome.runtime.lastError, so
  a storage write failure was silently treated as success everywhere in
  recipes.js — fixed by making persist() reject on a real error, with
  every caller (saveScraper/updateScraper/setMonitoringConfig/
  updateMonitoringStatus/renameScraper/deleteScraper) surfacing
  {ok:false, error} instead of pretending the write landed. Also added a
  render-generation guard in popup.js's renderMonitoringSection() (it's
  triggered from two independent places — an explicit post-action call
  AND a chrome.storage.onChanged listener — with no ordering guarantee
  between them) so a slower, earlier-started render can never paint
  stale data over a newer one that already landed.
- Round 2 (the ACTUAL root cause of the reported symptom): the disabled-
  state renderer simply never showed Last run/summary/next run AT ALL —
  a UI design gap, not a data-loss bug (the stored monitoring record was
  correct throughout, snapshots were never touched). Fixed by having the
  disabled card still show "Monitoring disabled" + Last run + summary/
  error (only "Next run" hides, since no alarm exists while disabled).
  Also fixed nextRunAt going stale across a Disable/Enable cycle:
  SET_MONITORING now recomputes nextRunAt from the actual NEW
  chrome.alarms entry on every enable (was previously left untouched,
  showing the old pre-Disable schedule) and clears it to null on
  disable. Confirmed fixed in real Chrome: full Enable -> Run Now ->
  Disable -> Enable (and rapid repeats) preserves history and shows a
  fresh Next run.

============================================================
V1.9 — Notifications on monitored-run completion/changes
============================================================
Goal (the item V1.8's own "not yet built" list named as the next
candidate): a native chrome.notifications alert when a monitored run
(scheduled OR manual "Run Now") finds real changes or fails — not on
every routine successful check with zero changes, which would defeat
the point.

New permission: "notifications" (the only new one).

- Saved Scraper's monitoring object gained:
    notifyOnChanges: boolean — per-scraper opt-out, defaults true (same
      "on by default, always user-controlled" pattern as
      autoApplyTransforms/skipDuplicates elsewhere in this project)
  via recipes.js's existing setMonitoringConfig (now also accepts a
  notifyOnChanges patch field) — no schema-breaking change, a pre-V1.9
  record simply lacks the field and every read treats it as true via the
  same "!== false" defaulting convention used throughout this codebase.
- background.js's runScheduledScrape() calls notifyMonitoringResult()
  after every run:
    - a successful run with previousSnapshot present AND
      new/removed/changed > 0 -> notifies with the compact change
      summary as the message
    - a successful run with ZERO changes -> silent (no notification)
    - the very first snapshot (no previousSnapshot to compare against)
      -> silent (nothing to report as "changed")
    - any failure (extraction error, missing host permission, etc.) ->
      always notifies, high priority, a visually distinct (⚠) title
  Notification id is `ws_monitor_notify::<scraperId>` (stable per
  scraper, so a later notification replaces rather than stacks) and uses
  chrome.runtime.getURL('icons/icon128.png') for its icon. A failure to
  display the notification itself is best-effort — never breaks or
  fails the monitoring run.
- New SET_NOTIFY message (deliberately SEPARATE from SET_MONITORING):
  toggling notifyOnChanges on an already-enabled scraper must NEVER
  recreate the chrome.alarms entry or recompute nextRunAt the way
  SET_MONITORING's enable path does — SET_NOTIFY touches
  notifyOnChanges only, nothing else.
- Popup: a 🔔 checkbox in the Monitoring section, default checked —
  shown next to the interval select before Enable (its value is sent
  along with Enable), and inline on an already-enabled row (toggling it
  sends SET_NOTIFY immediately, live-persisted).

Known V1.9 limitations (by design):
- No "notify me even with zero changes" option — only changes/errors,
  keeping the feature aligned with its stated purpose
- No notification click-to-open-popup action (chrome.action.openPopup()
  has version/context restrictions not worth the complexity for this
  scope) — clicking a notification just dismisses it, standard Chrome
  behavior
- Real chrome.notifications delivery/OS-level display was NOT verified
  in real Chrome as of this entry — validated only via mocked
  chrome.notifications.create in automated tests; needs a real-Chrome
  pass before being treated as fully confirmed working (see this
  version's manual verification steps)

============================================================
V1.10 — Monitoring History / Activity Log
============================================================
Goal: keep a per-scraper log of past monitoring runs (not just the
latest one) so the user can see a trend, not just a single snapshot of
"last run" — a small, reviewable History panel inside the existing
Monitoring card. No new permission.

- Saved Scraper's monitoring object gained:
    history: Array<{ at, status, totalRows, newCount, removedCount,
                      changedCount, error }>
  Newest-first (unshift on append), capped at MONITOR_HISTORY_LIMIT (50)
  per scraper — the oldest entry silently drops off once the cap is
  exceeded, no separate pruning step needed. A pre-V1.10 record simply
  lacks the field; every read defaults it to [] (same backward-compat
  convention used throughout this codebase).
- recipes.js: updateMonitoringStatus(id, patch) accepts an optional
  historyEntry field and appends it (unshift + cap) in the SAME persist()
  call as the existing lastRunStatus/lastRunAt/summary/error write — one
  storage write per run outcome, not two. The transient "running" status
  write (no historyEntry passed) does NOT touch history, so a run that's
  still in progress never appears as a phantom log entry.
  New clearMonitoringHistory(id): empties history ONLY — verified (unit
  + integration tests) to leave lastRunAt/lastRunStatus/lastRunSummary/
  enabled/intervalMinutes/notifyOnChanges/columns/containerSelector/
  transforms/snapshots completely untouched. Uses the same
  serializeMonitoringOp queue as every other monitoring-mutating message,
  so it can never race a concurrent scheduled run's status write.
- background.js: runScheduledScrape()'s success path now computes the
  same newCount/removedCount/changedCount used for the notification
  summary (V1.9) into a historyEntry — no duplicate comparison logic, one
  utils/changes.js call feeds both the human-readable summary string AND
  the structured history entry. A first-ever run (no previousSnapshot)
  logs totalRows as newCount, 0/0 for removed/changed, matching "every
  row is new". Failure paths (missing host permission, extraction error)
  log status:'error' with all four row-count fields left null rather
  than fabricated zeros — the popup renders these entries as an error
  line instead of a stats line.
  New CLEAR_MONITORING_HISTORY message handler, routed through
  serializeMonitoringOp like every other monitoring op.
- Popup: a "History" button appears on a scraper's Monitoring row only
  once it has at least one history entry (an empty log shows no button
  at all, rather than an empty panel). Clicking it toggles an inline
  panel listing every entry, newest first, in the exact format:
    21 Ağu 19:28  SUCCESS
    60 rows | +2 new | -1 removed | ~3 changed
  (date/time formatting reuses the same formatMonitorTimestamp() the
  existing Last-run line already used — locale-aware, no new formatter).
  An error entry shows "⚠ <error message>" instead of a stats line. A
  "Clear History" button inside the panel asks for confirmation first
  (confirm()), and on accept sends CLEAR_MONITORING_HISTORY and closes
  the panel — declining leaves the log and the panel exactly as they
  were. The panel's open/closed state is tracked per-scraper-id in
  popup.js so re-rendering (e.g. from a chrome.storage.onChanged tick
  mid-run) doesn't unexpectedly collapse an open panel.
- History survives everything the underlying ws_saved_scrapers blob
  already survives: popup close/reopen, Chrome restart, a full Disable
  -> Enable cycle (Disable never touches history, only enabled/
  nextRunAt), and an extension reload while storage persists — no new
  storage key was introduced, so no new persistence surface exists.

Known V1.10 limitations (by design):
- History is per-scraper, not a single cross-scraper timeline — matches
  how the rest of the Monitoring card is already organized (one row per
  scraper), no separate "Activity" screen was introduced
- No export of history itself (CSV/XLSX/JSON) — only the dataset/changes
  already have export paths (V1.6); revisit only if asked for
- Cap is a fixed 50, not user-configurable — kept simple; matches the
  fixed-10-snapshots-per-scraper precedent from V1.6

============================================================
V1.11 — Monitoring Dashboard / Summary
============================================================
Goal: make monitoring useful once several Saved Scrapers are being
watched at once — a compact aggregate view (how many active/succeeding/
changed/erroring) plus a quick way to jump straight to the ones that
need attention, without opening every card's History individually. No
new permission.

- Saved Scraper's monitoring object gained:
    lastRunHasChanges: boolean — whether the latest COMPLETED run found
      at least one new/removed/changed row. A field of its own, parallel
      to lastRunStatus/lastRunSummary/lastError, deliberately NOT derived
      from history[0] — history is user-clearable (V1.10's Clear
      History) and clearing it must never make "did the last run find
      changes" disappear too. background.js sets it explicitly (true or
      false) on every finished run, success or error, so it never lingers
      stale from an older run. A pre-V1.11 record simply lacks it and
      reads as false (same backward-compat convention as every other
      additive field in this project) — see this version's dedicated
      backward-compatibility test for the exact behavior: a V1.10 record
      with a "+4 new" summary but no lastRunHasChanges renders SUCCESS,
      never a fabricated CHANGED.
  Set via recipes.js's existing updateMonitoringStatus (now also accepts
  a hasChanges patch field) — no schema-breaking change, no new storage
  key.
- Card status (the single source of truth for the badge, the Summary
  counters, sorting, AND filtering — one function, not four separate
  rules that could drift from each other) is exactly one of four values,
  computed from lastRunStatus + lastRunHasChanges:
    ERROR    — latest completed run failed
    CHANGED  — latest completed run succeeded AND found changes (takes
               precedence over SUCCESS per spec — a successful run with
               changes is never shown as plain SUCCESS)
    SUCCESS  — latest completed run succeeded with zero changes
    NEVER RUN — no completed run yet (also covers a run currently
               in-progress with no prior completed run to fall back on;
               a currently-running card keeps its own distinct RUNNING
               badge in the meantime — unchanged since V1.8 — and simply
               isn't reclassified into one of these four until the run
               actually finishes, matching spec #11's "update
               immediately after Run Now COMPLETES", not while it's
               still running)
- Popup: a "Monitoring Summary" block above the scraper list —
    4 Active | 3 Success | 1 Changed | 0 Errors
  Active/Success/Changed/Errors are all scoped to ENABLED scrapers only
  (spec #3's "latest monitoring result of each enabled scraper") — a
  disabled scraper's leftover run outcome still shows on its own card
  (unchanged from V1.10 — disabling never erases history/last-run info)
  but doesn't count toward the aggregate. Recomputed on every
  renderMonitoringSection() call, so it's never a separately-cached
  number that could drift from what the cards actually show.
- Popup: All / Changed / Errors filter chips above the list, applied to
  EVERY monitoring card (not just enabled ones — a disabled-but-CHANGED
  card still shows under "Changed", since a filter answers "which cards
  currently read this way", a different question from the Summary's
  "how many active schedules are in this state"). A filter matching
  nothing shows a small "No monitoring cards match this filter" message
  rather than a silently empty list. The active filter is a live
  in-popup preference, not persisted — it resets to "All" on next open by
  design, so a stale filter can never quietly hide new activity.
- Popup: cards are sorted by attention priority — ERROR, then CHANGED,
  then SUCCESS, then NEVER RUN; within a group, newest Last run first —
  computed with the same card-status function the badges/Summary/filters
  use, so the four presentations can never disagree with each other.
- Popup: every card now always shows a status badge (ERROR/CHANGED/
  SUCCESS/NEVER RUN) — V1.10 showed no badge at all for a never-run
  scraper; V1.11 makes NEVER RUN an explicit, visible state instead of an
  absence. Badges are never color-only: the label text (SUCCESS/CHANGED/
  ERROR/NEVER RUN) is always rendered, color is a secondary cue.
- Everything a V1.10 card already showed — name, interval, Last run,
  Next run, latest change summary, Run Now, Disable, the 🔔 notification
  toggle, History — is unchanged; V1.11 only adds the badge/Summary/
  filter/sort layer on top.
- Live updates: Summary/filters/sort are recomputed inside
  renderMonitoringSection() itself, which was already wired (since V1.8)
  to fire after every Run Now / Enable / Disable action AND on every
  chrome.storage.onChanged event for ws_saved_scrapers (from this popup
  or background.js) — so the dashboard needed no new update-triggering
  plumbing of its own to satisfy "no manual refresh required" (spec
  #11); it simply reads current data every time that already-existing
  trigger fires, including the very first render when the popup opens.

Known V1.11 limitations (by design):
- No per-scraper "acknowledge" or "dismiss" action for a CHANGED/ERROR
  card — clearing that state means either checking History or letting
  the next monitored run supersede it; kept simple, matches spec scope
- Filter is All/Changed/Errors only, no separate "Success" or "Never Run"
  chip — spec's stated purpose is finding scrapers that need attention,
  which those two states inherently don't
- A card mid-run (RUNNING) is excluded from ERROR/CHANGED/SUCCESS/NEVER
  RUN classification for the few seconds/minutes the run takes — it
  reappears in its correct group the instant the run completes

============================================================
V1.12 — Research Dataset Builder / Export Bundle
============================================================
Goal: turn scraped results + downloaded images/files into a structured,
portable research dataset — manifest.csv/xlsx/json + dataset-info.json +
images/ + files/, all under one Research/<dataset name>/ folder — where
every row keeps a stable, permanent link to its own downloaded asset(s),
even when an asset is missing, fails, or is a duplicate of another row's.
No new permission (chrome.downloads was already granted in V1.5).

- New utils/research.js (popup-only, pure/chrome-API-free): assigns
  sequential zero-padded Dataset IDs (0001, 0002, ... — widens past 4
  digits only once a dataset actually needs it) fresh over WHATEVER row
  set is handed to it, so a filtered-scope bundle gets its own clean
  0001..NN range rather than sparse IDs inherited from a larger dataset;
  plans image/file asset queues by reusing WSDownloads.buildDownloadQueue
  VERBATIM (spec #13 — no second downloader anywhere in this version);
  shapes manifest columns/rows and dataset-info.json.
- Dataset IDs and asset filenames are 100% DETERMINISTIC — computed once,
  up front, from Dataset ID + a chosen "name" column (reusing
  pickTitleColumnName(), the same heuristic Snapshots already uses), via
  the EXACT SAME {ColumnName}-template + sanitizeFilename() machinery
  V1.5 already has (a synthetic "Dataset ID" pseudo-column is injected
  into a COPY of each row so the existing template syntax can address
  it). This is what makes spec #6/#7/#16 all fall out for free: a row's
  filename is known and shown in the manifest whether or not its asset
  ever actually downloads, and Retry Failed can never produce a
  different ID or filename than the original attempt — only the Asset
  Status changes.
- Duplicate image/file URLs across different rows (spec #19): resolved
  by REUSE, not a second copy — WSDownloads' own dedup already skips
  queuing a second network download for a URL it's already seen, and
  research.js maps every row (queued or not) to the correct filename by
  normalizeUrlForDedup-keying against whichever item DID get queued, so
  two rows sharing one image URL always resolve to the identical
  filename in the manifest with only one download ever happening.
- Manifest rows never drop a row (spec #7): a row with a missing,
  invalid, or not-yet-downloaded asset still gets a full manifest row,
  just with an empty filename field and an explanatory 4-value "Asset
  Status" (Downloaded/Failed/Missing/Skipped — spec #18; split into
  separate Image/File Asset Status columns when both are included, since
  a single shared column would be ambiguous when the two independently
  differ). Values already reflect V1.7 TRANSFORMED data, never re-parsed
  or re-formatted by research.js itself (spec #9) — it only ever copies
  through whatever value the results pipeline handed it.
- Asset downloading reuses background.js's EXISTING chrome.downloads
  queue completely unchanged (START_DOWNLOAD_RUN/STOP_DOWNLOAD_RUN/
  RETRY_FAILED_DOWNLOADS/ws_download_run, the same infrastructure V1.5's
  Bulk Download has used since it shipped) — each asset item's filename
  is simply prefixed "images/" or "files/" before being queued, so the
  SAME folderName+filename concatenation background.js already does
  lands it directly in the right Research/<dataset>/images|files/
  subfolder with zero background.js changes (sanitizeFolderName has
  always supported a "/" in a filename for exactly this "optional
  sub-folder" case). A new activeDownloadPurpose popup.js flag
  ('bulk'|'research'|null) keeps the plain Bulk Download panel and the
  Research Bundle panel from ever rendering over each other's progress,
  since both features now share this one ws_download_run slot (there has
  only ever been a single download-run slot, since V1.5 — this was
  already an implicit constraint, V1.12 just made it explicit).
- Manifest files (manifest.csv/xlsx/json, dataset-info.json) are NOT
  scraped content — they're the extension's own generated output — so
  they're written via a direct chrome.downloads.download() call from the
  popup itself (a privileged extension page, exactly like background.js
  — see that file's header comment) using a blob: URL, the same
  technique the plain CSV/XLSX/JSON Export buttons already use, just
  through the downloads API (conflictAction:'overwrite') instead of an
  <a download> click so the result lands in the Research subfolder
  rather than the default Downloads location. They're written ONCE the
  asset run reaches a terminal state (not up front), so the Asset Status
  column always reflects a real outcome — and Retry Failed re-triggers
  this same finalize step, regenerating the manifest in place with the
  updated statuses (never accumulating manifest_2.csv-style copies).
- Popup UI: a "📦 Create Research Bundle" button in the Results toolbar
  opens a setup panel (dataset name, Include CSV/Excel/JSON/Images/Files,
  image/file column pickers, a filtered-vs-full-dataset scope choice
  shown only when a filter/sort/dedupe is active — same "Current filtered
  results" vs "Entire dataset" pattern V1.5's own download scope already
  uses, defaulting to filtered when active per spec #8) followed by a
  "Creating Research Bundle" progress panel (Rows/Images/Files counts,
  live asset download progress, Failed count, Stop/Retry Failed) that
  becomes a "Research Bundle Complete" summary (rows, images downloaded/
  failed, manifest file count, folder path) with Retry Failed / Done.
- Saved Scraper gained (spec #21, additive — a scraper saved under V1.11
  or earlier simply lacks `research` and the panel computes fresh
  defaults instead):
    research: { datasetNameTemplate, includeCsv/Xlsx/Json,
      includeImages/Files, imageColumnId, fileColumnId, filenameTemplate }
  via a new setResearchPrefs() in recipes.js (same narrow-patch shape as
  setMonitoringConfig) — persisted automatically whenever a bundle is
  created while a Saved Scraper is loaded, restored the next time that
  scraper is loaded and the panel is opened.
- Defaults exactly match spec #3: Excel ON, JSON ON, CSV OFF, Images ON
  only when an Image (src-attribute) column actually exists, Files OFF
  regardless of whether a Link column exists.
- Spec #22 (complete separation from monitoring/snapshots): verified,
  not just asserted — research.js never imports or calls WSSnapshots or
  any WSRecipes monitoring.* function, setResearchPrefs touches only the
  `research` object, and the regression suite includes an explicit check
  that building a full bundle (including a Retry Failed pass) never
  sends a single SET_MONITORING/SET_NOTIFY/CLEAR_MONITORING_HISTORY/
  RUN_MONITORED_NOW message and never creates a ws_snapshots entry.

Known V1.12 limitations (by design):
- Only one Research Bundle (or Bulk Download) can run at a time — they
  share the single ws_download_run slot that has existed since V1.5;
  starting one while the other is active would take over that slot
  rather than running both concurrently
- No per-row "Asset Status" for files when only Images are included, and
  vice versa — the column is simply omitted for whichever asset type
  isn't included in that particular bundle, matching "optional" in spec
  #18
- The Dataset folder name is sanitized as a single segment (spec #11) —
  a name that would otherwise clash with an existing folder is handled
  by Chrome's own download-uniquify behavior for the asset queue, but
  manifest files deliberately use conflictAction:'overwrite' (regenerate
  in place), not uniquify — see this section's finalize-step note above

============================================================
V1.13 — UI/UX Consolidation / Productization Pass
============================================================
Goal: the popup had grown into one very long single-column scroll across
12 versions of additive features. V1.13 reorganizes it into 4 tabs
without rewriting, removing, or behaviorally changing any V1.1–V1.12
feature — a pure presentation-layer refactor. No new permission, no
content/*.js or background.js changes (one small popup.js bugfix aside
— see "Bugs found" below).

New tab layout:
  SCRAPE   — Saved Scrapers, Columns, Auto Detect, Run Mode (+ Auto
             Scroll/Multi-page settings), Run Status, Preview/Reset
  RESULTS  — the results table, grouped toolbars (Data: Transform/
             Filter/Sort/Remove Duplicates; Export: CSV/Excel/JSON/Copy;
             Assets: Download Images/Files; Change Tracking: Snapshots),
             and the Changes/Compare screen
  MONITOR  — the V1.11 Monitoring Summary/filters/dashboard cards
             (moved out of the scrape flow entirely — column
             configuration is never shown here, per spec #4)
  RESEARCH — the V1.12 Research Dataset Builder, promoted to its own
             tab (previously embedded inside Results) with a "Rows
             available / Image coverage" status line and its CTA hidden
             (not shown disabled) until there's actually data to build a
             bundle from

Implementation approach (deliberately the lowest-risk option, per spec
#22/#23's "incremental, not risky rewrite"): every existing section/
element keeps its EXACT id, event listener, and render function — V1.13
only moved WHERE each section's HTML lives (into one of 4
`.ws-tab-panel[data-tab-panel]` wrappers) and added a small
switchTab()/updateResultsEmptyState()/updateResearchTabState() layer on
top. Switching tabs only ever toggles `.hidden` on existing DOM nodes —
it never re-renders, re-fetches, or clears anything, which is what makes
spec #19 ("columns/results/transforms/filters/monitoring/research
settings must survive tab switching") true by construction rather than
by a special case that has to be kept in sync. Verified directly: every
id popup.js references via getElementById was cross-checked against the
restructured HTML (none missing, none duplicated).

- Empty states added/aligned to spec #6's wording: Results ("No results
  yet. Configure a scraper and run Preview."), Research ("No data yet.
  Scrape a page first." — also hides the Create Research Bundle button
  entirely rather than showing it disabled), Monitor's existing V1.10
  message reworded to match ("No monitored scrapers yet. Save a scraper
  and enable monitoring."); Scrape's existing V1.1 "No columns yet..."
  message reused as-is.
- Last-active tab is remembered via one isolated chrome.storage.session
  key (`ws_active_tab`) and restored on reopen (spec #20) — a read/write
  failure on that one key falls back to the Scrape tab rather than
  blocking init().
- Collapsible "Advanced" `<details>` sections added for the most
  clearly-secondary settings named in spec #14: Auto Scroll/Multi-page
  row/scroll/page limits, Bulk Download's filename template/folder/
  duplicate-handling, Research Bundle's image/file column pickers,
  Snapshots' compare-key selector. Deliberately NOT applied everywhere
  spec #14 named as a candidate (e.g. dedupe strategy) — scoped to what
  could be wrapped with zero risk to existing tests/behavior, per the
  "incremental, not risky" instruction.
- Toolbar group labels (Data/Export/Assets/Change Tracking) added above
  the Results tab's existing button rows per spec #3's exact grouping;
  Copy moved next to CSV/Excel/JSON (Export group) and Snapshots moved
  under its own Change Tracking group — both pure reordering, same
  buttons/handlers.
- setStatus() gained an optional third `type` argument
  ('success'|'warning'|'error'|'running', spec #11) — fully additive:
  every pre-V1.13 call site still passes just (msg, isError) and renders
  IDENTICALLY to before. A handful of representative call sites (preview
  scanning, download/bundle starting, a bundle-format validation
  warning, an export/enable success) were updated to the fuller
  vocabulary as a demonstration of the pattern; a full sweep of every
  setStatus call site was deliberately NOT done (spec #22's "do not pile
  more logic in solely for aesthetics" / avoid a risky wide-reaching
  edit for cosmetic-only gain).
- Results table (spec #10): sticky header, horizontal scroll, cell
  truncation with a `title` tooltip, and "Showing first N of M rows"
  already existed since V1.1/V1.2 — confirmed still intact, and the row
  counts in that note now use toLocaleString() for exactly the spec's
  own "1,248"-style example.
- Tab buttons are real `<button role="tab">` elements with
  `aria-selected`, the active tab is marked with both a visual
  underline/color AND `aria-selected="true"` (never color-only, spec
  #16), and every status badge already carried its text label from
  earlier versions (V1.11 already made this a hard rule for monitoring
  badges specifically).

Bug found & fixed while implementing this (not new in V1.13, but
surfaced by testing the tab-reopen path more thoroughly than V1.12's own
suite had): V1.12 added an `activeDownloadPurpose` gate to
`renderDownloadProgress()`/`renderResearchProgress()` so the plain Bulk
Download panel and the Research Bundle panel could never paint over each
other's progress (they share one `ws_download_run` slot). That gate
defaults to `null` on a fresh popup load — so the existing "if a
download run is already in progress when the popup reopens, show it
immediately" restore path (working since V1.5) was silently broken for
ALL restores, not just Research Bundle ones: `renderDownloadProgress`
would simply never render because the purpose was never set. Fixed by
inferring the purpose from the restored run's folderName (a Research
Bundle's always starts with "Web Scraper/Research/") before restoring,
and added a dedicated regression test for exactly this scenario so it
can't quietly regress again.

Known V1.13 limitations (unchanged from V1.12, not introduced by this
version): a Research Bundle's in-memory plan (columns/rows/Dataset IDs/
filename maps) still isn't persisted, so a popup closed mid-asset-
download and reopened before it finishes can't show that bundle's live
progress or auto-write its manifest in the new popup instance — the
downloads themselves are unaffected and still complete via
background.js. This existed since V1.12; V1.13 only made the popup
degrade cleanly (no stuck state) instead of silently breaking Bulk
Download in the same session.

V1.13 real-Chrome bug found & fixed post-release: after running a Saved
Scraper (62 rows on a real eBay search) and confirming Results showed
them, opening the Research tab still said "No data yet. Scrape a page
first." Root cause: switchTab() only called
updateResearchTabState()/updateResultsEmptyState() when the TARGET tab
was 'research'/'results' respectively — correct for the direct paths
V1.13's own test suite exercised, but it meant every OTHER
rawRows-populating code path (a Saved Scraper's current-page Run,
View Results after a completed Auto Scroll/Multi-page run) had to
separately remember to keep Research in sync, rather than it being
structurally guaranteed. Fixed by having switchTab() unconditionally
resync BOTH checks on every switch, regardless of target tab — Research
and Results can now never disagree with rawRows (the one real source of
truth; no separate Research-specific dataset was ever introduced, before
or after this fix) no matter which tab sequence got you there. Verified
with a dedicated regression test reproducing the exact reported sequence
(Run -> Results -> Research) plus two more (Results -> Monitor ->
Research, Results -> Scrape -> Research) confirming the dataset survives
any detour.

============================================================
V1.13.1 — Guided Workflow / UX Simplification
============================================================
Goal: V1.13's real-Chrome verification confirmed the 4-tab layout and
data flow work correctly, but the workflow itself felt confusing in
actual use — no clear "what do I do next", and Saved Scraper delete/
recreate made the relationship between Scrapers/Results/Monitoring/
Research hard to follow. V1.13.1 is a pure UX-clarity pass on top of the
existing V1.13 structure — no engine, storage, or scheduling changes.

- Scrape tab gained a compact workflow status area: "1. Configure →
  2. Run → 3. Review Results" with the current step highlighted, plus a
  one-line status text derived directly from state.columns/rawRows (no
  columns: "Add or auto-detect columns to get started."; columns but no
  results: "Ready to scrape."; results present: "N rows captured." + a
  "View Results →" primary button that jumps straight to the Results
  tab). Saved Scrapers gained an explicit "Unsaved configuration" /
  'Editing: "<name>"' status line, and Update/Save as New Scraper now
  visually swap which one looks primary depending on whether a scraper
  is actually loaded (setButtonVariant() toggles ws-btn-primary/
  ws-btn-secondary explicitly on both, never just adding one on top of
  the other — CSS source order would otherwise let secondary silently
  win).
- Results tab gained a "N rows ready" status line and two quick-nav chips
  ("📦 Go to Research", "📡 Go to Monitor") — pure switchTab() calls, so
  Research is reached via the SAME rawRows-backed state, never a second
  fetch or copy. The empty state changed from a bare message to an
  actionable one ("No results yet. Configure and run a scraper first."
  + a "Go to Scrape" button) via a new results-empty-wrap container.
- Research tab gained a static explainer ("Build a structured research
  dataset from your current results."), its status text reformatted to
  the spec's literal two-line shape ("N rows available" / "Image
  coverage: X% (ColumnName)"), and its empty state changed to "No data
  available yet." + a "Go to Scrape" button (a wording change from
  V1.13's original "No data yet. Scrape a page first." — superseded by
  this version's spec).
- Monitor tab: NEVER RUN cards now always show an explicit "No
  monitoring snapshot yet." note (previously only the badge said NEVER
  RUN, with no explanation). More importantly, a disabled-and-never-run
  card previously rendered NO status line at all (the one combination
  V1.10's original gating condition skipped entirely) — a real ambiguity
  bug caught while implementing this: it looked identical to having
  nothing to say, rather than clearly stating "Monitoring disabled".
  Fixed by always rendering the status line; "Monitoring disabled" vs
  "Every N min/No runs yet" vs "⚠ <error>" now unambiguously distinguish
  disabled / never-run / error in every combination. Enable/Run Now
  (whichever applies) are now visually styled as each card's one primary
  action via a new ws-monitor-btn-primary class (these buttons had no
  CSS class at all before this version).
- Saved Scraper deletion (spec #6): the confirmation text was rewritten
  to match the ACTUALLY INSPECTED deleteScraper() behavior, not assumed
  behavior — see "Exact delete semantics" below. No behavior change was
  made to deletion itself; only what the user is told before confirming.
  Post-delete UI correctness (no ghost Saved Scraper row, no ghost
  Monitor card, no stale "Editing: X" status, current Results/Research
  remaining exactly as they were) was verified directly — deleteScraper()
  never touches rawRows or the ws_snapshots store, so this was already
  correct in the underlying code; V1.13.1 added regression coverage for
  it, not a fix.
- switchTab() now also unconditionally resyncs the new Scrape workflow
  status on every switch (same "structural guarantee, not a
  per-call-site obligation" pattern used for the V1.13 Research-tab
  bugfix), so all three tabs' status areas can never drift from
  state.columns/rawRows regardless of navigation path.

Exact delete semantics (inspected in utils/recipes.js's deleteScraper()
and popup.js's handleDeleteScraper(), not assumed):
  - REMOVED: the entire saved scraper record from ws_saved_scrapers —
    which is where monitoring.enabled/intervalMinutes,
    monitoring.notifyOnChanges, and monitoring.history all live
    (embedded fields, not a separate store), so all three go with it.
    If monitoring was enabled, its chrome.alarms entry is explicitly
    cleared FIRST, before the record is deleted.
  - NOT deleted, but ORPHANED: snapshots (utils/snapshots.js's
    ws_snapshots store) are keyed by scraperId in a completely separate
    chrome.storage.local key that deleteScraper() never touches — they
    remain in storage but become unreachable from the UI once the
    scraper record referencing them is gone (no cleanup, no re-surfacing
    risk either, since scraper ids are never reused).
  - NOT affected at all: the popup's current in-memory Results/Research
    dataset (rawRows — deletion only ever writes to chrome.storage.local,
    never touches this in-memory variable), and any already-exported or
    downloaded files (deletion never calls chrome.downloads).

Known V1.13.1 limitations (not addressed, out of scope for a UX-only
pass): snapshots orphaned by a deletion have no UI to view or manually
purge them — they simply sit in storage until/unless a future version
adds snapshot management independent of a live Saved Scraper record.

============================================================
V1.13.2 — ZIP Bundle Downloads (single-download packaging)
============================================================
Goal: downloading many images (e.g. 60 product images for a research
dataset) previously meant Chrome's download manager handling dozens of
individual save operations — unacceptable for real research-dataset
sized use. V1.13.2 replaces one-chrome.downloads.download()-call-per-
asset (both V1.5 Bulk Download and V1.12 Research Bundle's asset queue,
which reused V1.5's) with fetch()-everything-then-package-into-ONE-zip-
then-ONE-download. Every dataset/filename guarantee from V1.6-V1.12 is
preserved exactly; only the download MECHANISM changed.

New shared module: utils/zip.js — the general-purpose ZIP writer
(STORED/uncompressed entries) extracted out of utils/xlsx.js (an .xlsx
file IS a ZIP archive, so xlsx.js already had a complete, working,
tested ZIP implementation buried inside it since V1.1 — this pulls it
out unchanged so the new image/research-bundle packaging reuses it
verbatim instead of a second implementation; xlsx.js's own byte-for-byte
output is unchanged, confirmed by its existing test). Also gained
bytesToBase64/base64ToBytes (chunked, avoids a call-stack overflow on
large arrays) for moving raw bytes across chrome.runtime.sendMessage and
building the final data: URL chrome.downloads.download() needs.

Architecture:
  - The POPUP still plans everything synchronously/purely (Dataset IDs,
    filenames, dedup, manifest CONTENT) via the exact same WSDownloads/
    WSResearch/WSCsv/WSXlsx calls as before — V1.13.2 changed WHERE the
    resulting bytes end up, never how they're computed.
  - BACKGROUND.js (its new "V1.13.2 ZIP Bundle" section) is now also
    responsible for fetch()ing each asset's bytes (concurrency-limited,
    same shape as V1.5's chrome.downloads queue), assembling the final
    ZIP via WSZip.buildZip, and making the ONE chrome.downloads.download()
    call — living here for the same reason every other multi-step
    background operation in this file does: it keeps progressing even
    if the popup that started it closes (with one caveat, see Known
    Limitations).
  - New chrome.storage.session key `ws_zip_run` (replaces `ws_download_run`
    for all NEW downloads — the old key/queue is no longer written by
    any UI path, see "Old download queue" below) tracks
    {runId, kind:'image'|'file'|'research', status, items, counts,
    folderName, zipFilename, downloadId, error}. Status progression:
    fetching -> zipping -> completed (images/files), or
    fetching -> awaiting-manifest -> completed (research — see below).
  - New messages: START_ZIP_RUN, STOP_ZIP_RUN, RETRY_FAILED_ZIP_ITEMS,
    GET_ZIP_RUN_STATE, PROVIDE_RESEARCH_MANIFEST.
  - Research Bundle's two-phase handoff: Asset Status (Downloaded/
    Failed) can only be computed AFTER fetching finishes, and
    utils/research.js (manifest generation) is popup-only — so a
    'research'-kind run pauses at 'awaiting-manifest' once every asset
    has been fetched; the popup computes manifest.csv/xlsx/json +
    dataset-info.json from the run's final per-item outcomes and sends
    the finished bytes back via PROVIDE_RESEARCH_MANIFEST, which resumes
    background.js to build and download the single zip. A manifest-only
    bundle (Images/Files both off) reaches 'awaiting-manifest'
    immediately (zero items to fetch) — no special-casing needed.
  - Missing/failed assets never abort the run (spec requirement) —
    they're simply excluded from the zip while the run still completes
    and downloads whatever succeeded; their status is recorded on the
    item (and, for Research Bundle, in the manifest's Asset Status
    column) rather than silently dropped.
  - Duplicate image/file URLs across rows: unchanged — already resolved
    at the PLANNING layer (WSDownloads' own dedup inside
    buildDownloadQueue/WSResearch.buildAssetPlan), so only one item per
    unique URL is ever queued; background.js's fetch loop has no
    special-casing to do and needs none.
  - Retry Failed: re-fetches ONLY the still-failed items (same ids/
    filenames — Dataset IDs and filenames are deterministic and were
    never recomputed, matching V1.12's existing guarantee), then
    rebuilds and re-downloads ONE fresh zip. A Research Bundle retry
    routes back through 'awaiting-manifest' too, so the manifest's Asset
    Status is correctly regenerated to reflect the retry's outcome.
  - Cancellation: STOP_ZIP_RUN aborts an in-memory AbortController for
    that run (aborting in-flight fetch() calls and preventing new ones
    from starting); a cancelled run produces NO zip download at all.
  - Host permission for fetch(): fetch() (unlike chrome.downloads
    .download(), which always bypassed CORS via Chrome's own download
    manager) DOES enforce CORS from an extension context unless a
    matching host permission is held. The popup computes the exact
    origins the queued items' URLs need (WSDownloads.uniqueOriginPatterns
    — never a blanket grab) and requests them via chrome.permissions
    .request() at the real user gesture (the Download/Create Bundle
    click) — reusing the ALREADY-DECLARED optional_host_permissions
    (["*://*/*"]) from V1.3, so manifest.json needed NO permission
    changes. background.js only ever calls the gesture-free
    chrome.permissions.contains(), same pattern Monitoring has used
    since V1.8.
  - Old per-file download queue (ws_download_run, START_DOWNLOAD_RUN/
    STOP_DOWNLOAD_RUN/RETRY_FAILED_DOWNLOADS/GET_DOWNLOAD_RUN_STATE,
    chrome.downloads.onChanged tracking): INSPECTED and confirmed no
    longer used by any UI path (both Bulk Download and Research Bundle
    now exclusively use the zip pipeline) — deliberately left in place
    rather than deleted, to keep this already-large change's risk
    surface minimal; it is dead code, safe to remove in a future
    cleanup pass. The "downloads" permission itself is still required
    (now only for each run's single final zip file).

Bug found & fixed while implementing this: runWithConcurrency's
worker-dispatch loop closed over a shared `var item` from inside a
synchronous while loop, instead of passing it as a function parameter —
since the while loop's several iterations (up to `concurrency` of them)
all run before ANY of their deferred .then() callbacks fire, every
concurrent worker in one batch was silently processing the SAME final
item instead of its own, permanently skipping the others (with
concurrency=4-6, this could silently drop most of a batch while
redundantly re-fetching one URL several times). Caught directly by this
version's own 60-image regression test; fixed by making the per-item
callback its own function with `item` as a real parameter.

Tests added: utils/research.js's computeAssetStatus gained recognition
of the new pipeline's 'fetched' status string alongside the legacy
'completed' one (a second bug the same rewrite of test-popup-v112-research.js
caught — the manifest's Asset Status column was reading every
successful fetch as "missing" until this was fixed, since it only ever
recognized chrome.downloads' old vocabulary).

Known V1.13.2 limitations (documented, not silently accepted): the
bytes fetched so far and the AbortController backing Stop both live only
in the service worker's in-memory state — chrome.storage.session only
ever holds per-item STATUS, never the actual image bytes (would blow
past session storage's quota for even a modest bundle). If the service
worker is killed and restarted mid-run (the same general MV3 constraint
V1.8's own docs already call out for long monitored runs), an
in-progress zip run cannot resume — a short Bulk-Download/Research-
Bundle-sized run (tens of images, well under a minute of fetching) is
very unlikely to hit this in practice. A reopened popup mid-run also
can't finalize an in-progress Research Bundle (the manifest-building
plan is popup-only and not persisted) — unchanged from V1.12's own
already-documented limitation, just now also gating the awaiting-
manifest handoff specifically.

============================================================
V1.14 — Productization / Free-Pro Foundation / Release Readiness
============================================================
Goal: turn the extension from "a working tool" into "a product ready for
Chrome Web Store distribution" — a Free/Pro feature-access architecture,
a Settings/About area, a first-run empty state, an error-message audit,
a reproducible production packaging script, and an automated release-
readiness check. No backend, payment provider, login system, analytics,
or external account of any kind was added — those are explicitly
deferred (see NOT YET BUILT below). This version adds NO new user-facing
scraping capability; every feature that existed in V1.13.2 still exists
and works identically for a Pro user (or a dev-mode-switched Free/Pro
tester) — V1.14 only adds a plan layer ON TOP of what was already built.

New modules:
  - utils/plan.js (WSPlan) — the single source of truth for "what does
    this plan allow". Holds a static PLAN_LIMITS table (free/pro), a
    FEATURE_INFO table (label + one-sentence explanation per gated
    feature, used by the upgrade modal), and the stored plan flag itself
    (chrome.storage.local key `ws_plan`, schema {schemaVersion, plan}).
    A missing key defaults to 'free' — the same "missing = safe default"
    convention every other V1.1-V1.13 additive field already uses, so no
    migration step was needed to introduce it. isDevelopmentInstall()
    wraps chrome.management.getSelf() (no manifest permission required —
    that one specific method is explicitly permission-free per Chrome's
    extension platform) to detect an unpacked/sideloaded install; fails
    closed (false) on any error, so it can never be accidentally exposed
    in a real Chrome Web Store install.
  - utils/settings.js (WSSettings) — app-level preferences beyond the
    plan itself; currently just the Research Bundle format defaults
    (chrome.storage.local key `ws_settings`). Same additive-default
    pattern; self-heals a partial/legacy shape via Object.assign against
    its own defaults rather than failing.

Free/Pro feature matrix:
  FREE — Manual scraping, Current Page mode, up to 100 rows/scrape,
    Text/Link/Image columns, Auto Detect, Preview/Results, CSV export,
    Copy results, Filter, Sort, Remove Duplicates, up to 3 saved
    scraper configurations.
  PRO (everything in Free, plus) — unlimited rows/scrape, Auto Scroll,
    Multi-page scraping, Excel export, JSON export, Bulk Image ZIP
    download, Bulk File ZIP download, Research Dataset Builder +
    Research Bundle ZIP, Monitoring (enable/disable, scheduled runs,
    "Run Now"), Change Detection, Notifications, Snapshots, Monitoring
    History, advanced Transforms, unlimited saved scrapers.

Gating architecture (popup.js, "Free/Pro Plan Foundation" section):
  - guardProFeature(featureKey, actionIfAllowed) — the ONE reusable gate
    every PRO control routes through: calls actionIfAllowed() unchanged
    if the current plan has that feature, otherwise shows the upgrade
    modal and never calls it. No feature has its own bespoke modal
    logic. Wired onto: Export Excel/JSON buttons, Download Images/Files
    buttons, Research Bundle button, Transform toggle, Snapshots toggle,
    Monitor card's Enable/Run Now buttons (Disable is NEVER gated — a
    user must always be able to turn monitoring off), and the Auto
    Scroll/Multi-page run-mode radios (reverts to Current Page + shows
    the modal if selected while Free).
  - showUpgradeModal(featureKey)/hideUpgradeModal() — a single shared
    modal (#upgrade-modal-overlay in popup.html) with a title, a one-
    sentence explanation pulled from WSPlan.featureInfo(featureKey), and
    exactly one action: "Maybe Later". No checkout flow of any kind.
  - Every gated button/label also carries a small inline "PRO" tag
    (span.ws-pro-tag) that's simply hidden when the active plan is Pro —
    features are always visible, never hidden outright, so a Free user
    can see what exists (spec #3).
  - applyRowLimitToRawRows(rows) — applied the moment a fresh extraction
    becomes the current dataset (Preview / View Run Results), entirely
    in popup.js; content/*.js (the scraper engine) is completely
    unaware of plan limits and was not touched. A >100-row Free scrape
    keeps the first 100 rows and shows a persistent note ("Showing
    first 100 rows on Free."); the underlying scraper/selector
    configuration is never altered or corrupted by truncation.
  - updateScraperLimitText() — "Saved Scrapers: N / 3 used" on Free
    (blank on Pro); an existing user with MORE than 3 saved scrapers
    (legacy data, or a Pro->Free downgrade) sees "3 / 3 used (over
    limit — existing extras are kept, but new ones can't be saved on
    Free)" — every existing scraper stays fully loadable/usable/
    deletable; the limit only blocks creating a NEW one past the cap.
  - background.js's runScheduledScrape() — the one PLAN-AWARE check
    outside popup.js, since a chrome.alarms-triggered scheduled run
    fires with no popup open at all. Reads WSPlan.loadPlanState()
    directly and, if the plan lacks 'monitoring', records a clear error
    result ("Monitoring requires Pro. Your schedule and history are
    preserved — upgrade to resume automatic checks.") via the SAME
    finishMonitoringRun() path every other monitoring error already
    uses, then returns — deliberately WITHOUT calling
    notifyMonitoringResult() (a repeating "needs Pro" notification every
    interval would be noise, not useful). Both the alarm listener and
    the manual "Run Now" message share this one function, so the gate
    covers both triggers identically. Snapshots, monitoring history, and
    the schedule (enabled/intervalMinutes) are never touched by this
    check — only NEW execution is blocked.

Storage / migration: introduced exactly two new isolated storage keys
(`ws_plan`, `ws_settings`), both additive with safe missing-key
defaults — no existing key (`ws_saved_scrapers`, `ws_snapshots`,
`ws_state::*`, `ws_loaded_scraper::*`) was touched, renamed, or
reshaped. A V1.1-V1.13.2 user upgrading to V1.14 keeps every Saved
Scraper, snapshot, monitoring config/history, and notification
preference exactly as-is, and simply starts on the Free plan by
default (silent, no prompt/wizard) until they explicitly test the dev-
mode Pro switch or (in the future) actually upgrade. Verified directly:
legacy >3-saved-scrapers data is fully preserved and listed; a Pro-to-
Free downgrade never deletes monitoring history or snapshots; switching
back to Pro immediately restores access to the same preserved data
(nothing is ever re-created — it was never gone).

Settings/About (popup.html #settings-panel, opened via the header ⚙
button — an overlay screen replacing the 4 tabs while open, NOT a 5th
tab): General (current plan + a one-line summary; the dev-only plan
switcher, itself hidden unless WSPlan.isDevelopmentInstall() resolves
true), Downloads (the 5 Research Bundle format-default checkboxes,
persisted via WSSettings), Data & Privacy (explicit, verified-against-
the-actual-code language: configs/snapshots/history/preferences are
chrome.storage-local-only and never uploaded; scraped page content
stays on the device and only ever leaves the browser when the user
explicitly exports/downloads, which saves straight to their own
computer via Chrome's Downloads feature; image/file fetches for a ZIP
bundle go directly from the source site to the browser, never through
any Web Scraper server; the extension has no backend, no accounts, and
sends no analytics/telemetry — confirmed true by inspecting every
network-capable code path: fetch() calls in background.js's ZIP
pipeline only ever hit URLs already present in the scraped page's own
data), About (live manifest version + a short description), and three
explicit placeholders — Privacy Policy / Support / Chrome Web Store
links all literally read "(coming soon)" rather than a fabricated URL.

First-run UX: the Scrape tab's existing workflow-status area gained a
compact 4-step list (#first-run-help) — "1. Add or auto-detect columns
2. Preview the page 3. Review results 4. Save the scraper if desired" —
shown ONLY when there are truly zero columns AND zero saved scrapers;
hidden the moment either exists (even an unloaded saved scraper already
counts as "not first run"). No tutorial wizard, no multi-step overlay.

Error-message audit (spec #7): most of this codebase's ~50 setStatus()
error strings were already clean, user-facing copy (this project's
established convention since early versions). Found exactly 3 call
sites that showed a raw `e.message` straight from a try/catch to the
user (2 in the Transform apply/preview path, 1 in the Research Bundle
manifest hand-off) — a genuinely unexpected exception there (as opposed
to WSTransforms' own clean, app-authored validation errors, which flow
through the same catch blocks and are NOT altered) could have surfaced
a raw TypeError/"Cannot read properties of..."-style message. Added one
small helper, friendlyErrorMessage(e, fallback), right next to
setStatus(): always logs the full original error + stack to
console.error for real debugging, and only swaps in `fallback` for the
user-facing text when the message matches a denylist of technical-
looking patterns (TypeError/ReferenceError/etc. prefixes, "Cannot read
properties", "is not a function", "message port closed", etc.) — a
legitimate app-authored message (e.g. "Invalid regular expression: ...")
passes through unchanged. Wired into the 3 sites found; the other ~50
sites were already fine and were not touched.

Release Readiness Audit (spec #11 — manifest.json and architecture,
permissions traced by actual usage before any conclusion, nothing
removed):
  - manifest_version: 3. version: 1.14.0 (description also updated to
    mention Free/Pro).
  - permissions: ["activeTab", "storage", "scripting", "downloads",
    "alarms", "notifications"] — all 5 confirmed actively used:
    activeTab (implicit access to the tab the popup was opened on,
    combined with default_popup — every chrome.tabs.query/sendMessage
    call in popup.js depends on it), storage (chrome.storage.local/
    .session, used throughout every module), scripting
    (chrome.scripting.executeScript/registerContentScript/
    unregisterContentScripts — the on-demand content-script injection
    this extension has used since V1.2, still the ONLY way content/*.js
    ever runs; there is no static content_scripts manifest entry by
    design), downloads (chrome.downloads.download/cancel/onChanged —
    CSV/Excel/JSON exports use a plain <a download> Blob URL and don't
    need it, but Bulk Image/File ZIP and Research Bundle ZIP do),
    alarms (chrome.alarms.create/clear/getAll/onAlarm — V1.8 Scheduled
    Monitoring), notifications (chrome.notifications.create — V1.9).
    None are unused; none were removed.
  - optional_host_permissions: ["*://*/*"] — requested narrowly at the
    real user gesture via chrome.permissions.request() for only the
    specific origin(s) actually needed (Multi-page's cross-page
    navigation, Monitoring's background-tab re-visits, and each ZIP
    run's fetch() targets via WSDownloads.uniqueOriginPatterns) — never
    a blanket request. Confirmed still exercised correctly by the full
    existing test suite (permissions.contains/request mocks throughout
    test-background-monitoring.js, test-background-downloads.js,
    test-background-zip-bundle.js).
  - background: a classic (non-module) service worker
    (background/background.js) using importScripts() to load 9 local
    utils/*.js files — every path is a same-origin, package-relative
    file (../utils/*.js); NO remote URL appears in any importScripts()
    call anywhere in the codebase (checked programmatically by
    scripts/release-check.js, see below).
  - content scripts: no static "content_scripts" manifest key at all —
    intentional since V1.2; both background.js and popup.js declare an
    identical CONTENT_FILES path array (utils/storage.js, utils/
    runstate.js, then the 6 content/*.js files) and inject it on demand
    via chrome.scripting. This is fine for MV3 and was left as-is
    (changing it would be a real architecture change, out of scope for
    a productization pass) — noted here only so a future reviewer isn't
    surprised not to find a content_scripts block.
  - externally loaded code / remote code concerns: NONE found. No
    <script src="http...> in popup.html (14 local script tags only, all
    same-origin). No eval()/new Function() anywhere in background/
    content/popup/utils. No CDN dependency of any kind — utils/zip.js
    and utils/xlsx.js are both dependency-free, hand-written ZIP
    writers; utils/csv.js is hand-written CSV; no npm-bundled library
    ships in the extension at all.
  - CSP: no custom content_security_policy override in manifest.json —
    MV3's own strict default (no remote code, no eval, no inline
    <script>) already covers everything above; nothing needed loosening.
  - unused permissions: none found (all 5 traced to real call sites
    above) — nothing was removed.
  - debugging/development leftovers: chrome.management (used ONLY for
    the dev-only plan switcher's install-type check) needs no manifest
    permission and fails closed to false in production, so it cannot
    leak into a real install's behavior. One finding NOT auto-fixed,
    left for review: content/pagination.js (12 calls) and content/
    content.js (3 calls) contain verbose console.log diagnostic output
    (auto-scroll/multi-page progress, element-pick results) that fires
    in the PAGE's own DevTools console on every real scrape run —
    harmless (no data leaves the browser, nothing sent anywhere) but
    arguably unpolished for a shipped product. Deliberately NOT removed
    here per this project's standing rule that content/*.js (the
    scraper engine) is only touched when strictly required — flagging
    it as a candidate for a future small cleanup pass instead.
    background.js's own single console.log ("Web Scraper installed.")
    is an intentional one-time install log, left as-is.
  - test/development files shipped: NONE — every test file for this
    entire project (V1.1 through V1.14, ~40 files) lives outside the
    repository in an isolated scratchpad directory and always has;
    scripts/build-release.js's file-name denylist (dotfiles, Thumbs.db,
    desktop.ini, test-*/*.test.js/*.spec.js patterns) is a defensive
    backstop, not a fix for anything actually present.

Production packaging (spec #12): scripts/build-release.js — an
explicit ALLOWLIST (not denylist) of runtime paths: manifest.json plus
the background/, content/, popup/, utils/, and icons/ directories in
full, walked recursively. Reuses utils/zip.js's own dependency-free
buildZip() (the same ZIP writer V1.13.2 uses at runtime for bulk-
download/research-bundle packaging) rather than a second implementation
or an external library — requiring it under plain Node attaches WSZip
to globalThis directly, no shim needed (Node 18+ provides the
TextEncoder/btoa globals that file already assumes). Run with:
    node scripts/build-release.js
Produces dist/web-scraper-v1.14.0.zip (manifest.json first/at the ZIP
root; re-run any time to regenerate — fully reproducible, deterministic
file set). dist/ is gitignored (build output, not source).

Release check (spec #13): scripts/release-check.js — 14 automated
checks: manifest.json parses as valid JSON; manifest_version is 3;
version is a valid x.y.z string; the 8 required runtime files exist on
disk; background.service_worker's file exists; every icon path in
manifest.icons/action.default_icon resolves; action.default_popup
resolves; every path in the CONTENT_FILES array (parsed directly out of
background.js's own source, not hand-duplicated) exists on disk; no
<script src="http...> in popup.html; no eval()/new Function()/remote
importScripts() anywhere in background/content/popup/utils; the
production package actually builds; the resulting ZIP has manifest.json
at its root; the ZIP contains zero forbidden dev files; the ZIP's entry
count matches the exact expected runtime file set (catches both a
missing AND an accidentally-included file). Run with:
    node scripts/release-check.js
Current result on this codebase: 14/14 checks pass, exit code 0.

Tests added (all in the isolated scratchpad, none in the repo):
  - test-plan.js (54 assertions) — pure utils/plan.js logic: fresh-
    install default, malformed/legacy-garbage stored values failing
    closed to free, setPlan() persistence + its own fail-closed
    coercion of an invalid value, the full limits/features table for
    both plans, featureInfo() always returning something usable
    (including for an unknown key), and isDevelopmentInstall() across
    development/normal/absent-API/lastError-set scenarios.
  - test-popup-v114-plan-gating.js (100 assertions) — the bulk of the
    scenario coverage: Free-is-default, Pro hides every PRO tag, the
    100-row Free truncation (+ the exact note text) vs. no truncation on
    Pro, the 3-saved-scraper Free limit blocking a 4th save (with the
    exact "why" in the modal) while NOT touching the existing 3, all 5
    legacy scrapers from a >3-scraper seed staying fully listed and
    loadable, FREE CSV export never gated, all 6 of Excel/JSON/Image-
    ZIP/File-ZIP/Transform/Snapshots individually verified blocked-on-
    Free-with-modal and allowed-on-Pro-without-modal (12 sub-cases),
    Research Bundle gating, Auto Scroll/Multi-page radio-revert
    behavior, Monitoring Enable gating (including the button's own
    "(PRO)" label suffix and confirming no SET_MONITORING message is
    ever sent while blocked), the dev-only switcher's visibility rule
    (hidden on a normal install, shown + starts on the live plan on a
    development install) and its LIVE effect (header badge/PRO tags
    update with no reload), the full Pro->Free->Pro round trip proving
    monitoring history + a snapshot survive both transitions untouched
    and access is fully restored, Settings panel rendering (all 4
    sections + the exact placeholder-link text), the Downloads section's
    checkboxes persisting to WSSettings, and the first-run help list's
    exact 4 steps plus its hide condition.
  - test-background-plan-gating.js (19 assertions) — the ONE plan-aware
    background.js check: a Free plan blocks both a manual "Run Now" AND
    an alarm-triggered scheduled run (no tab ever opened, no
    notification fires, a clear Pro-required error is recorded), a
    disabled scraper still short-circuits before the plan check even
    runs (unchanged from pre-V1.14 behavior), and the CRITICAL full
    round trip: a real Pro run creates genuine history + a snapshot,
    downgrading to Free blocks the next run WITHOUT deleting that
    history/snapshot/schedule (byte-identical before/after), and
    switching back to Pro produces a genuinely new successful run.
