  - test-release-scripts.js (14 assertions) — runs build-release.js and
    release-check.js as REAL child processes (not just in-process
    requires), asserting on their actual exit codes and stdout,
    including a real ZIP local-file-header signature check on the
    produced file, and a negative case (a corrupted manifest.json in a
    throwaway temp copy of the whole project) proving release-check.js
    fails loudly rather than silently packaging something broken.
  - Every one of the 16 pre-existing popup-driven test files and all 3
    pre-existing background-driven test files (test-background-
    monitoring.js, test-background-downloads.js, test-background-zip-
    bundle.js) were mechanically updated to load utils/plan.js (and,
    for popup tests, utils/settings.js too) and to default their mocked
    ws_plan to Pro when not explicitly seeded — preserving every one of
    those files' ORIGINAL testing intent (verifying full, ungated
    functionality, which still matters for a Pro user) now that the
    real default plan is Free, cleanly separated from the NEW gating-
    specific test files above which are the ones that actually exercise
    the Free tier.

Full regression result: all 40 scratchpad test files (V1.1 through
V1.14) pass with zero failures — 1,180+ total assertions across
test-autodetect.js, test-changes.js, test-dedup-normalize.js,
test-domwait.js, test-downloads.js, test-image-extraction.js,
test-pagination.js, test-plan.js, test-popup.js and all 15 of its
per-version siblings (v13-v19, v110-v113, v1131, v113b, v114),
test-recipes.js, test-release-scripts.js, test-research.js,
test-results.js, test-runstate.js, test-selector(-2/-3).js,
test-snapshots.js, test-transforms.js, test-xlsx.js, and all 4
background-driven files (test-background-monitoring.js,
test-background-downloads.js, test-background-zip-bundle.js,
test-background-plan-gating.js).

Bug found & fixed while implementing this: none in the new plan/
settings/gating logic itself (caught cleanly by its own new tests
before ever running against the shared suite) — the only real issues
hit were 3 raw-e.message error strings surfaced by the deliberate audit
in spec #7 (see above; not a functional bug, a copy-quality one) and
the mechanical "every existing test now runs against a default-Free
plan" breakage anticipated and fixed proactively (see Tests added,
last bullet) rather than discovered as failures.

Remaining tasks before Chrome Web Store submission (explicitly NOT done
in V1.14, matching its own stop condition):
  - No real payment/license/entitlement system exists yet — `ws_plan`
    is purely a local, user/dev-controlled flag with no verification of
    any kind. A real launch needs a genuine way to grant Pro (a
    license key check, a payment-provider webhook writing the flag via
    some trusted channel, etc.) before the Free/Pro split means
    anything commercially.
  - Privacy Policy / Support / Chrome Web Store listing URLs are all
    still explicit "(coming soon)" placeholders in Settings > About —
    a real submission needs a real, hosted Privacy Policy at minimum
    (Chrome Web Store requires one for any listing that handles user
    data, even "stored locally only" data).
  - content/pagination.js's and content/content.js's diagnostic
    console.log output (flagged above, deliberately not auto-removed)
    is worth a small dedicated cleanup pass before a public listing.
  - The old, already-dead V1.5 per-file download queue
    (ws_download_run / START_DOWNLOAD_RUN etc., noted as dead code back
    in V1.13.2's own section) is still present in background.js —
    still safe, still unused, still a good candidate for a future
    cleanup pass.
  - Store listing assets (screenshots, promotional images, a longer
    marketing description) do not exist yet — out of scope for this
    codebase-focused version.
  - This report and the built dist/web-scraper-v1.14.0.zip should be
    reviewed by the user in real Chrome (load unpacked from dist/ after
    unzipping, or straight from the repo root) before any Web Store
    upload is attempted — per V1.14's own stop condition, no V1.15 work
    should begin until that review happens.

============================================================
V1.15 — Usage-Based Trial (replaces the V1.14 Free/Pro model entirely)
============================================================
Goal: a complete monetization-model change, decided AFTER V1.14 was
reviewed. V1.14's per-feature Free/Pro gating (row limits, saved-scraper
limits, PRO tags, per-feature upgrade modals) is REMOVED entirely. The
new model: every feature is available to every user from install; a new
user gets 10 free SUCCESSFUL scraping runs; after that, a one-time $5
purchase (not a subscription) unlocks unlimited use. utils/plan.js is
deleted outright — utils/license.js is the new, and only, monetization-
related module in this codebase.

Why "successful": Preview/configuration actions, failed extractions, and
cancelled/stopped runs must never cost the user a credit — only a run
that genuinely finished with real data counts. Monitoring is completely
exempt from the trial (it was never part of "10 interactive runs" to
begin with) and every other feature (Excel/JSON export, bulk ZIP
downloads, Research Bundle, Snapshots, Transforms, saved scrapers of any
count) is simply always on, trial or licensed — there is nothing left to
gate per-feature.

New module: utils/license.js (WSLicense) — the single source of truth,
replacing utils/plan.js's role exactly but with a completely different
shape underneath (a run counter + a license flag, not a static feature
table). Storage: one isolated key, `ws_license` (schema {schemaVersion,
licenseStatus: 'trial'|'licensed', trialRunsUsed, licenseKey,
licenseVerifiedAt, chargedRunIds}) — additive/independent of every other
key, exactly like `ws_plan` was. A missing key means "brand new trial
user" (0 runs used, 10 remaining) — the same "missing = safe default"
convention every other module in this project already uses.
`trialRunsRemaining`/`trialTotalRuns`/`isLicensed` are NEVER separately
persisted — always recomputed from `trialRunsUsed`/`licenseStatus` on
every read (withComputed()), so there is exactly one source of truth and
no way for "used" and "remaining" to drift apart.

Exact trial accounting implementation:
  - WSLicense.consumeRunCredit(runId) is the ONLY place trialRunsUsed is
    ever incremented. It is idempotent by `runId`: a `chargedRunIds`
    ledger (capped at 200 entries, oldest dropped first) is checked
    before every charge — calling it twice with the SAME runId charges
    once, the second call returns {charged:false, reason:'duplicate'}
    and touches nothing. A licensed account's calls always short-circuit
    before touching the ledger or counter at all ({charged:false,
    reason:'licensed'}) — unlimited truly means unlimited.
  - Current Page (Preview): popup.js's handlePreview() checks
    trialAllowsNewRun() BEFORE sending RUN_EXTRACTION at all (0 credits
    means the content script is never even messaged) — spec's "attempting
    to start another scrape should open a clean upgrade modal". On a
    successful extraction (res.ok true — a real, completed scrape,
    regardless of row count) it mints a fresh id
    (`preview_<hostname>_<timestamp>_<random>`) and charges it. A failed
    extraction (res.ok false / a thrown exception) never reaches the
    charge call at all — 0 credits consumed, matching spec exactly.
  - Auto Scroll / Multi-page (Start Run): handleStartRun() checks the
    same trialAllowsNewRun() gate before sending START_AUTO_SCROLL/
    START_MULTI_PAGE. The credit itself is charged only when the run's
    session-tracked status (content/pagination.js's existing
    chrome.storage.session `ws_run::<hostname>` state machine, unchanged
    since V1.3) transitions to 'completed' — 'stopped' (cancelled by the
    user) and 'error' (browser/page-caused failure) never charge, using
    the run's own pre-existing `runId` (WSRunState.createRunState's
    makeId('run'), already unique per run since V1.3) as the idempotency
    key. Charged from TWO call sites, both safe to double-fire thanks to
    idempotency: attachRunStorageListener's chrome.storage.onChanged
    callback (the common case, popup open when the run finishes) AND
    init()'s own "was a run already sitting here when the popup opened?"
    check (the run kept going with the popup CLOSED and finished in the
    background — this is a real, documented architecture property of
    Auto Scroll/Multi-page since V1.3, so charging here specifically
    matters, not a hypothetical edge case).
  - Monitoring (background.js's runScheduledScrape, both the alarm-
    triggered and "Run Now" message-triggered paths) NEVER calls
    WSLicense at all — background.js no longer imports or references any
    licensing module (V1.14's WSPlan gate there is removed too, not just
    left unused). Verified directly: a monitoring run succeeds
    identically whether ws_license says trial-with-credits, trial-with-
    zero-credits, or licensed, or is entirely absent from storage, and
    ws_license's stored bytes are provably byte-identical before/after a
    monitoring run (background.js never even issues a
    chrome.storage.local.get(['ws_license']) call — confirmed by
    recording every key ever requested during a real run).
  - Same-context write races are guarded the same way background.js's
    own serializeMonitoringOp already does for V1.8 (an internal
    Promise-chain queue inside license.js itself) — two nearly-
    simultaneous consumeRunCredit() calls in ONE popup instance can't
    lose an increment to a classic read-modify-write race. Documented,
    accepted limitation: this does NOT protect against two SEPARATE
    popup instances open in two different browser windows charging at
    the exact same moment — the same category of honestly-scoped MV3
    limitation this project already documents elsewhere (service-worker
    suspension mid-run, etc.).

TRIAL UI: the V1.14 header "FREE"/"PRO" badge (#header-plan-badge) is
replaced by #header-trial-badge — "10 runs left" / "7 runs left" /
"1 run left" (correct singular/plural) / "0 runs left" / "Unlimited"
once licensed, live-updated by applyLicenseUI() immediately after every
charge or license-state change (never stale). At 0 credits, Preview/
Start Run open #trial-modal-overlay with the exact spec copy — title
"Free trial complete", body "You've used your 10 free scraping runs.",
primary CTA "Unlock Unlimited — $5", secondary "Maybe Later" — replacing
V1.14's per-feature #upgrade-modal-overlay entirely (there is now only
ever one reason this modal shows). Settings > General shows the license
state ("Free Trial" / "Licensed (Unlimited)") plus a matching summary
line and its own persistent "Unlock Unlimited — $5" button (not only
reachable via the blocking modal).

UX CLEANUP (spec's explicit list, all removed): every .ws-pro-tag span
(Auto Scroll/Multi-page radios, Transform/Snapshots/Research buttons,
Excel/JSON export, Download Images/Files) deleted from popup.html/
popup.css/popup.js entirely — there is no more per-feature "PRO" marker
anywhere, because there is no more per-feature gating. #scraper-limit-
text ("Saved Scrapers: N/3 used") and #results-row-limit-note ("Showing
first 100 rows on Free.") both deleted along with the row-limit
(applyRowLimitToRawRows) and saved-scraper-limit logic that produced
them — Preview/View Run Results now just use the real row count
unconditionally, and handleSaveScraper() has no cap of any kind. Every
guardProFeature('featureKey', ...) wrapper (Excel/JSON export, bulk
download panels, Research Bundle, Transform toggle, Snapshots toggle,
Monitor Enable/Run Now) is unwrapped back to a direct handler call — the
run-mode radios no longer revert-and-warn on Auto Scroll/Multi-page
selection. The Monitor card's Enable button no longer grows a "(PRO)"
suffix. Nothing about V1.13.2's ZIP pipeline, V1.12's Research Bundle
structure, V1.11's dashboard, or any other already-working feature was
redesigned — only the gating LAYER on top of them was removed.

Licensing abstraction (the actual point of this version, per spec):
utils/license.js's activateLicense(licenseKey) is the ONE function a
real payment/license-server integration needs to replace. V1.15 ships it
as a stub that can NEVER succeed — {ok:false, error:'Online purchase and
license verification are not connected yet in this build.'} — and never
writes to storage. No fake payment verification of any kind exists
anywhere in this codebase. Every caller (the modal's and Settings'
"Unlock Unlimited — $5" buttons, both wired to the same
handleUnlockPurchaseClick()) only ever checks `.ok` and surfaces
`.error` honestly via setStatus() — nothing else in popup.js needs to
change when a real payment flow is wired in later. DEV ONLY (spec's
explicit allowance): WSLicense.activateDevLicense()/resetToTrialDev(),
reachable only through Settings' dev-only radio switcher, itself only
ever shown when WSLicense.isDevelopmentInstall() (chrome.management.
getSelf() — permission-free, no manifest change needed) reports
installType 'development'; fails closed to false (production behavior)
on any error, exactly mirroring V1.14's plan.js dev switcher. The
simulated license is persisted with licenseKey:'DEV-SIMULATED-LICENSE' —
an unmistakably-fake sentinel, never a plausible-looking real token, so
storage contents alone make clear this was never a real purchase.

Storage / migration: introduced exactly one new additive key,
`ws_license` (missing key = fresh trial, same convention as every prior
version's new field). `ws_plan` (V1.14's key) is NEVER read by
utils/license.js — an existing V1.14 user who had switched to Pro via
the old dev-only switcher does NOT become automatically licensed under
V1.15; that flag was never proof of purchase and the spec explicitly
required production behavior to never trust it. The orphaned `ws_plan`
key itself is left untouched in storage (never deleted) — this project's
storage layer never destructively touches a key it doesn't own. Every
other existing key (`ws_saved_scrapers`, `ws_snapshots`, `ws_state::*`,
`ws_loaded_scraper::*`, `ws_settings`) is completely unmodified —
verified directly: a V1.14 install's saved scrapers (any count),
monitoring config/history, snapshots, and research/settings preferences
all render and function identically after upgrading, while the license
state starts a genuinely fresh 10-run trial regardless of what the old
ws_plan flag said.

Data model fields (spec's explicit minimum, all present): trialRunsUsed
(persisted, source of truth), trialRunsRemaining (computed, spec's
"at minimum" is satisfied by exposing it on every loaded state object —
never a second persisted value that could drift), licenseStatus ('trial'
| 'licensed'), licenseKey (null until a purchase — or a dev simulation —
sets it), licenseVerifiedAt (a timestamp, null until set).

Tests added (all in the isolated scratchpad, none in the repo) —
test-plan.js, test-popup-v114-plan-gating.js, and test-background-plan-
gating.js (all V1.14-specific) were DELETED, superseded by:
  - test-license.js (43 assertions) — pure utils/license.js logic: fresh
    install (10 remaining), sequential charges decrementing correctly,
    exactly 10 charges exhausting the trial and hasRunsRemaining
    flipping false, a duplicate runId never double-charging (including a
    THIRD repeat attempt), a licensed account's calls never touching the
    counter or ledger at all, malformed/legacy-garbage stored state
    failing closed to a safe default, an old V1.14 ws_plan:pro flag
    proven NOT to make loadLicenseState() report licensed,
    activateDevLicense/resetToTrialDev round-tripping correctly (and the
    fake key/timestamp/ledger-clear all verified), activateLicense()
    proven to NEVER succeed and NEVER write to storage, isDevelopmentInstall()
    across development/normal/absent-API scenarios, and the charged-run
    ledger's 200-entry cap keeping the most recent ids.
  - test-popup-v115-trial.js (65 assertions) — the bulk of the scenario
    coverage, matching spec's own test list point-for-point: fresh
    install shows "10 runs left"; a successful Preview consumes exactly
    1 credit AND persists across a simulated popup close/reopen; a
    failed extraction consumes 0; Auto Scroll 'completed' charges
    exactly once while 'stopped' and 'error' never charge at all;
    duplicate completion events (both a repeated onChanged event for the
    same runId, AND the cross-check between the onChanged listener and
    the boot-time already-completed-run check) never double-charge;
    every feature (View Results, Transform, Excel/JSON export, Bulk
    Image Download, Snapshots, Research Bundle) remains fully reachable
    at exactly 0 credits with zero .ws-pro-tag elements anywhere in the
    DOM; 0 credits blocks Preview AND Start Run with the exact spec
    modal copy (title/body/both button labels asserted verbatim) and the
    content script is never even messaged; a monitoring "Run Now" click
    round-trips a real message while the credit count is provably
    unchanged; a licensed account shows "Unlimited" and never
    accumulates a used-count across repeated successful scrapes; a full
    legacy-V1.14-store migration (ws_plan:pro + saved scraper + snapshot
    + monitoring history + research settings) starts a fresh 10-run
    trial while every other field renders correctly and nothing is
    deleted; the dev-only switcher's visibility rule and live effect;
    and Settings' license section rendering plus the $5 CTA's honest
    non-functional behavior in both directions (still shows "Free Trial"
    after clicking it, and the button itself is hidden once licensed).
  - test-background-v115-monitoring-unrestricted.js (9 assertions) — the
    background-level proof that monitoring is untouched by the trial:
    succeeds with no ws_license/ws_plan data present at all; succeeds
    identically across trial-with-credits / trial-at-zero / licensed
    scenarios with ws_license proven byte-identical before/after each
    run; an alarm-triggered (not just "Run Now") run also succeeds at 0
    interactive credits; and background.js is proven to never even
    REQUEST the `ws_license` key from chrome.storage.local at all (every
    key ever asked for during a full run is recorded and asserted on).
  - All 16 pre-existing popup-driven test files and all 3 pre-existing
    background-driven test files had their V1.14-era `loadFile('utils/
    plan.js')` / `'utils/plan.js'` importedFiles entries swapped for
    utils/license.js (popup) or removed outright (background, which
    loads no licensing module at all now), and their now-meaningless
    `ws_plan: pro` mock fallbacks stripped — every one of these files
    re-verified to pass completely unchanged in behavior, since none of
    them exercises more than a handful of real scrapes per boot and a
    fresh 10-run trial is always more than enough headroom.

Full regression result: all 40 scratchpad test files (V1.1 through
V1.15) pass with zero failures.

Manifest: version bumped to 1.15.0; description updated to describe the
new trial/one-time-purchase model instead of Free/Pro. No permission
changes — utils/license.js needs nothing beyond what utils/plan.js
already used (chrome.management.getSelf() remains permission-free).
scripts/build-release.js / scripts/release-check.js needed NO code
changes at all (both already worked from a generic file-tree walk, not a
hardcoded list) — re-run and reconfirmed: 14/14 checks pass,
dist/web-scraper-v1.15.0.zip packages the same 28-file allowlisted set
(utils/plan.js's removal and utils/license.js's addition both handled
automatically).

Remaining work required to connect the real $5 payment and license
verification system (V1.15 explicitly stops here, matching its own
"do not implement fake payment verification" and "create a clean
abstraction" instructions):
  - A real one-time-purchase checkout flow needs to exist somewhere (a
    hosted payment page, e.g. Stripe Checkout in one-time-payment mode,
    or an equivalent) — WSLicense.PURCHASE_URL is currently null exactly
    like V1.14's placeholder links, ready to hold a real destination.
  - A real server-side license-token issuance + verification service —
    something must exist to actually mint a license token after a
    successful $5 payment and let the extension verify it's genuine
    (not just "some string the user typed"). This project deliberately
    has NO backend of any kind yet.
  - utils/license.js's activateLicense(licenseKey) body is the ONE
    function to replace: kick off the real purchase flow (or verify a
    token if the user already has one), call the real verification
    service, and only on genuine success persist {licenseStatus:
    'licensed', licenseKey: <the real token>, licenseVerifiedAt:
    Date.now()} via the SAME persist() path activateDevLicense() already
    uses — no other file in this codebase needs to change, since every
    caller already only checks `.ok`.
  - Decide whether license verification should also be periodically
    RE-checked against the server (e.g. to handle a refund/chargeback)
    — today, once `licenseStatus` is 'licensed' locally, it stays that
    way forever with no server round-trip ever happening again. This is
    a real product decision, not a technical blocker, and was
    deliberately left out of V1.15's scope.
  - The multi-window same-context-only write-serialization limitation
    (documented above) is very unlikely to matter in practice but is
    worth a note if usage patterns ever suggest otherwise.
  - Everything already listed as a V1.14 Chrome Web Store blocker
    (Privacy Policy/Support/Store URLs still "(coming soon)", content/
    pagination.js's/content/content.js's diagnostic console.log output,
    the dead V1.5 per-file download queue, store listing assets) is
    still unresolved and unrelated to this version's scope.

============================================================
V1.16 — Commercial Release Architecture
============================================================
Goal: design (and scaffold, without integrating anything real) the
complete production commercialization flow on top of V1.15's trial/
license model. Explicitly NOT a feature-development version — no new
scraping capability, no Stripe or other real payment provider, no fake
production URLs or credentials, and every existing scraper feature
(Scrape/Results/Transforms/Filters/Sorting/Dedupe/CSV/Excel/JSON/Copy/
Image ZIP/Research Bundle/Monitoring/Notifications/History/Snapshots/
Saved Scrapers) preserved exactly as V1.15 left it — background.js was
not touched at all this version, and every one of V1.15's own tests
(test-license.js, test-popup-v115-trial.js,
test-background-v115-monitoring-unrestricted.js) still pass completely
unmodified, proving the trial-accounting contract itself never changed.

---- 1. Audit of the V1.15 implementation ----
Findings, all confirmed by re-reading utils/license.js and re-running
its full V1.15 test suite before making any change:
  - Trial accounting (consumeRunCredit/hasRunsRemaining/the idempotent
    charged-run ledger) is correct and complete — no changes needed or
    made to this logic in V1.16.
  - The ONLY path to `licenseStatus: 'licensed'` in the entire codebase
    was WSLicense.activateDevLicense() (DEV-only, gated behind
    chrome.management.getSelf() reporting an unpacked install) or the
    permanently-stubbed, always-failing activateLicense(). A production
    Chrome Web Store install therefore has NO way to ever reach
    'licensed' today — this is already the correct posture for "the
    server must be the source of truth", it just has no server to defer
    to yet.
  - Storage shape (schemaVersion 1, 6 fields: licenseStatus,
    trialRunsUsed, licenseKey, licenseVerifiedAt, chargedRunIds, plus
    the implicit schemaVersion) had no field for a purchase/order
    reference, no field distinguishing a dev-simulated license from a
    real one, and no concept of a license being later invalidated
    (refund/chargeback) — all three are required by this version's
    spec and did not exist in V1.15.
  - There was no concept of "verify with a server" at all — appropriate
    for V1.15 (no backend existed to call), but meant there was nowhere
    to attach V1.16's offline-behavior/anti-tamper design.
  - UI-wise, V1.15 only ever needed ONE modal message ("Free trial
    complete") and one Settings license line — V1.16's spec requires
    covering 10 additional UI states this simply didn't have room for.

---- 2. Complete production flow (design) ----
  Chrome Extension
    -> user clicks "Unlock Unlimited — $5" (trial-modal or Settings)
    -> WSLicense.createCheckoutSession(email?) calls POST
       {LICENSE_API_BASE_URL}/create-checkout
    -> backend creates a pending Purchase record, creates a session with
       the payment provider, returns {checkoutUrl}
    -> extension opens `checkoutUrl` in a NEW BROWSER TAB (never inside
       the popup — the popup closes as soon as focus leaves it, which is
       normal extension-popup behavior and does not interrupt a hosted
       checkout page running in its own tab)
    -> user completes (or cancels) payment on the hosted checkout page
       (a real payment provider's own hosted page — this codebase never
       collects card details itself)
    -> payment provider sends an asynchronous PAYMENT WEBHOOK to the
       backend (server-to-server, not through the extension at all) —
       this is the ONLY event the backend can fully trust as "a real
       payment happened"; a browser-side "redirected back successfully"
       signal is a UX convenience, never the source of truth
    -> backend verifies the webhook signature, marks the Purchase
       'paid', creates a License record, issues a license key/activation
       token
    -> the hosted checkout's SUCCESS redirect page (backend-hosted, not
       this extension) shows the license key/token and/or automatically
       hands it back to the extension — see "returning from checkout"
       below for the two supported mechanisms
    -> extension calls WSLicense.activateLicense(licenseKey) -> POST
       {LICENSE_API_BASE_URL}/activate-license
    -> backend validates the key against the License record, marks it
       "activated" (records device/activation metadata), returns a
       signed activation token
    -> extension persists {licenseStatus:'licensed', licenseKey,
       licenseVerifiedAt, verificationSource:'server'} via the EXACT
       SAME persist() path activateDevLicense() already uses (no new
       storage-writing code needed — see utils/license.js's own header
       comment on this)
    -> subsequent app opens: WSLicense.verifyLicenseWithServer() re-
       confirms periodically WHEN ONLINE (fail-open on any failure —
       see section 5); OFFLINE, the last-persisted licensed state is
       trusted directly from local storage, no network call attempted
    -> permanent Unlimited status, until/unless a future verify call
       receives an explicit {revoked:true} from the backend (refund/
       chargeback/dispute), which is the ONLY thing that ever downgrades
       'licensed' to 'revoked' (see utils/license.js's
       verifyLicenseWithServer — already implemented and tested this
       version, just never reachable yet since LICENSE_API_BASE_URL is
       still null)

  Returning from checkout — two supported mechanisms (decide when a
  backend is actually built; both are legitimate, this is a design
  choice with tradeoffs, not a technical blocker):
    (a) POLLING: after opening the checkout tab, the extension shows a
        "Waiting for payment confirmation…" state and polls
        GET {LICENSE_API_BASE_URL}/checkout-session/{id}/status every
        few seconds until it sees 'paid' (then auto-activates) or the
        user gives up/closes the tab. Simple, no new permissions, works
        even if the checkout tab's own redirect page never runs any
        extension-aware code.
    (b) MESSAGING: the checkout success page (backend-hosted, at a
        domain the extension would need host permission for, or via
        chrome.runtime.sendMessage's externally_connectable manifest
        key) directly notifies the extension of the license key. Faster
        UX (no polling delay) but needs the backend's success page to
        be extension-aware and needs a manifest.json externally_connectable
        entry naming the backend's exact domain once it's chosen — that
        domain does not exist yet, so this cannot be wired up now.
  V1.16 recommends (a) for the first real integration — no manifest
  changes needed, no coupling to a specific backend domain baked into
  the shipped extension ahead of time — with (b) as a possible later
  UX improvement.

---- 3. Backend API endpoints (contract) ----
  All under a single {LICENSE_API_BASE_URL} the extension does not yet
  know (utils/license.js's LICENSE_API_BASE_URL constant stays null
  until one is decided — spec: never invent a fake one). JSON in/out,
  HTTPS only. None of these are implemented server-side by this
  codebase — this is the CONTRACT the extension's stub functions
  (createCheckoutSession/activateLicense/verifyLicenseWithServer/
  recoverLicense, all in utils/license.js) are already written to call
  once a backend exists.

  POST /create-checkout
    Request:  { email?: string }
    Response: { ok: true, checkoutUrl: string, checkoutSessionId: string }
            | { ok: false, error: string }
    Creates a pending Purchase row and a payment-provider checkout
    session; returns the hosted URL to open in a new tab.

  POST /payment-webhook   (server-to-server only — the payment provider
                            calls this directly; the extension never
                            calls it and never sees its payload)
    Request: provider-specific signed event payload
    Response: 200 OK (or the provider's expected ack)
    On a verified "payment succeeded" event: marks the matching Purchase
    'paid', creates the License record, issues the license key/token.
    On a verified "refund"/"chargeback" event: marks the matching
    License 'revoked' — this is what a future verify-license call's
    revoked:true response comes from.

  GET /checkout-session/{id}/status
    Response: { status: 'pending' | 'paid' | 'cancelled' | 'expired', licenseKey?: string }
    Used by the extension's polling flow (section 2's mechanism (a)).

  POST /activate-license
    Request:  { licenseKey: string, deviceInfo?: { extensionVersion, chromeVersion } }
    Response: { ok: true, licenseStatus: 'licensed', licenseVerifiedAt: number }
            | { ok: false, reason: 'invalid' | 'already-activated' | 'revoked' | 'expired', error: string }
    Marks the License 'activated' server-side (records activation
    metadata for support/abuse-monitoring — this product's license is
    NOT seat-limited by spec, so "already-activated" is informational,
    not a hard block, unless a future decision changes that).

  POST /verify-license
    Request:  { licenseKey: string }
    Response: { valid: true } | { valid: false, revoked?: true }
    Called periodically by verifyLicenseWithServer() while online (see
    section 5). The extension's handling of this response is ALREADY
    implemented and tested: only an explicit `revoked:true` ever
    downgrades local state; anything else (including a network failure
    reaching this endpoint at all) leaves the local license alone.

  POST /recover-license
    Request:  { email: string }
    Response: { ok: true, licenseKey: string, purchaseId: string }
            | { ok: false, reason: 'not-found', error: string }
    Spec #7's "recover existing purchase/license" — looks up the most
    recent paid, non-revoked License for that email. Returning the raw
    licenseKey by email alone is a real security tradeoff (see section 6)
    — a production version of this endpoint should rate-limit
    aggressively and/or require an email-confirmation step before
    releasing the key, not just trust the email string as sent.

---- 4. Secure license data model ----
  This is the BACKEND's data model (does not exist in this codebase —
  no backend has been built) — the fields the spec explicitly named,
  plus the minimum extra needed to make revocation/support/anti-abuse
  actually work:
    License {
      licenseId          (primary key, server-generated, never guessable)
      licenseKey         (the token the extension holds locally — long,
                           random, NOT the primary key; rotatable
                           independently of licenseId if ever compromised)
      purchaseId         (foreign key -> Purchase; one Purchase should
                           normally produce exactly one License)
      paymentProvider     ('stripe' | ... — whichever is chosen; not yet decided)
      paymentStatus       ('pending' | 'paid' | 'refunded' | 'disputed')
      createdAt
      activatedAt          (null until /activate-license succeeds at least once)
      activationCount      (how many times /activate-license has ever succeeded —
                             informational; this product is NOT seat-limited by
                             spec, so this is for abuse-visibility, not enforcement,
                             unless a future decision changes that)
      customerEmail        (optional — spec explicitly allows it optional;
                             REQUIRED if the recovery flow is to work at all
                             for that purchase)
      revoked              (boolean, default false)
      revokedAt            (null until revoked)
      revokedReason         ('refund' | 'chargeback' | 'dispute' | 'manual' | null)
    }
  Purchase {
      purchaseId (primary key)
      paymentProviderSessionId  (the checkout-session id from the provider)
      amount / currency          (5.00 USD — fixed by spec, one-time, not a plan)
      status                     ('pending' | 'paid' | 'cancelled' | 'expired')
      createdAt
    }
  This mirrors the LOCAL client-side shape (utils/license.js's
  ws_license record) closely on purpose — purchaseId, customerEmail,
  and a paymentStatus-equivalent (licenseStatus locally) all already
  exist client-side today, ready to be filled from a real
  activate-license response instead of only the dev-simulate path.

---- 5. Offline behavior after successful activation ----
  Once `licenseStatus: 'licensed'` is persisted locally (from a real
  activation, exactly like the dev-simulated path already does today),
  the extension trusts that LOCAL state directly on every popup open —
  no network call is required or attempted just to use the extension.
  WSLicense.verifyLicenseWithServer() is the periodic re-check
  (implemented and tested this version): it is fail-OPEN by design —
  'not-configured' (today, always — no backend exists) and
  'network-error' (once one does, but this device can't reach it) both
  leave `licenseStatus` completely untouched; ONLY an explicit
  `{revoked:true}` response ever flips it to 'revoked'. `lastServerCheckAt`/
  `lastServerCheckResult`/the computed `daysSinceLastServerCheck` /
  `offlineGraceExceeded` fields exist so the UI CAN show "verified N days
  ago" (Settings' new #settings-license-verification-note does this
  today for a dev-simulated or server-verified license) without that
  ever being used to lock anyone out.
  OFFLINE_GRACE_DAYS (14, a named constant in utils/license.js) is
  DELIBERATELY informational only in V1.16 — `offlineGraceExceeded`
  becoming true does not currently change any gating behavior anywhere.
  See "remaining items requiring your decision" below: whether a
  license that hasn't successfully re-verified in a very long time
  (weeks/months of the extension never being online, or a leaked/shared
  license file being used far from any real activation) should
  EVENTUALLY be treated more cautiously is a real product/security
  tradeoff this version deliberately did not decide unilaterally.

---- 6. Protections against editing chrome.storage to fake Unlimited ----
  Blunt truth, stated plainly rather than oversold: a client-side
  browser extension can NEVER fully prevent a technically capable user
  from editing their OWN browser's local storage. No client-only check
  — however it's implemented — can be made unbypassable by the very
  person who controls the device it runs on. The correct goal is not
  "impossible to bypass" but "the server is the only thing that can
  create a legitimately-trusted 'licensed' state, and a locally-edited
  flag alone is worth nothing once real verification exists":
    - Today (V1.16, no backend): this is ALREADY true by construction —
      the only two ways to ever reach `licenseStatus: 'licensed'` are
      activateDevLicense() (gated behind an unpacked/dev-only install,
      impossible on a real Chrome Web Store install) and the permanently
      stub-failing activateLicense(). A production install literally has
      no code path that ever sets licensed to true from a local edit
      alone — editing chrome.storage.local.ws_license.licenseStatus to
      "licensed" by hand in DevTools DOES currently work (nothing re-
      verifies it), but this is the SAME category of limitation as any
      other purely-local flag and is explicitly why the rest of this
      section exists.
    - The real fix, once a backend exists: `verificationSource` (already
      in the V1.16 data model, values 'none'/'dev-simulated'/'server')
      should gate TRUST, not just describe it — a production build
      should treat a 'licensed' status with verificationSource other
      than 'server' as suspect and attempt an immediate
      verifyLicenseWithServer() call before fully trusting it (this is
      not yet implemented — the field exists and is populated correctly
      today, but nothing currently CONSUMES it for a trust decision;
      see remaining items).
    - A locally-edited licenseKey string alone should never be
      sufficient even after that: activate-license/verify-license should
      require the key to match a REAL, server-issued License record —
      an attacker who edits `licenseKey` to a plausible-looking made-up
      string still fails the moment any online verification runs,
      because the server has no matching record. The only way a local
      edit "wins" indefinitely is by ALSO ensuring verifyLicenseWithServer()
      never successfully runs (staying permanently offline) — which is
      the honest limit of client-side enforcement for any offline-
      capable product, not specific to this codebase.
    - Recommended (not yet implemented — a real backend is a
      prerequisite): issue licenseKey as a cryptographically signed
      token (e.g. a JWT-style structure) the extension can validate
      OFFLINE with an embedded public key, so even a plausible-looking
      hand-typed string fails signature verification instantly, without
      needing network access every single popup open. This still
      requires the periodic ONLINE verify-license check to catch
      revocation (a valid signature alone can't encode "revoked after
      the fact") — the two mechanisms are complementary, not
      alternatives.

---- 7. Extension UI architecture (states) ----
  All 10 states the spec named, and where each one lives today:
    - "10 free runs" / remaining runs: #header-trial-badge (V1.15,
      unchanged) — "10 runs left" down to "1 run left" down to "0 runs left".
    - Trial exhausted: #trial-modal-overlay, title "Free trial complete"
      (V1.15 copy, verbatim, unchanged) — shown by showTrialCompleteModal()
      when WSLicense.hasRunsRemaining() is false and uiStatus is NOT 'revoked'.
    - "Unlock Unlimited — $5": #trial-modal-unlock-btn (in the modal) and
      #settings-unlock-btn (always-available in Settings, not only when
      blocked) — both call the same handleUnlockPurchaseClick().
    - Activation success: handleUnlockPurchaseClick()'s success branch
      (real code, currently unreachable since activateLicense() always
      fails — exercised this version via a mocked activateLicense() in
      test-popup-v116-commercial.js) — closes the modal, refreshes the
      badge to "Unlimited", shows a clear success status message.
    - Invalid license: WSLicense.recoverLicense()'s
      {ok:false, reason:'not-found'} shape and activateLicense()'s
      reason:'invalid' are already defined in the contract (section 3);
      the recovery panel's handleSubmitRecover() already branches on
      `res.ok` and shows `res.error` — once a real backend returns
      reason:'not-found', the exact same code path shows it, no new
      code needed.
    - Payment cancelled: not a STORED state (the user simply returns
      from the checkout tab without a successful webhook ever firing) —
      see section 2's polling design; the "Waiting for payment
      confirmation…" UI (not yet built — there is no checkout to wait
      on) would simply time out or let the user close it and try again,
      no special "cancelled" record needed client-side.
    - License recovery: #settings-recover-btn / #settings-recover-panel
      (new this version) — an email field + Recover License / Cancel,
      wired to WSLicense.recoverLicense(), currently always shows the
      honest "not connected yet" message and never fakes a match.
    - Refunded/revoked license: `licenseStatus: 'revoked'` (new STATUSES
      value), a distinct trial-modal message ("License no longer
      active" / "Your license was refunded or revoked…", NOT the trial-
      exhausted copy), and a warning-styled note in Settings
      (#settings-license-verification-note.ws-license-verification-note-warn).
      DEV-only exercised via WSLicense.simulateRevokedDev() (a 3rd radio
      option alongside Trial/Licensed (DEV) in the same dev-only
      switcher V1.15 already built).
    - Network unavailable: verifyLicenseWithServer()'s fail-open
      'network-error' path (section 5) — today always short-circuits to
      'not-configured' instead (no URL configured yet), but the state
      machine and its `lastServerCheckResult` field are real and tested
      (test-license-v116.js exercises a genuinely rejected fetch via the
      test-only __fetchImpl hook).

---- 8. Placeholders / future backend & checkout URLs ----
  utils/license.js: `CHECKOUT_URL = null`, `LICENSE_API_BASE_URL = null`
  (both — no fake production URL invented, per spec). Every function
  that would need one (createCheckoutSession/activateLicense/
  recoverLicense/verifyLicenseWithServer) checks for null FIRST and
  fails closed with an honest "not connected yet in this build" message
  before attempting anything — none of them ever silently no-ops or
  pretends to succeed. `manifest.json` was NOT changed this version —
  no `host_permissions`/`externally_connectable` entry was added for a
  backend domain that doesn't exist yet (adding one now would either be
  a fake placeholder domain or premature commitment to a real one before
  it's chosen).

---- 9. Preserved scraper functionality ----
  background.js: zero changes this version (confirmed: no diff at all).
  content/*.js: zero changes this version (untouched, as always).
  popup.js: every V1.15 trial-accounting function (chargeRunCredit,
  maybeChargeForCompletedRun, trialAllowsNewRun, handlePreview/
  handleStartRun's gates) is completely unchanged — only the Settings/
  modal UI layer around license STATE gained new branches. Verified
  directly: test-popup-v115-trial.js's full 65 assertions still pass
  unmodified, and test-popup-v116-commercial.js's section G re-confirms
  Preview, CSV export, and the Transform/Filter/Sort/Dedupe panel toggles
  all still work exactly as before. Every other existing feature (Excel/
  JSON export, bulk ZIP downloads, Research Bundle, Monitoring,
  Notifications, History, Snapshots, any-count Saved Scrapers) received
  literally no code changes this version and is covered by its own
  still-passing, unmodified test file from V1.1-V1.15.

---- Files changed ----
  - utils/license.js: extended in place (schemaVersion 1 -> 2, additive
    fields, 4 new functions: simulateRevokedDev/createCheckoutSession/
    recoverLicense/verifyLicenseWithServer) — every V1.15 function
    signature and behavior byte-for-byte unchanged.
  - popup/popup.html: #settings-license-verification-note,
    #settings-recover-btn/-panel/-email/-submit-btn/-cancel-btn, a 3rd
    dev-switcher radio ("Revoked (DEV)"), two new Data & Privacy
    sentences about the future recovery/verification data flow — the
    V1.15 trial badge/modal markup is otherwise untouched.
  - popup/popup.css: one small new block (.ws-license-verification-note{-warn}),
    plus `input[type="email"]` added to the existing .ws-panel-inline
    input styling rule.
  - popup/popup.js: applyLicenseUI() gained the verification-note logic
    (additive, does not change any existing text this function already
    set); showTrialCompleteModal() branches on uiStatus==='revoked' for
    different copy (the trial-exhausted branch is verbatim-identical to
    V1.15); handleOpenSettings()'s dev-switcher radio selection now
    reads licenseStatus directly (trial/licensed/revoked) instead of an
    isLicensed boolean; handleDevLicenseSwitch() gained a 'revoked'
    branch; handleUnlockPurchaseClick() gained a real (currently
    unreachable) success branch; 3 new handlers
    (handleOpenRecoverPanel/handleCloseRecoverPanel/handleSubmitRecover).
  - manifest.json: version bumped to 1.16.0 only — no permission changes.
  - background/background.js, content/*.js: NOT touched.

---- Tests added ----
  - test-license-v116.js (44 assertions) — every new field's safe
    default on a fresh install; uiStatus across all 4 real combinations
    (trial-active/trial-exhausted/licensed/revoked); an EXACT V1.15
    6-field stored record migrating correctly with every new field
    defaulted and every old field preserved, then a subsequent write
    upgrading the persisted shape to the full V1.16 record;
    simulateRevokedDev() (including proving it never resets
    trialRunsUsed); createCheckoutSession()/recoverLicense() as honest,
    storage-untouched stubs; and verifyLicenseWithServer()'s full state
    machine via the test-only fetch/URL override hooks — not-configured
    (default), a genuinely rejected fetch proving the fail-OPEN
    guarantee (licenseStatus untouched), a valid:true response, and
    (CRITICAL) an explicit revoked:true response proving that is the
    ONLY thing that ever downgrades licensed -> revoked.
  - test-popup-v116-commercial.js (31 assertions) — a revoked license
    blocking a new Preview with the distinct "License no longer active"
    modal copy (never the generic trial-exhausted wording) and RUN_EXTRACTION
    never sent; the Settings verification note rendering correctly (or
    staying hidden) for trial/dev-simulated/revoked; the 3-way dev
    switcher starting on and correctly switching between all 3 states;
    the full license-recovery UI flow (open/validate-email/honest-stub-
    message/never-fakes-success/cancel); the "activation success" UI
    path exercised via a mocked future-real activateLicense() (closes
    the modal, shows a success message, badge updates to Unlimited); an
    exact V1.15-shaped stored record booting the full popup UI correctly
    with no crash; and a final re-confirmation that Preview, CSV export,
    and the Transform/Filter/Sort/Dedupe panels all still work.
  - test-license.js and test-popup-v115-trial.js (V1.15's own files) —
    re-run UNMODIFIED against the V1.16 code and still pass completely
    (108 combined assertions), proving nothing about the trial-accounting
    contract itself changed.

  Full regression result: all 42 scratchpad test files (V1.1 through
  V1.16) pass with zero failures.

---- Commercial release checklist (before any real money can move) ----
  [ ] Choose a payment provider (Stripe is the obvious default for a
      one-time $5 charge; not decided by this version per its own
      explicit instruction not to integrate one yet).
  [ ] Stand up the backend implementing the 6 endpoints in section 3
      (any stack; must terminate HTTPS, verify webhook signatures using
      the provider's own mechanism, and own the License/Purchase tables
      from section 4).
  [ ] Decide and configure LICENSE_API_BASE_URL / CHECKOUT_URL in
      utils/license.js once the backend has a real domain — this is the
      ONLY code change needed to "turn on" the whole flow client-side,
      by design.
  [ ] Build the hosted checkout success/cancel pages (backend-hosted,
      not part of this extension).
  [ ] Decide the checkout-return mechanism (section 2: polling vs.
      messaging) and, if messaging, add the resulting domain to
      manifest.json's externally_connectable.
  [ ] Implement the trust-escalation described in section 6
      (verificationSource-based re-verification) before shipping a real
      activateLicense() — do not just wire the network call without it.
  [ ] Decide the offline-grace enforcement policy (see "remaining items"
      below) and implement it in hasRunsRemaining()/consumeRunCredit() if
      the decision is to enforce anything at all.
  [ ] Write real backend-side tests (rate limiting on recover-license
      especially — section 3's own security note) — entirely outside
      this codebase's scope.
  [ ] Re-run this project's full regression suite one more time after
      LICENSE_API_BASE_URL is actually set, since that flips several
      "unreachable today" code paths (verifyLicenseWithServer's real
      branches, activateLicense's real branch) into live ones for the
      first time — they are unit-tested via mocks today, not integration-
      tested against a real server.
  [ ] Everything already listed as a V1.14/V1.15 Chrome Web Store
      blocker (Privacy Policy URL, content/pagination.js's/content.js's
      diagnostic console.log output, the dead V1.5 per-file download
      queue, store listing assets) — still unresolved, still unrelated
      to this version's scope.

---- Remaining items requiring a decision (not resolved by this version
     on purpose — genuine product/security tradeoffs, not oversights) ----
  - Should a licensed device that hasn't successfully verified online in
    a very long time (weeks/months) EVER be treated more cautiously
    (e.g. a soft warning, or eventually blocking new runs until it
    reconnects)? OFFLINE_GRACE_DAYS (14) exists as a starting number but
    enforces nothing today — this is a real tradeoff between piracy
    resistance and not punishing a legitimate offline-heavy user.
  - Should /activate-license actually LIMIT how many devices one
    licenseKey can activate on? Spec doesn't mention seat limits and
    this product is priced as a simple one-time unlock, not a metered
    seat license — the data model tracks activationCount for visibility
    either way, but nothing currently enforces a cap.
  - Recover-license-by-email-alone (section 3) is convenient but a real
    security tradeoff — worth deciding whether to add an email-
    confirmation step (a short-lived verification code/link) before a
    production launch, rather than returning the licenseKey directly to
    whoever types a matching email.
  - Which payment provider, and Stripe Checkout vs. a custom-hosted
    payment page — a real decision with real tradeoffs (fees, PCI scope,
    implementation time) this version deliberately left open.
  - The checkout-return mechanism (polling vs. messaging, section 2) —
    a real UX/architecture decision, not just an implementation detail.

============================================================
V1.17 — Advanced Selection & Auto Detect Engine
============================================================
Goal: substantially strengthen field selection and automatic detection —
closing extraction-capability gaps versus leading scraper extensions —
while staying strictly within V1.x's scope (raw data collection). No
statistical analysis, charts, market recommendations, AI recommendations,
or "what you should do" insights were added anywhere; V2.0 remains a
future, separate phase.

AUDIT FIRST (spec's own explicit instruction): before writing anything,
content/selector.js, content/autodetect.js, content/content.js, and
content/scraper.js were fully re-read. Finding: this codebase's selector-
generation and Auto Detect engines were ALREADY remarkably close to
spec's targets from earlier versions — id/stable-class/data-attribute
priority selector generation, dynamic/generated-class-name filtering,
shadow-DOM-aware traversal, whole-page repeating-structure scanning with
scoring, semantic field naming (Price/Author/Date/Rating/Count), and
native HTML `<table>` detection (headers/rows/cells) all already existed
and were already well-tested. V1.17 therefore deliberately did NOT
rewrite any of that — every addition below is strictly ADDITIVE on top
of it, per spec #13's explicit instruction, and every pre-existing test
(test-selector.js, test-selector-2.js, test-selector-3.js,
test-autodetect.js, test-image-extraction.js, test-pagination.js) passes
completely unmodified.

---- 1. Advanced Element Picker (content/content.js) ----
  - Hover highlighting is now requestAnimationFrame-throttled (with a
    setTimeout(16ms) fallback for any environment without a real paint
    loop) — a burst of mousemove events between two frames now produces
    at most one highlight-position DOM write, never one-per-event (spec
    #14 performance).
  - A new "what will be selected" hover label (spec #1) shows a cheap
    tag+class description (e.g. "span.price") that follows the cursor
    BEFORE the user commits with a click — computed without running the
    expensive selector-generation algorithm on every hovered element.
  - The naming panel now shows, in the spec's own worked-example shape:
    Field Name / **Selector** (the real generated selector text, new) /
    **Match count** ("N matches", reworded from "N similar items") /
    Extraction type. A compact **quality tag** (Good/Fair/Fragile, see
    #12 below) sits next to the selector.
  - Extraction types extended: **HTML content** (raw innerHTML, never
    trimmed/normalized) and **Attribute…** (any arbitrary attribute name
    — data-*, aria-*, title, alt, srcset, anything) are now offered
    alongside the existing Text/Link URL/Image source/Alt text options,
    for every element type (spec #1/#7). Choosing Attribute… reveals a
    text input for the attribute name; the live example preview updates
    as it's typed.
  - **Advanced** (spec #16 progressive disclosure): a collapsed-by-
    default toggle reveals the Live Selector Tester (#3 below) — never
    shown for a shadow-DOM (array-form) selector, which has no safe plain-
    text representation; the section is simply omitted with the quality
    tag alone still shown, rather than silently broken (spec #15).
  - A power user's edited Advanced selector becomes what "Add Column"
    actually SAVES, not just what's tested — the precise-control path
    spec #1 explicitly asks for (verified end to end in
    test-picker-v117.js's TEST F: the saved relativeSelector is the
    user's edited one, not the auto-generated original).

---- 2. Selector Generation Engine ----
  Unchanged in its actual GENERATION algorithm (id > stable classes >
  safe data-*/semantic attributes > uniqueness-search over ancestor
  chains > nth-child fallback) — already exactly spec's described
  priority order, already avoiding brittle nth-child chains whenever a
  more stable signal exists, already filtering out random-looking
  generated class/id names (isLikelyDynamicClass/Id). What's genuinely
  NEW is WSSelector.scoreSelectorQuality(selector) (#12) — a purely
  informational scoring layer OVER an already-generated selector, never
  fed back into generation itself, so nothing about existing saved
  selectors' actual VALUES changes.

---- 3. Live Selector Tester (content/content.js, inside the Advanced
     section) ----
  Tests the SAME semantics real extraction uses — the same container
  instances content/scraper.js's runExtraction would use (containerSelector
  ? document.querySelectorAll(containerSelector) : [document.body]) — not
  a disconnected page-wide query, so what the tester reports is exactly
  what a real scrape would produce. Shows, live as the user types
  (200ms-debounced — spec #14):
    - "MATCHES: N" (or "N of M items" when scoped to a repeating
      container), or a clear "0 matches" when nothing matches.
    - A preview of up to 5 real extracted values (spec's own worked
      example format).
    - On-page highlighting of every matched element (capped at 60 — spec
      #14 — via a plain inline outline restored exactly on the next test
      or on exit, never a leaked global stylesheet class).
    - An explicit invalid-selector check (document.querySelectorAll(...)
      syntax validation, run BEFORE the extraction-safe
      WSSelector.queryFromScope() call, which deliberately swallows a bad
      selector internally for real-extraction robustness and would
      otherwise indistinguishably report "0 matches" for a genuinely
      broken selector) — shows "Invalid selector." rather than crashing
      or silently misleading the user (spec #3/#15).

---- 4. Repeating Item / Row Detection ----
  Already handled by the pre-existing WSSelector.findRepeatingContainer()
  (climbs from a clicked element looking for the outermost contiguous
  level of tag+stable-class-matching siblings) and
  WSAutoDetect.findCandidateGroups() (whole-page grouping + scoring +
  dedupeCandidates() to avoid merging unrelated repeating structures) —
  both already correct and already tested across product cards, list
  items, table rows, and shadow-DOM custom-element feeds (Reddit-style).
  No changes made; V1.17 only added the NEW field-level Advanced/tester
  UI on top.

---- 5. Auto Detect 2.0 ----
  Additive on top of the already-strong existing engine:
    - A new generic **"Description"** naming heuristic (a non-first text
      field whose average sample length is >=60 chars) — applies equally
      to product/job/article/listing descriptions, deliberately checked
      only for the SECOND-or-later text field so a long headline is
      still correctly named "Title", never misnamed "Description" purely
      for being lengthy.
    - Every detected field (both repeating-card and table-row detection)
      now carries `fieldType` (#6) and `quality` (#12) metadata.
    - Verified generic (spec's explicit "not mandatory e-commerce"
      requirement) against a real-estate-style listing fixture with zero
      marketplace-specific code (test-autodetect-v117.js TEST D) — Price/
      Image/Link detection all work identically for houses as products,
      confirming the engine is domain-structure-driven, not hardcoded.

---- 6. Field Type Detection (metadata only) ----
  WSSelector.detectFieldType(samples) — classifies an already-extracted
  sample array into currency/number/url/date/text/empty via regex
  agreement-ratio heuristics (>=60% of non-empty samples must match,
  mirroring the existing guessSemanticName's "don't overclaim" pattern —
  a low-agreement mix always falls back to 'text', never a false-
  confident guess). Purely informational: NEVER mutates the extracted
  samples, never alters what gets saved/exported — shown only as a small
  badge in the Auto Detect field list (e.g. "Currency-like") and (via
  quality) in the picker panel. Explicitly NOT statistical analysis of
  the full dataset — a small per-field sample classification only,
  staying inside V1.x's raw-collection scope.

---- 7. Attribute Extraction ----
  New `attribute: 'attr'` column type + a new `attributeName` field
  (e.g. `{attribute:'attr', attributeName:'data-product-id'}`) — the
  value is `el.getAttribute(attributeName) || ''`, always the raw,
  unmodified attribute value. Missing/empty attributeName safely returns
  '' rather than erroring. Fully wired through the real pipeline: the
  picker panel's Attribute… option -> saved column ->
  content/scraper.js's runExtraction (which now passes `col.attributeName`
  as extractValue's 4th parameter, a no-op for every other existing
  attribute value) -> WSRecipes save/load — verified end to end in both
  test-picker-v117.js (TEST G) and test-migration-v117.js.

---- 8. Image Extraction Improvements ----
  Already comprehensive from an earlier version — srcset parsing +
  best-candidate selection (width/density descriptors), a lazy-load
  attribute list (data-src/data-original/data-lazy-src/data-lazy/
  data-image + data-srcset), `<picture>/<source>` ancestor resolution,
  and generic icon/tracking-pixel scoring/rejection — all of spec #8's
  explicit requirements were already met. No changes made; confirmed via
  the full, unmodified, still-passing test-image-extraction.js (22
  assertions).

---- 9. Link Extraction Improvements ----
  Already comprehensive — WSSelector.resolveUrl() uses the real
  `new URL(url, document.baseURI).href` browser API, which correctly
  handles relative paths, protocol-relative URLs, anchors, and query
  parameters natively (this is the actual browser URL-resolution engine,
  not a hand-rolled regex). javascript: URLs are explicitly filtered out
  by hrefFromAnchor(). No changes made; spec #9 was already fully
  satisfied.

---- 10. Table Detection ----
  Native `<table>` detection (headers from `<thead>`, rows grouped by
  structural signature so non-uniform legacy layout tables don't corrupt
  detection, per-row-group scoring) already existed. V1.17 adds **colspan
  handling**: a new flattenRowCells(rowEl) helper repeats a colSpan cell
  reference across the column-index positions it visually occupies, so a
  wide summary/merged cell in one row no longer shifts every LATER
  column's positional alignment for that row (verified: a table mixing 8
  uniform rows with one 2-column-colspan row keeps Price correctly
  aligned across all 8 normal rows). rowSpan (a cell spanning DOWN into
  later rows) is a documented, deliberate limitation — NOT carried
  forward into subsequent rows, since correct rowspan tracking requires
  a full virtual-grid algorithm whose complexity risk (spec's own "do not
  allow malformed tables to crash Auto Detect" matters more than perfect
  rowspan fidelity) outweighs the benefit; a rowspan'd column simply
  shows blank on rows it has no literal cell for, the same as any other
  honestly-missing value.

---- 11. Preview Improvements ----
  Auto Detect's structure-meta line now reads "Detected dataset: N rows,
  M columns" (spec's own worked-example wording, was "N repeating items
  found") — computed from the structure's real itemCount and its full
  (not just checked) field count. Each field row already showed a live
  sample + coverage %; V1.17 adds the new fieldType badge and a
  "Fragile selector" warning badge (with its reasons in a tooltip) so a
  likely-to-break selection is visible BEFORE running a large scrape,
  per spec's explicit intent.

---- 12. Selector Health / Quality ----
  WSSelector.scoreSelectorQuality(selector) — a 0-100 score + a single
  Good/Fair/Fragile label (spec: "do not overwhelm normal users with
  technical scoring" — only the one-word label is shown in the basic
  picker view; the underlying `reasons` array is available as a tooltip/
  for tests, never dumped into the main UI). Dimensions, all purely
  structural (never a claim about the underlying DATA — spec: "do not
  claim certainty"): a leading #id (+25), meaningful class/data-attribute/
  semantic-attribute segments (+10 each family), each :nth-child()/
  :nth-of-type() step (-15, capped -35 total — the spec's own explicit
  brittle-selector example, `body > div:nth-child(4) > div:nth-child(2)`,
  scores Fragile), a generated-looking class name slipping through
  (-20), and excessive combinator depth (-5/level past 4). A shadow-DOM
  array-form selector always scores a neutral 'Fair' (its per-hop pieces
  are already the most-stable-available choice by construction, so
  there's nothing further to usefully score). Every detected Auto Detect
  field and every picker panel's selector carries this metadata now.

---- 13. Saved Scraper Compatibility (CRITICAL) ----
  Verified directly, not just assumed: an EXACT pre-V1.17 saved scraper
  (columns with only {id, name, relativeSelector, attribute} — no
  attributeName/fieldType/quality field anywhere in storage) extracts
  100% identically under V1.17's code (test-migration-v117.js TEST A). A
  scraper mixing an OLD-shaped column and a NEW attr-typed column, saved
  through the real WSRecipes layer (not just raw storage.js), round-trips
  and extracts correctly end to end (TEST B), as does an html-typed
  column (TEST C). Updating an existing legacy scraper to ADD a new
  attr-typed column preserves its id/name/createdAt unchanged (TEST D).
  No selector was ever silently rewritten — buildRelativeSelector/
  buildContainerSelector/findRepeatingContainer are byte-for-byte
  unchanged from V1.16. Monitoring, Snapshots, History, Research, and
  Transforms configuration objects were not touched by any V1.17 file
  and are proven unaffected by the full, unmodified V1.1-V1.16 regression
  suite passing alongside every new V1.17 test.

---- 14. Performance ----
  Mousemove hover: requestAnimationFrame-throttled (see #1). Live
  Selector Tester: 200ms-debounced re-testing, on-page highlight capped
  at 60 elements. Auto Detect's existing budgets (MAX_SCAN_ELEMENTS 6000,
  MAX_TIME_MS 4000, MAX_CANDIDATES 8, COVERAGE_SAMPLE_SIZE 30) were
  already in place and untouched. Every highlight/listener is cleaned up
  on Cancel/Esc/exitPickMode — clearMultiHighlight() restores each
  highlighted element's EXACT prior inline outline style (never leaves a
  style leaked on the real page — verified in test-picker-v117.js TEST H),
  and the tester's debounce timer is cleared too, so no stray timeout can
  fire an update after the panel has already closed.

---- 15. Error Handling ----
  Invalid selector syntax: explicitly caught and shown clearly (#3),
  never crashes. Zero matches: explicitly and clearly shown ("0
  matches"), never confused with an error. Huge DOM trees / malformed
  tables: Auto Detect's pre-existing time/element budgets and per-table
  try/catch-free-but-defensive cell access (colSpan-flattened, never
  assumes a fixed cell count) mean a genuinely malformed table degrades
  to partial/skipped detection rather than crashing (verified:
  test-autodetect-v117.js TEST B, every row colspan'd). Shadow DOM: an
  element living inside a CLOSED shadow root remains unreachable (as
  always — traversal simply stops there); inside an OPEN shadow root, the
  existing shadow-aware traversal (unchanged) still works, but the
  Advanced Live Selector Tester is deliberately NOT offered for an
  array-form (multi-hop) selector — explained via its simple absence
  rather than a broken/silently-wrong text box (spec #15's explicit "if
  something cannot be supported, explain it clearly rather than failing
  silently"). Detached elements / DOM mutations during picking: picking
  resolves synchronously from the click event's own target, so there is
  no async window where the target could disappear mid-pick.

---- 16. UX Rule (progressive disclosure) ----
  The default beginner path is completely unchanged: AUTO DETECT ->
  PREVIEW -> RUN, or click an element -> name it -> Add Column, with only
  Field Name / Selector / Match count / Extraction type visible by
  default. Every new advanced capability (selector text editing, the
  Live Selector Tester, arbitrary attribute names, quality-score reasons)
  sits behind the explicit "Advanced" toggle or the "Attribute…" choice
  a user has to deliberately make — nothing new is forced into the
  default view.

---- Files changed ----
  - content/selector.js: +WSSelector.detectFieldType, +scoreSelectorQuality;
    extractValue() gained 'html' and 'attr' (+ a new attributeName 4th
    param) branches — every existing branch (text/href/src/alt) is
    byte-for-byte unchanged.
  - content/scraper.js: runExtraction's single extractValue() call site
    now also passes col.attributeName (undefined/no-op for every
    existing column).
  - content/autodetect.js: +flattenRowCells (colspan alignment), +the
    Description naming heuristic, +fieldType/quality attached to every
    returned field (both card- and table-detection paths).
  - content/content.js: rAF-throttled hover + a new hover label;
    renderPanel() extended with a Selector display + quality tag,
    HTML/Attribute… extraction options + an attribute-name input, and
    the whole Advanced/Live-Selector-Tester section; +applyMultiHighlight/
    clearMultiHighlight; cleanup wired into exitPickMode/stopCapturing.
  - popup/popup.js: +fieldTypeLabel(); attrLabel() extended for 'html'/
    'attr'; the Auto Detect panel's structure-meta wording updated + a
    fieldType/quality-warning badge added per field row.
  - popup/popup.html, popup/popup.css: no structural changes beyond the
    two small field-type/quality-warning CSS classes.
  - manifest.json: version bumped to 1.17.0 only — no permission changes
    (every new feature is pure client-side DOM analysis, same as before).
  - background/background.js, utils/license.js, utils/recipes.js,
    utils/snapshots.js, utils/research.js, utils/transforms.js,
    utils/downloads.js, utils/zip.js, utils/xlsx.js, utils/csv.js: NOT
    touched at all this version.

---- Tests added ----
  - test-selector-v117.js (35 assertions) — detectFieldType across
    currency/number/url/date/text/empty/low-agreement-mix; html/attr
    extraction (including missing/empty attributeName never crashing,
    and every existing extraction type proven completely unaffected);
    scoreSelectorQuality across id/class/nth-child/dynamic-class/data-
    attribute/:scope/shadow-array selectors, plus a fuzz-style pass over
    5 malformed/edge-case inputs proving it NEVER returns NaN or an
    undocumented label.
  - test-autodetect-v117.js (29 assertions) — colspan alignment across
    mixed uniform/colspan table rows (never crashes, Price stays aligned
    for every normal row), a table where EVERY row has a colspan cell,
    the Description heuristic (correctly distinguishing it from a long-
    but-still-first Title field), a real-estate-style non-e-commerce
    listing proving the engine's genericness, and fieldType/quality
    metadata correctness + non-mutation of raw samples.
  - test-picker-v117.js (31 assertions) — the ONLY content.js-driving
    test in this project so far: throttled hover highlight + hover
    label, the panel's Selector/match-count/quality read-out, HTML and
    Attribute… extraction (including the live example updating), the
    full Live Selector Tester (valid/zero-match/genuinely-invalid-syntax,
    on-page highlighting proven via real inline style inspection), a
    power user's edited selector actually being what gets SAVED, a full
    attr-typed column round trip through the REAL extraction pipeline
    (20/20 rows, correct per-row values), and Esc cleanup leaving zero
    leaked highlight styling on the page.
  - test-migration-v117.js (16 assertions) — see #13 above.
  - Bug found & fixed while writing these tests: the Live Selector
    Tester's original invalid-selector detection relied on
    WSSelector.queryFromScope() throwing, but that function deliberately
    SWALLOWS a bad selector internally (the right behavior for real
    extraction robustness) — so it never actually signaled "invalid" to
    the tester, indistinguishably reporting "0 matches" for a genuinely
    broken selector instead. Fixed by adding an explicit
    document.querySelectorAll() syntax-validation pre-check in
    content.js's own tester code (never touching queryFromScope's real-
    extraction-safe behavior at all) before falling through to the
    normal per-container query loop.

  Full regression result: all 46 scratchpad test files (V1.1 through
  V1.17) pass with zero failures — ~1,458 total assertions.

---- Known limitations ----
  - Table rowSpan is not carried into subsequent rows (documented
    deliberate scope decision, see #10).
  - The Live Selector Tester is unavailable for a shadow-DOM (array-form)
    relativeSelector — the quality tag and every other panel feature
    still work, only the free-text editor/tester is omitted.
  - Multi-highlight on-page outlines are capped at 60 elements for very
    high-match-count selectors (a display cap only — the reported match
    COUNT is always exact, never capped).
  - detectFieldType/guessSemanticName remain simple regex/ratio
    heuristics, not exhaustive locale-aware parsers — e.g. unusual
    currency symbols or non-English date formats may fall back to
    'text'/no semantic name rather than misclassifying, which is the
    intended safe-default behavior, not a bug.
  - content/pagination.js's/content/content.js's pre-existing diagnostic
    console.log output (flagged back in V1.14's release audit, still
    unresolved) is unrelated to this version's scope.

============================================================
V1.18 readiness
============================================================
V1.17 leaves the extraction/selection layer substantially stronger and
fully backward compatible, with zero known regressions across the whole
V1.1-V1.17 suite. Nothing about this version blocks V1.18 (Subpage / Deep
Scraping) from starting cleanly: the column model gained exactly one new
optional field (attributeName) and two purely-informational metadata
fields (fieldType, quality) that V1.18 can ignore entirely if irrelevant
to subpage work, or reuse (e.g. attribute-based subpage-link detection)
if useful. content/content.js's picker panel architecture (Advanced
toggle, Live Selector Tester scaffolding) is a reasonable pattern to
extend for a future "pick a subpage link column" flow, but V1.17
deliberately did not anticipate or half-build any V1.18 functionality.

============================================================
V1.18 — Subpage / Deep Scraping Engine
============================================================
Goal: let a user follow a Link column from a list page out to each item's
detail page and merge additional fields back into the SAME table — still
strictly raw data collection (no statistics/charts/AI/business
recommendations; that stays V2.x). Depth is intentionally fixed at
exactly 1 — this is not a recursive crawler and never will be inside V1.x.

---- 1. Architecture ----
  A hybrid two-step pipeline per URL, both steps living in
  background.js (a service worker, so it survives popup close/reopen):
    Step 1 — deepScrapeFetch()/validateDetailUrl(): a lightweight
      fetch() that reads ONLY status, content-type and the final
      (post-redirect) URL, then immediately cancels the response body
      (res.body.cancel()) without ever parsing it. Classifies the
      outcome (classifyHttpFailure): 404/403 are permanent, 429/5xx are
      transient and retryable, a non-HTML content-type is rejected
      outright (never DOM-extracted).
    Step 2 — extractDetailFields(): only reached if step 1 passed.
      Reuses V1.8 Monitoring's EXACT tab lifecycle — chrome.tabs.create
      ({active:false}) -> waitForTabComplete -> chrome.scripting.
      executeScript(CONTENT_FILES) -> sendMessageToTab('RUN_DETAIL_
      EXTRACTION') -> chrome.tabs.remove() in a finally block — so the
      real browser (and the SAME selector engine V1.17 already tested)
      does the actual field extraction, never a hand-rolled HTML parser.
  Splitting validation (cheap, no visible tab) from extraction (a real
  tab) means a 404/403/blocked/wrong-content-type detail page never
  opens a tab at all — the majority of failure cases in practice cost
  nothing but one fetch().

---- 2. Detail-page field configuration & Element Picker reuse ----
  A new "Deep Scraping" panel (Results tab) lets a user enable Deep
  Scraping, pick a source column (any column typed as a Link, auto-
  offered), and add detail fields two ways:
    - manually (name + selector + extraction type, reusing V1.17's full
      type list: Text/HTML/Link/Image/Attribute incl. arbitrary data-*/
      aria-*, plus a "match multiple — extract all" checkbox), or
    - via the picker: content.js's picker gained a new 'detail-field'
      purpose. Clicking "Pick from a sample page" opens the FIRST
      unique detail URL in a new tab, activates the SAME picker UI
      V1.17 already built, and LOOPS ("Add Field" -> back into pick
      mode) so multiple fields can be picked in one session before
      "Done" returns the picks to the popup via a session-storage relay
      key (ws_detail_field_picks::<hostname>) — no new picker engine.
  A DETAIL PREVIEW ("Test on a sample page") runs the real 2-step
  pipeline against ONE sample URL before committing to a full run,
  showing each configured field as found/missing so a broken selector
  is caught before hundreds of requests go out.

---- 3. Data schema ----
  Saved Scraper (utils/recipes.js), fully additive and defaulted:
    deepScrape: {
      enabled: boolean,
      sourceColumnId: string|null,
      fields: [{ id, name, relativeSelector, attribute, attributeName,
                 multiple: 'first'|'all' }],
      concurrency: number (1-8, default 4),
      delayMode: 'auto'|'custom', customDelayMs: number|null
    }
  A scraper saved before V1.18 simply lacks this key; normalizeDeepScrape
  self-heals it to emptyDeepScrape() (disabled, no fields) on both load
  and update — verified with an EXACT pre-V1.18 saved-scraper record
  (test-popup-v118-deepscrape.js TEST G).
  Run state (chrome.storage.session, key ws_deepscrape_run — the SAME
  "one blob, storage.onChanged-driven" pattern as ws_run::<hostname>/
  ws_zip_run/ws_download_run):
    { runId, status: 'preparing'|'running'|'completed'|'stopped'|'error',
      fields, results: { <url>: { status: 'pending'|'fetching'|
        'completed'|'partial'|'failed'|'skipped', fields, finalUrl,
        httpStatus, error } }, counts, currentUrl, startedAt, updatedAt,
      finishedAt }

---- 4. URL normalization & permissions ----
  Every candidate URL from the source column passes WSDownloads.
  validateDownloadUrl (V1.5/V1.13.2, reused verbatim) — javascript:/
  data:/chrome:/chrome-extension: schemes are rejected before ever being
  queued (defense-in-depth: rejected again inside background.js right
  before fetch(), marked 'skipped', never silently dropped). Relative/
  protocol-relative URLs resolve against the list page's own URL.
  Host permission is requested via WSDownloads.uniqueOriginPatterns
  (the SAME per-origin-pattern helper V1.5's Bulk Download already
  uses) at the real Start-click user gesture in popup.js — never from
  the service worker, which has no user-gesture context. A decline
  shows a clear "Permission was declined" message and never starts.

---- 5. Deduplication & row alignment (CRITICAL, both spec-flagged) ----
  computeUniqueDetailUrls() collects only the UNIQUE, valid detail URLs
  across every row before anything is sent to background.js — a 4-row
  dataset with 2 rows sharing one detail URL sends exactly 3 requests,
  never 4 (test-popup-v118-deepscrape.js TEST A/C). Results are keyed
  by URL, not by row position, so mergeDeepScrapeResults() maps each
  result back to EVERY row whose source column held that URL — proven
