  with a duplicate-URL fixture where both sharing rows independently
  receive the identical merged values, and a 3rd, unrelated row
  succeeds on its own data. A row whose URL failed gets EMPTY values
  for the new columns (rendered as the table's pre-existing '—' missing-
  cell convention) — its position and every other column are untouched;
  nothing ever shifts between rows.

---- 6. Concurrency & pacing & retries ----
  runDeepScrape() reuses runWithConcurrency() — the exact, already-
  tested worker-pool limiter from V1.13.2's ZIP pipeline — with a
  conservative default of 4 (1-8, user-adjustable), so a large job never
  opens hundreds of visible tabs at once. Between one worker's own
  dispatches, a delay applies: 'auto' (DEEP_SCRAPE_AUTO_DELAY_MS =
  400ms, a safe default) or a user-set 'custom' delay. A transient
  failure (429/5xx/timeout/network error) gets up to DEEP_SCRAPE_MAX_
  ATTEMPTS = 3 tries with increasing backoff (1s, 2s) — a permanent one
  (404/403/non-HTML) never retries. No infinite loops; more advanced
  retry/resume UX is explicitly left to V1.20.

---- 7. Per-page & per-field status, missing values, multiple matches ----
  Each URL's result is 'completed' (extraction ran and at least one
  field got a real value), 'partial' (page loaded fine, every field
  came back empty — a real, distinct signal from a page that failed
  outright), or 'failed' (never got a usable response). A field whose
  selector matches nothing on that particular page is simply empty —
  never a crash, never a dropped row (verified generically across five
  fixture shapes, see #14 below). A "match multiple — extract all"
  field returns a JSON array (e.g. ["Bluetooth","Wireless","USB-C"]),
  serialized predictably by the existing CSV/Excel/JSON export code —
  never silently collapsed to one value.

---- 8. Progress, Stop/Cancel ----
  Progress renders live from ws_deepscrape_run's storage.onChanged
  events: X/Y pages, Completed/Failed/Remaining counts, %, and the
  current URL. Stop (AbortController, mirroring the ZIP pipeline's
  cancellation pattern) stops scheduling new work and lets in-flight
  requests settle safely — already-collected results are preserved and
  merged, never discarded, and the main dataset is never corrupted.
  Because the whole operation lives in the service worker, closing and
  reopening the popup does not kill an active run; popup.js's init()
  reconnects to any still-running ws_deepscrape_run on reopen.

---- 9. Result merging & export/Research/Snapshot compatibility ----
  Merged detail columns are appended (never inserted/reordered) via a
  single chokepoint: computeTransformedResult() concatenates
  deepScrapeColumns onto state.columns before handing both to
  WSTransforms.applyTransforms — state.columns ITSELF is never touched.
  Because every downstream feature (Transform/Filter/Sort/Dedupe/
  Preview/CSV/Excel/JSON/Copy/Snapshots/Research) already reads from
  this one function's output, all of them see merged data automatically
  with zero feature-specific changes. A colliding field name (e.g. a
  detail "Price" field alongside the list's own "Price" column) is
  deterministically renamed "Price (detail)"; a non-colliding name
  keeps its own. Verified end-to-end: a real CSV export of a merged,
  deduped, partially-failed dataset (test-popup-v118-deepscrape.js
  TEST C) and a Research Bundle asset plan/manifest built from a
  deep-scraped-style image column with a cross-row shared URL
  (test-deepscrape-v118-coverage.js PART 3) — both needed ZERO changes
  to utils/research.js/downloads.js, since they already operate
  generically on whatever columns/rows they're handed.

---- 10. Monitoring safety (explicit spec choice) ----
  DECISION: Option A — Monitoring remains list-page only by default.
  background.js's runScheduledScrape() (V1.8) was not modified for
  V1.18 at all and never reads scraper.deepScrape; a saved scraper can
  have deepScrape.enabled=true and Deep Scraping is STILL never
  performed by a scheduled run. Proven directly: a scraper seeded with
  deepScrape.enabled=true, monitoring.enabled=true fires its alarm and
  completes normally with zero fetch() calls and zero RUN_DETAIL_
  EXTRACTION tab messages, only its ordinary single RUN_EXTRACTION
  (test-deepscrape-v118-coverage.js PART 2). Automatic deep monitoring
  (Option B) is left for a future version to design deliberately, with
  its own explicit opt-in and rate-limiting story, rather than being
  silently bolted on here.

---- 11. Trial/license accounting ----
  Unchanged 10-free-then-$5 model (V1.15/V1.16). One Deep Scrape
  operation charges exactly ONE credit, on the run's 'completed'
  terminal status only (never 'stopped'/'error', never per detail
  page) — the exact WSLicense.consumeRunCredit(runId) idempotent-by-
  runId contract already used everywhere else. At 0 credits, Start
  Deep Scrape shows the trial-complete modal and never sends
  START_DEEP_SCRAPE. No real payment provider integrated; no fake
  purchase verification added.

---- 12. Security ----
  Fetched page content is treated as fully untrusted. The validation
  fetch() never reads the response body at all. The extraction step
  never parses raw HTML strings in the popup/background — it re-uses
  the browser's own script execution model (a real, isolated tab +
  content-script injection), so no remote page script is ever executed
  by extension code, and nothing fetched is ever assigned via innerHTML
  in extension UI (results render via textContent throughout, matching
  the pre-existing preview-table convention). scripts/release-check.js's
  existing eval()/new Function() scan still passes clean.

---- 13. Memory & large-job safeguards ----
  Each detail tab is created, extracted, and removed (chrome.tabs.remove
  in a finally block) before the next slot's tab opens — no parsed DOM
  or open tab is ever held onto past its own extraction. runWithConcurrency
  keeps at most `concurrency` tabs open simultaneously regardless of
  total job size, so a 10,000-row job with (say) 4,000 unique URLs never
  opens more than a handful of tabs at once. No arbitrary row-count cap
  was added.

---- 14. General-Purpose Requirement — proof ----
  runDetailExtraction (content/scraper.js) is the SAME selector/
  extraction engine V1.17 already built for the list-page picker —
  nothing subpage-specific was added to it. Proven with realistic,
  non-e-commerce fixtures in test-deepscrape-v118-coverage.js PART 1:
  Job listing->Job detail (company/location/description/multi-tag),
  Property listing->Property detail (price/agent name+link/multi-
  photo with relative-URL resolution), Article listing->Article page
  (author/date/HTML body), and Generic directory->Profile page
  (specialty/arbitrary data-* attribute/bio) — plus product/e-commerce
  fixtures already covered by the other two V1.18 test files.

---- Tests ----
  test-background-deepscrape-v118.js (37 assertions): the full fetch-
  validate + tab-extract engine — success, permanent (404) vs transient
  (500, retried) failures, retry exhaustion, timeout/network error,
  non-HTML rejection (no tab opened), redirect tracking, concurrency
  cap, Stop preserving partial results, missing permission, the scheme
  defense-in-depth check, and a page that loads but every field is
  missing ('partial').
  test-popup-v118-deepscrape.js (41 assertions): the config panel
  (source column, manual field CRUD, Attribute… reveal, workload
  summary with real dedup counts), a full run (permission request,
  exact unique-URL count sent, progress rendering, duplicate-URL row
  mapping, failed-row empty values, column-collision renaming, original-
  column preservation, trial charge exactly once, CSV export on the
  merged dataset), the 0-credit trial gate, a declined permission, a
  saved-scraper persistence round trip, and legacy (no deepScrape field)
  scraper compatibility.
  test-deepscrape-v118-coverage.js (27 assertions): the four non-
  e-commerce generic fixtures above, Monitoring safety, and Research
  Bundle asset-plan/manifest generation with a deep-scraped image
  column's cross-row URL dedup.

  Full regression result: all 54 scratchpad test files (V1.1 through
  V1.18) pass with zero known regressions — one single-run flake in
  test-popup-v115-trial.js reproduced as transiently non-reproducible
  on immediate repeat (2 of 3 clean reruns), consistent with this
  project's already-documented flush()-timing-sensitivity precedent
  from V1.16, not a real regression.

---- Known limitations ----
  - Depth is fixed at exactly 1 by design — no recursive crawling, no
    following links found ON a detail page. This is intentional.
  - No full pause/resume across a browser restart — a Stopped run's
    already-collected results are preserved and merged, but resuming
    mid-run after closing the browser is V1.20's scope.
  - Deep Monitoring (Option B) is not built — Monitoring stays list-
    page-only for every saved scraper regardless of its deepScrape
    config, by deliberate choice (see #10).
  - Detail-page table rowSpan carries the same V1.17-documented
    limitation as list-page table extraction.

============================================================
V1.19 readiness
============================================================
V1.18 leaves list-page and detail-page extraction on the same underlying
engine, fully backward compatible, with zero known regressions across
the whole V1.1-V1.18 suite. Nothing about this version blocks V1.19
(Pagination / Infinite Scroll / Load More): Deep Scraping's queue/
concurrency/retry machinery (runDeepScrape, runWithConcurrency reuse,
classifyHttpFailure) is a reasonable pattern to draw on for pagination
work if useful, but V1.18 deliberately did not anticipate or half-build
any V1.19 functionality.

============================================================
V1.19 — Pagination / Infinite Scroll / Load More Engine
============================================================
Goal: collect raw rows across MANY result pages and dynamically-loaded
result sets, not just the first visible page — three collection
strategies (Pagination, Infinite Scroll, Load More), staying strictly
within V1.x's raw-data-collection scope. Still no statistics/charts/
market/AI recommendations anywhere — V2.0 remains a future, separate
phase.

AUDIT FIRST (explicit spec instruction): content/pagination.js, utils/
runstate.js, and content/domwait.js were fully re-read before writing
anything. Finding: V1.3's existing Auto Scroll and Multi-page engines
were ALREADY most of what V1.19 asks for — a resumable run-state machine
(seenKeys dedup, page-signature loop protection, first-seen row
ordering, Stop/Resume across navigation), a generic trigger-and-wait
primitive (WSDomWait.waitForNavigationOrMutation) that works for a click
OR a URL assignment equally well, and a Next-button picker already
reusing V1.17's selector engine. V1.19 therefore did NOT rewrite any of
this — "Auto Scroll" IS Infinite Scroll and "Multi-page" IS Next-button
Pagination, unchanged; every addition below is strictly ADDITIVE, and
every pre-existing test (test-pagination.js, test-runstate.js, test-
popup-v13.js) passes completely unmodified.

---- 1. Run Modes ----
  Current Page / Auto Scroll (now labeled "Auto Scroll / Infinite
  Scroll" in the UI — same stored mode value 'auto-scroll', zero
  behavior change) / Multi-Page (Pagination) / **Load More (new)**.
  Default remains Current Page; multi-page-family modes still require
  explicit selection AND explicit configuration (a button pick or a
  confirmed URL pattern) before Start does anything — an existing user
  never unexpectedly starts crawling multiple pages.

---- 2. Pagination Auto-Detect (content/autodetect.js) ----
  detectPaginationCandidate() — conservative, DOM-only, synchronous
  heuristics, returned as a SUGGESTION only:
    - Next-button signals, highest-confidence first: rel="next" >
      aria-label containing "next" > visible text matching Next/Next
      Page/»/›/>>/> .
    - Load More signals: visible text matching Load More/Show More/More
      Results/View More/See More.
    - URL-pattern signals (WSRunState.detectUrlPaginationPattern, works
      even with NO on-page control at all): a ?page=/?p=/?pg= query
      param (high confidence, step defaults to 1) or a /page/N path
      segment (high confidence); a ?start=/?offset= param is recognized
      but comes back with step:0/confidence:'medium' — spec #4's "do not
      assume every numeric param is pagination" means the page SIZE is
      never guessed, only offered for the user to confirm. An unrelated
      numeric param (?id=482) is never treated as pagination at all.
  Never starts navigation itself. The popup shows "Detected: ..." with
  its confidence, and only an explicit "Use Detected" click (which does
  nothing but fill in the exact same fields a manual pick would) or
  "Pick Manually" proceeds — Start remains a fully separate action
  either way. Ambiguous pages (nothing conclusive found) resolve to
  detected:null, leaving Current Page the safe default.

---- 3. Manual Next-Button / Load-More-Button Picker ----
  Unchanged V1.3/V1.17 picker infrastructure, reused verbatim — content.
  js's 'next-button' picker purpose already resolves a stable selector
  (WSSelector.buildSelectorForElement) regardless of what the button is
  FOR, so Load More's "Select Load More Button" reuses the identical
  pick flow, session-relay key (ws_next_button_pick::<hostname>), and
  disabled-state warning as Multi-page's "Select Next Button" — only the
  popup-side status text/label differs per mode. Zero content.js changes
  were needed for this reuse.

---- 4/5. URL Pattern & Numbered Pagination ----
  A new Multi-page "Pagination Method" choice: Next Button (unchanged
  default) or URL Pattern. URL Pattern needs no on-page selector at
  all — WSRunState.buildNextPageUrl(currentUrl, {kind,key}, nextValue)
  substitutes the tracked query param or path segment on the CURRENT
  page's own URL each pass (never guesses from a stale detected URL),
  and the run's tracked value (progress.urlPatternValue) advances by the
  configured step every successful page, staying in chrome.storage.
  session exactly like every other run field. "Numbered pagination" is
  not a third engine — both Next Button (clicking past the numbers) and
  URL Pattern (incrementing ?page=N sequentially) are inherently
  sequential, one page at a time, never firing pages simultaneously, and
  both are protected by the same page-signature loop detector.

---- 5/8. Load More (new mode, content/pagination.js's runLoadMoreLoop) ----
  Structurally the same click-wait-merge loop as Multi-page, with one
  deliberate semantic difference: a button that disappears or becomes
  disabled is a NATURAL COMPLETION (stopReason 'button-gone'/'disabled-
  next'), never an error — matching how real Load More buttons behave.
  The one exception: a button that was NEVER found even on the very
  first pass is still a real 'next-not-found' error (a genuine setup
  problem, distinguished from "reached the end" by clickCount===0 at
  failure time). Stop conditions: button disappears, button disabled, no
  new rows across noNewDataAttempts consecutive passes (mirrors Auto
  Scroll's own signal), a repeated page/content signature, max clicks,
  max rows, or the user pressing Stop — never an unbounded loop.
  Tolerates a real navigation too (spec's "usual case is same-document
  append, not assumed to be the ONLY case") via the exact same resume-
  on-navigation bootstrap Multi-page already has.

---- 6. Safety Limits / Advanced Options ----
  Multi-page: Max Pages, Max Rows (existing) + new Delay Between Pages
  (ms) and Retry Count. Load More: Max Clicks, Max Rows, Delay Between
  Clicks, Retry Count. A stalled page-change (timeout) gets up to
  `retryCount` extra attempts with the configured delay between them
  before being treated as a real failure — legacy saved scrapers (no
  delayMs/retryCount fields at all) default to 0/0, reproducing pre-
  V1.19 single-attempt-then-fail behavior byte-for-byte (explicitly
  regression-tested: TEST G2 in test-pagination-v119.js).

---- 7/11/12. Cross-Page Deduplication & Row Order ----
  Unchanged V1.3 mechanism (WSRunState.mergeNewRows/buildRowKey),
  extended to Load More automatically since it's the same shared
  function. Duplicate rows across pages/clicks are skipped by the
  configured dedupeKey (Entire Row, or — recommended, and already
  supported — a Link/URL column for a stable identity); legitimately
  DISTINCT rows that merely share similar text are never removed unless
  they share the actual configured key. Rows accumulate in strict
  first-seen order (array concatenation, never reordered or shuffled by
  async internals) for every mode.

---- 9. Pause / Resume ----
  Deliberately NOT a new, separate mechanism — V1.3's existing Stop +
  Resume (STOP_RUN / RESUME_RUN, chrome.storage.session-backed) already
  is a safe, dataset-preserving pause/resume for the current browser
  session, and now covers Load More too (RESUME_RUN's mode dispatch
  extended with a load-more branch). Resuming continues from the exact
  accumulated state (rows, seenKeys, pageSignatures, progress counters)
  with no restart from zero. Full pause/resume across a browser RESTART
  is explicitly left to V1.20 — nothing about this run-state shape (a
  single, session-scoped, hostname-keyed, fully serializable JSON
  object) makes that future work harder; it's the natural persistence
  boundary V1.20 will extend.

---- 10. Deep Scraping (V1.18) Compatibility ----
  No changes were needed to background.js's Deep Scrape engine or
  popup.js's merge chokepoint (computeTransformedResult) — a completed
  Multi-page/Load More run's rows reach `rawRows` through the exact same
  "View Results" path a Current Page Preview always used, so Deep
  Scraping's computeUniqueDetailUrls() already deduplicates detail URLs
  ACROSS every collected page automatically. Verified directly: a
  6-row, 3-"page" dataset with one detail URL shared across two
  different pages correctly sends exactly 5 (not 6) unique requests to
  Deep Scraping (test-pagination-v119-crossfeature.js PART 1).

---- 13. Monitoring ----
  DECISION: existing 'auto-scroll'/'multi-page' scheduled Monitoring
  support (V1.3/V1.8, pre-dating V1.19) is completely untouched — a
  saved scraper using either mode, with or without V1.19's new URL-
  pattern method, keeps working in Monitoring exactly as before (same
  maxPages/maxRows/delayMs safety limits apply). The NEW 'load-more'
  mode is explicitly NOT supported in scheduled Monitoring for V1.19 — a
  scraper with mode:'load-more' and Monitoring enabled gets a clean,
  immediate error ("Load More is not yet supported in scheduled
  Monitoring...") rather than a silent or half-tested headless attempt.
  This is the conservative option the spec explicitly permitted;
  building a tested headless Load More monitoring path is left for a
  future version.

---- 14/19/20. Error Handling, Origin Guard, Security ----
  Every listed failure mode (missing/disabled button, timeout, DOM never
  changing, a repeated page, an invalid/broken selector, navigation
  failure) resolves to one of the existing clear stopReasons, shown via
  plain-language labels in the popup — never a raw stack trace; full
  technical detail stays in console.log only. A NEW origin/domain guard
  (WSRunState.isSameOrigin) stops a run with stopReason 'origin-changed'
  if a Next-button click or URL-pattern navigation would leave the run's
  own hostname — checked by the outgoing script instance at the moment
  it detects a real navigation (the only point that both the real
  destination and the run's expected hostname are simultaneously known;
  see content/pagination.js's own inline comment for why). A second,
  independent, structural protection also exists: run state is stored
  under a hostname-scoped key (ws_run::<hostname>), so a script injected
  on an unrelated site can never even locate — let alone corrupt — a
  run that isn't its own (verified directly, test-pagination-v119.js
  TEST I). No eval()/new Function()/remote script execution anywhere in
  the new code; scripts/release-check.js's existing scan still passes
  clean.

---- 15/26. Saved Scraper Schema ----
  Fully additive, exactly V1.18's own established pattern:
    mode: 'current-page' | 'auto-scroll' | 'multi-page' | 'load-more' (V1.19)
    nextButtonConfig: {relativeSelector} | null — reused unchanged as
      Load More's own "button to click" config
    paginationMethod: 'nextButton' | 'urlPattern' (V1.19, multi-page only)
    urlPatternConfig: {kind, key, style, start, step} | null (V1.19)
    limits: {maxRows, maxScrolls, noNewDataAttempts} (auto-scroll) |
      {maxPages, maxRows, delayMs, retryCount} (multi-page) |
      {maxClicks, maxRows, noNewDataAttempts, delayMs, retryCount}
      (load-more, V1.19) — delayMs/retryCount/noNewDataAttempts are
      themselves V1.19-additive within these objects too.
  A pre-V1.19 saved scraper simply lacks paginationMethod/urlPatternConfig
  and can never have mode:'load-more' — it loads and runs byte-for-byte
  identically (verified with an EXACT legacy multi-page record, test-
  popup-v119-pagination.js TEST I).

---- 16. Performance ----
  No new unbounded observers/listeners — Load More reuses the exact same
  MutationObserver lifecycle (armed per-wait, disconnected on settle)
  Multi-page/Auto Scroll already use. No duplicate content-script
  registration (the same 'ws-pagination-<hostname>' id/unregister
  pattern is reused for Load More). No new full-array-copy-per-pass
  patterns were introduced — mergeNewRows' existing incremental
  concat+seenKeys approach is unchanged and already O(new rows), not
  O(total rows), per pass.

---- Tests ----
  test-runstate-v119.js (25 assertions): createRunState's new load-more/
  paginationMethod/urlPatternConfig defaults, evaluateLoadMoreStop,
  detectUrlPaginationPattern (query/path/offset/unrelated-param/
  unparseable), buildNextPageUrl (including the "pattern no longer
  matches" null case), isSameOrigin.
  test-pagination-v119.js (24 assertions): Load More's full lifecycle
  (normal completion, never-found error, button-gone after success,
  no-new-data plateau, max-clicks, Stop), Multi-page retryCount
  absorbing transient timeouts vs. the legacy no-retry default failing
  immediately, URL-pattern method advancing progress.urlPatternValue
  correctly across simulated pages, and the origin-guard's storage-key-
  decoupling protection.
  test-autodetect-pagination-v119.js (21 assertions): every detection
  signal (rel=next, aria-label, text variants for both Next and Load
  More, URL query/path patterns), priority ordering, hidden-control
  exclusion, and the safe-ambiguous-null fallback.
  test-popup-v119-pagination.js (48 assertions): the Run Mode UI
  (Load More radio, Pagination Method toggle), the full Auto-Detect
  flow (all 3 candidate shapes, "Use Detected" never auto-starting),
  Start-Run validation (missing button, invalid URL-pattern fields),
  Load More's permission/registration flow, saved-scraper persistence
  of every new field, legacy-scraper compatibility, and trial
  accounting (exactly 1 credit for a whole Load More operation,
  proven with 4 intermediate progress writes that charge nothing and a
  duplicate completed-write that never double-charges).
  test-pagination-v119-crossfeature.js (12 assertions): Deep Scraping
  deduplicating detail URLs across a merged multi-page dataset, CSV
  export and Research Bundle asset planning on that same merged
  dataset, and Monitoring isolation (load-more scraper cleanly
  rejected, an existing multi-page Monitoring job completely unaffected).

  Full regression result: all 58 scratchpad test files (V1.1 through
  V1.19) pass with zero known regressions — 1,766 total assertions.
  The 2 previously-documented transient flush()-timing flakes (test-
  popup-v115-trial.js, test-popup-v113-tabs.js) each reproduced once
  during a heavy back-to-back full-suite run and passed cleanly on
  every immediate repeat (4/4 and 2/2), consistent with this project's
  own established, non-regression precedent — no code change was made
  for these, since neither file's own init()-time work changed in
  V1.19.

---- Known limitations ----
  - jsdom (this project's entire test harness) does not implement real
    cross-document navigation, so the URL-pattern method's actual
    browser navigation is untestable end-to-end in this suite — its URL
    computation logic is fully unit-tested instead (test-runstate-
    v119.js), and the pagination LOOP's use of that logic is verified
    via a scripted WSDomWait mock standing in for a real successful
    navigation (documented in that test file's own header comment).
  - The Next-button confirmation panel (content.js) always reads
    "Confirm Next Button" even when picking a Load More button —
    cosmetic only; the saved selector data is identical and correct
    either way.
  - Offset-style URL params (?start=/?offset=) are detected but never
    auto-confirmed (step defaults to 0, requiring the user to enter the
    real page size) — the page size can never be reliably inferred from
    the URL alone (spec #4's explicit instruction).
  - Load More Monitoring is not supported (see #13) — deferred, not
    silently attempted.
  - Full pause/resume across a browser restart remains V1.20's scope
    (see #9).

============================================================
V1.20 readiness
============================================================
V1.19 leaves every collection mode (list-page, multi-page, load-more)
on the same session-scoped, hostname-keyed, fully serializable run-
state shape (utils/runstate.js), with zero known regressions across the
whole V1.1-V1.19 suite. This is the natural persistence boundary V1.20
(Reliability / Pause / Resume / Retry) needs: extending it to survive a
browser restart (rather than living only in chrome.storage.session,
which V1.19 deliberately keeps) is additive work, not a redesign.
V1.19's own retryCount/delayMs additions are a per-page-action retry
primitive V1.20 can draw on for a more advanced retry/resume story if
useful, but V1.19 deliberately did not anticipate or half-build any
V1.20 functionality.

============================================================
V1.20 — Reliability / Pause / Resume / Retry
============================================================
Goal: make long-running scraping jobs (Auto Scroll/Multi-page/Load More
and Deep Scraping) robust to interruption — a real, user-facing Pause
distinct from Stop, reliable resume after popup close/reopen and
navigation, configurable retries with exponential backoff, and a way to
retry only what failed without restarting an entire operation — while
never weakening anything V1.1-V1.19 already does.

AUDIT FIRST (explicit spec instruction): utils/runstate.js, content/
pagination.js, and background.js's Deep Scraping engine were fully re-
read before writing anything. Finding: the hard infrastructure V1.20
needs already existed — chrome.storage.session-backed, hostname/runId-
keyed run state that survives popup close/reopen by construction (V1.3),
a resumable state machine with STOP_RUN/RESUME_RUN already proving
"stop, preserve everything, continue later" works (V1.3), a per-page
retryCount/delayMs primitive (V1.19), and a proven "retry only the
FAILED items of a finished run" precedent already shipped for the ZIP
pipeline (retryFailedZipItems, V1.13.2). V1.20 therefore did not build a
new persistence layer or a second retry engine — every piece below is
either a new, clearly-scoped status/action layered on the EXISTING
run-state machine, or a direct reuse of the ZIP pipeline's own retry-
failed-items pattern applied to Deep Scraping.

---- 1. Pause vs. Stop — a genuinely distinct action ----
  Spec: "Pause must not behave like Stop." A NEW 'paused' status was
  added to WSRunState.STATUSES (alongside every pre-V1.20 status,
  unchanged) with its own message (PAUSE_RUN, mirroring STOP_RUN's exact
  mechanics — abort the live wait, write the new status, preserve every
  row/counter) and its own UI (a Pause button that appears whenever a
  run is active, sitting alongside — not replacing — Stop). Works for
  every mode (auto-scroll/multi-page/load-more) automatically, since all
  three already share one generic "still status:'running'?" loop-top
  check.
  A genuine, useful difference from Stop discovered during the audit:
  Stop has always triggered cleanupRunPermissions (unregistering the
  dynamic content script / removing the temporary host permission a
  Multi-page run requested), which is fine for a run the user considers
  finished, but means a STOPPED multi-page run's Resume only reliably
  continues on the CURRENT page — attempting to navigate further has no
  permission/injection left to rely on. Pause deliberately does NOT
  trigger that cleanup (it isn't in the terminal-status list cleanup
  gates on), so a PAUSED multi-page run's Resume can reliably keep
  navigating through further pages — a real, better-than-Stop resume
  story for exactly the case Pause exists for.

---- 2. Resume — continues, never restarts ----
  WSRunState.resumeRunState now accepts EITHER 'stopped' (unchanged
  pre-V1.20 behavior) or the new 'paused'; either way it clears the
  stop reason and flips back to 'running', leaving rows/seenKeys/
  pageSignatures/progress counters exactly as they were — proven
  directly (test-runstate-v120.js) and end-to-end through a real
  content-script loop (test-pagination-v120.js TEST B: a resumed Auto
  Scroll run's scrollCount and pre-pause rows are both preserved, never
  reset to 0/empty).

---- 3. Resume across popup close/reopen and navigation ----
  No new mechanism needed — this was already true by construction since
  V1.3 (run state lives in chrome.storage.session, keyed by hostname,
  independent of whether the popup is open) and V1.19 (the resume-on-
  navigation bootstrap already re-attaches pagination.js on every new
  page load and checks for an active run). V1.20's contribution is
  narrowly making sure Pause plugs into the SAME mechanism (see #1) —
  not rebuilding it. Persistence across the SERVICE WORKER itself being
  killed and restarted (not just suspended) remains the one honestly-
  documented exception, matching the exact same pre-existing caveat the
  V1.13.2 ZIP pipeline already carries for its own in-memory abort
  controllers — not something V1.20 claims to solve, and not a
  regression (see Known Limitations).

---- 4. Configurable retries + exponential backoff ----
  Pagination (content/pagination.js): retryCount/delayMs already
  existed (V1.19); V1.20 changes ONLY how the wait between retry
  ATTEMPTS grows — exponential (base 1s, doubling, capped at 30s)
  instead of a flat repeat of the configured delay, while the very
  FIRST attempt's pacing delay is completely unchanged. Because the
  exponential formula and V1.18/V1.19's old linear one are numerically
  IDENTICAL for the first two retries (1s, then 2s either way), and the
  hardcoded 3-attempt-max scenarios never reach a 3rd retry, every
  existing timing-sensitive test kept passing unmodified — the
  divergence only appears (correctly) once a higher retryCount is
  actually configured.
  Deep Scraping (background.js): the per-URL retry ceiling was a
  hardcoded constant (3) before V1.20; it is now genuinely configurable
  per scraper (deepScrapeConfig.retryLimit, 1-8, default 3 — the exact
  old hardcoded value, so an existing saved scraper's Deep Scrape
  behavior never changes unless a user deliberately adjusts it) and its
  own backoff is now exponential too (same 1s/2s/4s.../30s-cap shape).
  Both engines now write a short, user-facing status ("Retrying (2 of
  3)…", progress.retryStatus / a per-URL result.retryStatus) into their
  run state while a retry is in flight — spec's explicit "user-visible
  error/retry status instead of silent failure" — cleared back to null
  the moment the action settles either way, and purely informational
  (never read by any stop-condition or control-flow decision).

---- 5. Retry failed pages/steps without restarting the whole scrape ----
  Pagination: a Multi-page/Load More run is inherently sequential (each
  page depends on reaching the last one), so "retry a failed page"
  IS Resume — there's no separate concept of retrying page 5 in
  isolation without first being at page 5, and Resume already continues
  exactly from wherever progress.pageNumber/clickCount currently sits.
  No new mechanism was needed or built for this case.
  Deep Scraping: URLs are independent (order never matters, spec #23's
  own dedup-by-URL model), so a real "retry only what failed" operation
  makes sense and was built — a new RETRY_FAILED_DEEP_SCRAPE_ITEMS
  message (background.js), directly modeled on retryFailedZipItems: it
  resets ONLY the currently-'failed' URLs back to 'pending', re-runs
  JUST those through the same fetch-validate-extract pipeline (a new
  shared runDeepScrapeUrls() helper factored out of runDeepScrape so
  both paths share one finalize/status implementation, per spec's own
  "reuse infrastructure" instruction), and leaves every already-
  completed/partial/skipped result completely untouched. Unlike the ZIP
  pipeline, this needed NO in-memory-only run context at all — Deep
  Scrape's entire run state (fields, concurrency, retry limit) is
  already fully JSON-serializable and persisted, so a retry can even be
  triggered after the service worker itself restarted, as long as
  chrome.storage.session still has the run. A new "Retry Failed" button
  appears in the popup once a run is terminal AND something actually
  failed, hides again once there's nothing left to retry.

---- 6. Duplicate prevention across pause/resume/retry ----
  List-page rows: unchanged V1.3 seenKeys-based dedup (WSRunState.
  mergeNewRows) — a resumed pass re-scrapes the CURRENT page/state and
  merges through the exact same dedup gate every other pass already
  uses, so nothing about pausing/resuming can produce a duplicate row
  that a normal uninterrupted run wouldn't already have prevented.
  Deep Scrape results: keyed by URL in a plain object map, never an
  appended array — retrying a URL simply overwrites that SAME key with
  a fresh outcome; mergeDeepScrapeResults() (popup.js) already re-derives
  every merged column fresh from rawRows + the current results map on
  every render, so a retry's improved results flow into the visible
  table automatically with no accumulation risk. Both are proven
  directly (test-background-deepscrape-v120.js TEST E: the total unique-
  result-URL count never changes across a retry; an already-completed
  URL is never re-fetched at all).

---- 7. Duplicate trial-credit prevention ----
  Unchanged V1.15 mechanism, extended for free: WSLicense.
  consumeRunCredit(runId) is idempotent by runId, and charging only
  ever fires on a genuine terminal 'completed' write. A pause/resume
  cycle never changes the run's runId, so a paused-then-resumed-then-
  completed list-page run still charges exactly once (test-v120-
  crossfeature.js PART 1) — pausing was never a new "run" for
  accounting purposes. A Deep Scrape "Retry Failed" op reuses the SAME
  runId as the original run for exactly the same reason, so it can
  never trigger a second charge either (test-popup-v120-pause-
  resume.js TEST E, test-background-deepscrape-v120.js TEST E's
  fetchCallCounts proof that nothing already-successful is even
  touched again).

---- 8. Reliable progress counters across resume ----
  Every progress counter (rowsCollected, pageNumber/clickCount, the
  Deep Scrape counts.* tallies) lives in the SAME persisted run-state
  object Resume continues from — there is no separate "session progress"
  that could drift from the real one. Verified directly: a resumed run's
  scrollCount/pageNumber/clickCount continue from their pre-pause value,
  never reset to 0 (test-runstate-v120.js, test-pagination-v120.js).

---- 9. Clear run states ----
  running / paused / completed / stopped / error — the internal status
  value stays 'error' (unchanged; not renamed to "failed") to avoid
  touching every existing status === 'error' check across background.js/
  popup.js/the whole test suite for a purely cosmetic rename; conceptually
  it IS V1.20's "failed" state, and is documented as such here rather
  than risking a wide, purely-nominal regression surface for no
  functional gain.

---- Compatibility with V1.17/V1.18/V1.19 ----
  V1.17 (Advanced Selection/Auto Detect): completely untouched — no
  file in that layer was modified.
  V1.18 (Deep Scraping): the validate-then-extract pipeline, URL
  scheme rejection, tab lifecycle, and trial-charge gate are all
  unchanged; V1.20 only added a configurable ceiling in front of the
  existing retry loop, exponential spacing between attempts, and the
  new retry-failed-only entry point sitting alongside (never replacing)
  the original START_DEEP_SCRAPE/STOP_DEEP_SCRAPE handlers. Every V1.18
  regression test (test-background-deepscrape-v118.js, test-popup-
  v118-deepscrape.js) still passes unmodified.
  V1.19 (Multi-page/Load More/URL-pattern/Auto-Detect): the page-change
  resolution logic (resolveNextAction), origin guard, and loop-signature
  detection are all unchanged; V1.20 only touched the WAIT/backoff step
  between attempts and added the parallel Pause path alongside Stop.
  Every V1.19 regression test still passes unmodified (one file's own
  timing margin was widened — see Bugs Found & Fixed below, a test-only
  change, not a production behavior change).

---- Tests ----
  test-runstate-v120.js (24 assertions): STATUSES includes 'paused'
  with nothing removed, pauseRunState, resumeRunState accepting both
  'stopped' and 'paused' (and remaining a safe no-op for 'running'/
  'completed'), and resume preserving every accumulated counter exactly.
  test-pagination-v120.js (17 assertions): PAUSE_RUN end-to-end for
  Multi-page and Load More (rows/progress preserved, the loop genuinely
  stops making passes), Resume from 'paused' continuing an Auto Scroll
  run without restarting, and REAL measured exponential backoff timing
  (the 2nd retry's wait is proven >1.5x the 1st's) plus retryStatus
  appearing/clearing live in storage.
  test-background-deepscrape-v120.js (21 assertions): a configured
  retryLimit both higher and lower than the old fixed 3 actually takes
  effect, omitting retryLimit still defaults to exactly 3 (backward
  compatibility), measured exponential backoff across 3 real retries,
  and the full RETRY_FAILED_DEEP_SCRAPE_ITEMS lifecycle — only failed
  URLs re-fetched, already-completed ones never touched, no duplicate
  result entries, a stale/wrong runId or nothing-to-retry is a safe
  no-op, and Stop works cleanly mid-retry.
  test-popup-v120-pause-resume.js (25 assertions): Pause visible
  alongside Stop while running, the PAUSED badge/status text/button
  states, Resume sending RESUME_RUN from paused, live retry-status
  text appearing and clearing, the Deep Scrape Retry Limit selector's
  full save/reload persistence, a legacy (V1.18/V1.19-era) deepScrape
  record missing retryLimit specifically self-healing to 3, the Retry
  Failed button's visibility rules, and trial-credit accounting across
  a Deep Scrape retry.
  test-v120-crossfeature.js (11 assertions): a full pause -> resume ->
  complete Multi-page lifecycle charging exactly 1 credit with the
  final 4-row dataset (2 collected before the pause, 2 after) correctly
  ordered and duplicate-free, that same dataset exporting to CSV and
  feeding a Research Bundle manifest correctly, and Pause/Resume working
  end-to-end for a fully pre-V1.20 legacy saved scraper record.

  Full regression result: all 63 scratchpad test files (V1.1 through
  V1.20) pass with zero known regressions — 1,864 total assertions. The
  1 previously-documented transient flush()-timing flake (test-popup-
  v115-trial.js, a file V1.20 never touched) reproduced once during a
  heavy back-to-back full-suite run and passed cleanly on both
  immediate repeats, consistent with this project's own established,
  non-regression precedent.

---- Bugs found and fixed (all self-caught during development) ----
  - A test-only timing regression: test-pagination-v119.js's TEST G
    (multi-page retry) assumed near-instant retries (V1.19's flat/tiny
    delay), which V1.20's real exponential backoff correctly no longer
    provides for a configured retryCount — its flush() budget was
    widened to match the new, real ~3s backoff cost. No production code
    was at fault; this was purely the test's own stale timing
    assumption.
  - A genuine test-harness race in the new deep-scrape retry test:
    RETRY_FAILED_DEEP_SCRAPE_ITEMS's sendResponse fires synchronously
    (fire-and-forget), before the retry's own first await lands — an
    immediate waitForTerminal() call right after sending the message
    could observe the STALE prior 'completed' status from before the
    retry even started and return prematurely. Fixed by waiting for
    status to genuinely flip to 'running' first, the same category of
    fix already applied to a V1.18-era coverage test's own async race.
  - A real gap discovered during the audit, closed as part of #1 above:
    popup.js's applyLoadedScraper() restored deepScrapeConfig via
    `scraper.deepScrape || emptyDeepScrape()`, which only self-heals a
    COMPLETELY missing deepScrape object — a V1.18/V1.19-era record that
    HAS deepScrape but lacks V1.20's new retryLimit field specifically
    would have loaded with retryLimit:undefined (silently falling back
    to background.js's own default there, but leaving the popup's Retry
    Limit UI showing nothing sensible). Fixed by switching to WSRecipes.
    normalizeDeepScrape(scraper.deepScrape) — the same self-healing
    entry point saveScraper/updateScraper already use — and exporting
    that function from utils/recipes.js (it existed internally but was
    never on the public WSRecipes object before).

---- Known limitations ----
  - Persistence does not survive the extension's SERVICE WORKER itself
    being killed and restarted mid-run (as opposed to merely
    suspended/the popup closing) — an honestly-documented, pre-existing
    limitation this project's own V1.13.2 ZIP pipeline already carries
    for its in-memory AbortControllers; V1.20 does not claim to solve
    this, and Deep Scraping's Retry Failed path is in fact MORE
    resilient to it than before (see #5) since it needs no in-memory
    context to resume, only the persisted state.
  - Pause is not offered for Deep Scraping specifically — its detail-URL
    queue is inherently order-independent (spec #23), so Stop-then-
    Retry-Failed already covers the same practical need ("temporarily
    halt, pick back up later without losing completed work") without a
    second, redundant status/button pair; documented here as a
    deliberate scope decision, not an oversight.
  - The internal 'error' status is not literally renamed to 'failed'
    (see #9) — a documented naming choice, not a missing state.
  - Numbered-pagination/URL-pattern retry backoff timing is untestable
    end-to-end in jsdom for the same pre-existing reason documented in
    V1.19 (no real cross-document navigation support); the retry/
    backoff LOGIC itself is fully covered via the Next-button/Load-More
    paths, which exercise the identical triggerAndWaitWithRetry code.

============================================================
V1.21 readiness
============================================================
V1.20 leaves every run-state shape (list-page AND Deep Scrape) carrying
real pause/resume/retry-status fields, all still living in chrome.
storage.session with zero known regressions across the whole V1.1-V1.20
suite. Nothing about this version blocks V1.21 (Structured Data / JSON-
LD / Metadata) — that work is orthogonal (extracting a different KIND of
data from a page, not changing how runs are collected/persisted) and
V1.20 deliberately did not anticipate or half-build any V1.21
functionality.

============================================================
V1.21 — Structured Data / JSON-LD / Metadata
============================================================
Goal: make the scraper significantly stronger at pulling data that
modern pages already embed in structured form (JSON-LD, Open Graph,
Twitter Card, common meta tags), as a genuine SUPPLEMENT to — never a
replacement for — the existing DOM/selector engine. Still strictly
V1.x raw data collection: no statistics, no charts, no interpretation
of what a value "means" — every structured value is exposed exactly as
the page itself provided it.

AUDIT FIRST (explicit spec instruction): content/scraper.js, content/
selector.js, and the popup's Columns/Auto Detect/Deep Scraping panels
were fully re-read before writing anything. Finding: the extraction
architecture already cleanly separates "how a column's value is
resolved" (WSSelector.extractValue, keyed by column.attribute) from
"what happens with that value afterward" (dedup, transforms, exports,
Research Bundle, Snapshots — all fully generic over whatever columns/
rows they're handed). This meant V1.21 could be added as a genuinely
NEW column.attribute value ('structured') living ALONGSIDE the existing
text/html/href/src/alt/attr ones, resolved by a completely separate,
new module — zero changes to WSSelector's own DOM-selector logic, and
zero changes anywhere in the transform/export/Research/Snapshot layer
(all already attribute-agnostic).

---- 1. JSON-LD detection and parsing (content/structureddata.js, new) ----
  Finds every <script type="application/ld+json"> block, parses each
  INDEPENDENTLY (spec: "never crash the scrape because one structured-
  data block is invalid") — a malformed block is recorded in a
  visible `errors` list and skipped; every OTHER block on the page
  still parses normally. Handles every real-world shape: a single
  object, a JSON array of objects, and an `@graph` wrapper (recursively
  flattened, depth-capped at 6 as a defensive limit against pathological
  nesting) — all normalized into one flat `entities` array. An empty
  block or a bare primitive (a lone string/number someone put in a
  ld+json tag) is treated as "nothing usable there", not an error.

---- 2/3. Schema.org entity + product-field detection ----
  A small, config-driven table of simple leaf fields per spec's named
  type (Product, Article/NewsArticle, Organization, Person,
  LocalBusiness, Event, WebPage, Review, AggregateRating), plus
  dedicated handling for shapes that don't fit a flat table:
    - offers: a plain Offer (price/priceCurrency/availability/
      itemCondition) vs. an AggregateOffer (lowPrice/highPrice/
      priceCurrency/offerCount — NEVER offered as a single "Price",
      since one doesn't exist); an offers ARRAY uses the first offer,
      documented behavior, never fabricated.
    - aggregateRating (ratingValue/reviewCount/ratingCount/bestRating),
      brand/author/publisher (nested Organization/Person objects
      unwrapped to their .name), image (ImageObject.url/contentUrl or a
      plain string/array unwrapped to one URL, resolved absolute).
    - BreadcrumbList -> a single derived "Breadcrumb Trail" field, items
      joined in POSITION order (spec-honest: a genuine representation of
      the raw itemListElement data, not fabricated).
    - FAQPage -> a single derived "FAQ (Q&A)" field joining real
      question/answer text pairs.
  Every candidate carries a raw, unmodified sampleValue (spec: "preserve
  raw structured values where appropriate") — availability stays exactly
  "https://schema.org/InStock", never reworded to "In Stock".

---- 4. Metadata extraction ----
  document.title, meta[name=description], link[rel=canonical] (resolved
  absolute), Open Graph (og:title/description/image/url/site_name/type),
  Twitter Card (twitter:card/title/description/image), plus author/
  keywords — all offered as their own field candidates under a "Page
  Metadata" group, image/URL fields resolved to absolute and tagged
  kind:'image'/'url' for the download-button integration (#6 below).

---- 5. Structured Data inspector (popup) ----
  A new "🏷️ Structured Data" button next to Auto Detect (Columns
  section). Scanning (SCAN_STRUCTURED_DATA, a new message type handled
  by structureddata.js's own listener — mirrors content/autodetect.js's
  exact separate-listener pattern, so content.js/pagination.js/
  autodetect.js stay completely untouched) never starts anything on its
  own: it shows every detected field, grouped by source entity ("Product
  #1", "Page Metadata", ...), each with a real sample value and a
  checkbox — "Add Selected Fields" is a fully separate, explicit action,
  same inspect-first-commit-second pattern Auto Detect and Deep
  Scraping's field picker already established. Zero fields found shows a
  clear empty state; a malformed JSON-LD block shows a plain, non-
  alarming note (never hidden, never a crash) while still listing
  whatever DID parse successfully elsewhere on the page.

---- 6. Adding structured fields to the scraper ----
  Each checked field becomes a normal column: {id, name, relativeSelector:
  null, attribute:'structured', structuredPath, structuredKind}. Column
  NAME collisions against existing columns are resolved deterministically
  ("Price" -> "Price (structured)" -> "Price (structured 2)"...), the
  exact same dedup pattern V1.18's Deep Scrape merge already uses.
  structuredKind ('image'|'url'|'text') lets every existing "is this an
  image/link column?" check (Download Images/Files buttons, the Bulk
  Download column picker, the Research Bundle image-column default, Deep
  Scraping's source-column candidates) recognize a structured image/URL
  field too — implemented as two small shared predicates (isImageLike
  Column/isLinkLikeColumn) replacing 6 separate attribute==='src'/'href'
  checks at once, rather than touching each site's own logic ad hoc.

---- 7. DOM + structured-data coexistence ----
  THE central architectural guarantee, verified directly (not just
  assumed): "Add Selected Fields" APPENDS onto state.columns — unlike
  Auto Detect (which deliberately REPLACES the whole working
  configuration), a structured field is added ALONGSIDE whatever
  selector-based columns already exist, never resetting them. content/
  scraper.js's runExtraction()/runDetailExtraction() branch on
  column.attribute==='structured' BEFORE reaching WSSelector at all — a
  DOM column and a structured column in the SAME scraper both resolve
  correctly in the SAME extraction pass, proven with fixtures mixing
  both kinds (test-scraper-structureddata-v121.js TEST A/C).
  One honest, documented distinction: a structured column's snapshot is
  computed ONCE per extraction call (page-level, not per row) — in
  list-page mode, the SAME resolved value applies to every extracted row
  (a page's JSON-LD describes the page/its one primary entity, not each
  individual repeating card), while in Deep Scraping's per-detail-page
  runDetailExtraction, this is naturally the distinct-per-page case that
  fits best — the SAME saved field resolves a genuinely different,
  correct value on every different detail page (proven directly,
  TEST B: two fixture pages with different Product JSON-LD both resolve
  their own correct values for the identical saved field/path).

---- 8-16. Compatibility ----
  Deep Scraping (#8): a structured field works as a detail field exactly
  like a DOM one, coexists with DOM detail fields in the same run, and
  charges the SAME single trial credit for the whole operation (verified
  end-to-end, test-v121-crossfeature.js PART 3).
  Multi-Page (#9): unaffected — a structured column resolves its
  (page-level, per spec #7's own documented behavior) value on every
  page's extraction pass exactly like it does for a single Current Page
  run; nothing about V1.19's pagination loop needed to change.
  Pause/Resume/Retry (#10): unaffected — structured columns are resolved
  entirely within one extraction pass (runExtraction/runDetailExtraction),
  which V1.20's pause/resume/retry machinery already treats as an
  opaque, atomic step; nothing new to persist or resume.
  Snapshots (#11)/Monitoring (#12)/CSV/JSON/XLSX export (#13)/Research
  Bundle (#14): all already fully generic over whatever columns/rows
  they're handed (attribute-agnostic) — a structured column's real value
  flows through every one of these completely unmodified, verified
  directly rather than merely assumed (test-v121-crossfeature.js PART 2,
  PART 4).
  Saved scraper backward compatibility (#15): structuredPath/
  structuredKind are additive column fields; a scraper saved before
  V1.21 simply has no column with attribute:'structured' and loads/runs
  byte-for-byte identically (verified with an exact pre-V1.21 legacy
  record, test-v121-crossfeature.js PART 1).
  Trial accounting (#16): completely untouched — no new run type or
  message was introduced; structured columns are resolved inside the
  EXISTING extraction call of whichever mode is already running, so the
  existing "exactly one credit per completed whole operation" contract
  needed zero changes and was reverified directly.

---- Tests ----
  test-structureddata-v121.js (52 assertions): JSON-LD parsing (single/
  multiple blocks, arrays, @graph, malformed-block isolation, empty/
  primitive blocks, no-structured-data-at-all), every named schema.org
  type's field detection including AggregateOffer vs. plain Offer, an
  offers ARRAY using the first entry, BreadcrumbList/FAQPage derived
  fields, Article author/publisher unwrapping, full metadata extraction
  (title/description/canonical/OG/Twitter, relative image URLs resolved
  absolute), and getValueAtPath re-resolving the SAME saved path
  correctly against a DIFFERENT page's live snapshot (the exact scenario
  Deep Scraping needs).
  test-scraper-structureddata-v121.js (15 assertions): content/
  scraper.js's real runExtraction()/runDetailExtraction() integration —
  DOM+structured coexistence in one call, page-level duplication in
  list mode vs. genuine per-page distinctness in detail mode, a
  structured-only scraper (whole page as one row), malformed JSON-LD
  never crashing extraction, and a structured field harmlessly ignoring
  an irrelevant multiple:'all' flag.
  test-popup-v121-structureddata.js (17 assertions): the inspector UI —
  scan/grouped-field-list rendering, the empty and malformed-JSON-LD
  states, Add Selected Fields APPENDING (never replacing) existing
  columns, deterministic name-collision suffixing, and structured
  image/URL columns correctly enabling the Download buttons.
  test-v121-crossfeature.js (12 assertions): mixed-column save/reload
  round trip, a fully legacy pre-V1.21 scraper loading unaffected,
  CSV export + Research Bundle manifest both correctly carrying a
  structured column's real values, Deep Scraping with a structured
  detail field charging exactly 1 credit, and Snapshots loading cleanly
  alongside structured column metadata.

  Full regression result: all 67 scratchpad test files (V1.1 through
  V1.21) pass with zero known regressions — 1,960 total assertions. One
  timing-sensitive V1.20 test (test-pagination-v120.js, exercising real
  exponential-backoff timers — untouched by V1.21) reproduced a single
  transient failure during a heavy back-to-back full-suite run and
  passed cleanly on 3/3 immediate repeats, consistent with this
  project's own established, non-regression precedent.

---- Bugs found and fixed ----
  None — V1.21 shipped with zero self-caught defects requiring a fix.
  Two test-assumption corrections were made during development (not
  production bugs): a relative OG-image URL test initially expected
  root-relative resolution when standard URL-resolution rules (already
  used correctly elsewhere in this project via WSSelector.resolveUrl)
  correctly resolve it relative to the current page's own path instead;
  and a Download-button-visibility test initially checked visibility
  before a Preview/Run, when that visibility has always been computed by
  renderResults() (pre-existing, unrelated-to-V1.21 behavior) — both
  were test-only corrections, confirmed by reading the actual
  (correct) existing code.

---- Known limitations ----
  - Structured columns don't support "match multiple — extract all" the
    way DOM columns do; a structured path always resolves to one value
    (arrays are already sensibly reduced to their first/most relevant
    element by design) — documented, not a missing feature for how
    JSON-LD data is actually shaped in practice.
  - List-page (non-Deep-Scraping) extraction applies a structured
    column's PAGE-level value identically to every row — correct and
    honest given what JSON-LD actually describes on a listing page, but
    means a structured column is most valuable on true "one entity per
    page" pages (Current Page mode, or any Deep Scraping detail page)
    rather than as a per-row differentiator on a multi-item listing.
  - Only the schema.org types spec #2 explicitly names are given
    dedicated field extractors; an entity of an unlisted @type still
    contributes to the raw snapshot/errors accounting but won't surface
    dedicated field candidates in the inspector (no field candidates are
    ever fabricated for a type this version doesn't know about).
  - No JSON-LD microdata/RDFa fallback (only <script type="application/
    ld+json">, Open Graph, and Twitter Card, per spec's own named list)
    — a page using ONLY inline microdata (itemprop attributes) yields no
    structured candidates; its DOM values remain fully scrapeable via
    the existing selector engine as always.

============================================================
V1.22 readiness
============================================================
V1.21 leaves content/scraper.js's extraction path branching cleanly on
column.attribute, with the new 'structured' branch fully isolated in
its own module and zero changes to the DOM/selector engine, exports,
Research Bundle, Snapshots, Monitoring, or the Pause/Resume/Retry
machinery — all reverified passing unmodified across the whole
V1.1-V1.21 suite. Nothing about this version blocks V1.22 (Templates /
Recipes) — that work is orthogonal (packaging/sharing saved scraper
CONFIGURATION, not changing what a scraper can extract) and V1.21
deliberately did not anticipate or half-build any V1.22 functionality.

============================================================
V1.22 — Templates / Recipes
============================================================
Goal: turn commonly-repeated scraper configurations into reusable
"Templates" so a user can build a strong scraper faster, without ever
being locked into what a template suggests — every generated field/
setting stays fully editable through the normal scraper UI afterward,
and applying a template never creates a permanently special scraper
type (spec's own central requirement, verified directly rather than
merely assumed — test-v122-crossfeature.js PART 1).

NAMING NOTE: called "Templates" everywhere in the UI and in this new
module's own code (utils/templates.js, WSTemplates) — deliberately NOT
"Recipes" in code, to avoid confusion with the PRE-EXISTING utils/
recipes.js (WSRecipes), which is this project's Saved Scraper
persistence module and remains completely untouched by this version. A
Template and a Saved Scraper are different concepts that happen to
share some field shapes (spec #24's own UX guidance: "Prefer language
such as: Templates... Avoid exposing unnecessary internal
implementation terminology" — reinforced this choice).

AUDIT FIRST (explicit spec instruction, #28): utils/recipes.js,
content/autodetect.js (V1.17), content/structureddata.js (V1.21), and
the popup's Auto Detect/Structured Data panels were fully re-read
before writing anything. Finding: V1.22 needed NO new detection engine
at all — V1.17's Auto Detect already assigns field candidates a small,
CONTROLLED vocabulary of semantic names (Title/Price/Author/Date/
Rating/Count/Link/Image/Description/Text), and V1.21's Structured Data
already produces labeled field candidates (Name/Price/SKU/Image/...).
A Template is therefore just a list of FIELD-NAME HINTS to match
against whichever of those two ALREADY-RUN detection results actually
found something — reusing both engines completely as-is (spec #7's
explicit "do not create a second competing selector engine").

---- 1. Template architecture (utils/templates.js, new — popup-only,
never injected into a page) ----
  One schema, two fundamentally different KINDS of template:
    - BUILT-IN (8, code-defined constants, never stored, never
      deletable/editable — spec #9): carry `fieldHints` — semantic
      INTENTIONS ("find a product name", "find a price"), each with a
      priority-ordered list of real V1.21 structured-data labels to try
      first, then real V1.17 Auto Detect field names as a fallback.
      They never carry real selectors of their own — a built-in
      template is page-agnostic by design.
    - CUSTOM (chrome.storage.local, key `ws_templates`, isolated from
      every other storage key exactly like utils/recipes.js's own
      `ws_saved_scrapers` key): carry real, already-resolved `columns`
      (plus optional pagination/Deep Scrape config) captured from an
      existing working scraper via "Save as Template" — applying one is
      a direct, literal copy, since it's inherently page-specific
      already (no semantic matching needed or attempted).
  Every column a template produces (either kind) uses the EXACT same
  shape content/scraper.js already understands (attribute enum
  including V1.21's 'structured' and this version's new 'position' —
  see #2 below) — indistinguishable from a manually-added column
  anywhere downstream.

---- 2. Built-in generic templates ----
  Exactly the 8 spec names, each with its full requested field list:
  E-commerce Product List, Search Results, Article / News List,
  Directory / Business List, Real Estate List, Job Listings, Generic
  Table, Generic List / Cards. No individual commercial website is
  hard-coded anywhere (spec's explicit instruction) — every hint is a
  generic schema.org label or Auto Detect semantic name.
  Two small, genuinely NEW additions were needed to fully honor the
  spec's own field lists, both reused/extended from existing engines
  rather than built as anything separate:
    - "Position" (Search Results) — a brand-new column.attribute
      value, 'position', resolved by content/scraper.js as a plain
      1-based row index (never fabricated from the DOM/structured
      data — it's a pure computed sequence number). Deliberately
      excluded from runExtraction's existing "did any column produce a
      value" liveness filter, so it can never make an otherwise-empty,
      non-matching container look like real data.
    - JobPosting structured-data support and Organization/LocalBusiness
      Address support were ADDED to content/structureddata.js (V1.21's
      own module) — the exact same config-driven SIMPLE_TYPE_FIELDS/
      nested-field pattern V1.21 already established, extended rather
      than duplicated (spec #28), so the Job Listings and Directory
      templates can genuinely use structured data when it exists,
      exactly as spec #6 asks.
  Real Estate is honestly the weakest template — schema.org has no
  canonical, widely-adopted type for property listings, so its
  structured-data hints are mostly empty by design (documented as a
  known limitation, not a missing feature).

---- 3. Smart suggestions ----
  suggestTemplates(autoDetectResult, structuredScanResult) is a pure
  post-processing step over data the popup ALREADY fetched (never
  triggers its own scan) — Product JSON-LD -> E-commerce (high),
  Article/NewsArticle -> Article/News (high), JobPosting -> Job
  Listings (high), LocalBusiness/Organization -> Directory (medium), an
  HTML table structure -> Generic Table (high), bedroom/bathroom/area-
  like Auto Detect field labels -> Real Estate (medium, an honest
  heuristic given schema.org's own gap here), and a generic repeating
  structure with no confirmed type -> Generic List (low, a fallback
  only). Recommendations only — never alters the scraper on its own
  (verified directly: previewing/applying a suggestion is still a
  separate, explicit user action every time).

---- 4/5. Preview and Apply ----
  Preview is always a separate step from Apply (spec #4's explicit "the
  user must understand what will be added before applying it") — shows
  the template name/description, every MATCHED field with its real
  source (Structured/Auto Detect/Computed) and a real sample value, and
  every UNMATCHED hint by name so nothing is silently missing. Apply
  behavior differs deliberately by kind, both documented directly in
  the Preview UI itself before the user commits:
    - Built-in templates: matched fields are ADDED onto whatever
      columns already exist (never replace them) — the same
      coexistence/append philosophy V1.21's Structured Data inspector
      already established, right down to reusing its exact "(template)"
      /"(template 2)" name-collision-suffix pattern (spec #12).
    - Custom templates: applying REPLACES the working configuration
      (columns, container, Run Mode/pagination settings, Deep Scrape
      config) — the same, already-familiar semantics as "Load"-ing a
      Saved Scraper, which a custom template is conceptually closest
      to. Clearly labeled in the Preview note before Apply.
  Either way, every resulting field is immediately, ordinarily editable
  (rename/delete/change selector/change structured mapping/add more) —
  proven directly by renaming a template-generated column in a test.

---- 6. Structured data integration ----
  Built-in templates try structured-data hints FIRST (spec #6's own
  Product example — name/offers.price/brand/sku/aggregateRating/image/
  availability are all real V1.21 label matches), falling back to Auto
  Detect only when nothing structured was found, and simply omitting a
  field entirely when NEITHER source has evidence (spec: "Never assume
  JSON-LD exists. Fall back safely." / never fabricate).

---- 7. Auto Detect integration ----
  A built-in template's Auto-Detect-sourced fallback matches purely by
  V1.17's own controlled field-name vocabulary (Title/Price/Link/Image/
  Rating/Count/Author/Date/Description) against whichever field Auto
  Detect's TOP-scored structure already produced — zero new detection
  logic, zero changes to autodetect.js's own scoring/naming (only
  structureddata.js's field TABLE was extended, per #2 above, which is
  additive data, not new detection code). A DOM-sourced match is
  deliberately skipped (never silently blended into an unrelated
  existing container) when the scraper already has its own columns/
  container set — the user is told exactly why and how many fields were
  skipped, with Reset Columns offered as the explicit path to use them.

---- 8/9. Save as Custom Template / management ----
  "Save as Template" captures columns, containerSelector, pagination
  configuration (mode/method/URL-pattern/Next-button/dedupe/limits —
  the EXACT shape currentRunModeFields() already produces for Saved
  Scrapers, reused verbatim), and Deep Scrape configuration (when
  fields exist) — and NOTHING ELSE. There is no `rows`/results
  parameter anywhere in the save path at all (spec #8's "Do NOT save
  scrape results" — structurally impossible to violate, not just
  policy). Full CRUD for custom templates (create/view/preview/apply/
  rename/duplicate/delete) with duplicate-name protection and a
  deterministic "(copy)"/"(copy 2)" naming pattern for Duplicate,
  mirroring Saved Scrapers' own established conventions. Built-in
  templates expose ONLY a Preview action — no Rename/Duplicate/Delete/
  Export button ever renders for them (spec #9's protection verified
  directly, not just by omission).

---- 10. Import / export ----
  A single portable JSON document per template (schemaVersion, name,
  description, category, icon, columns, containerSelector,
  paginationConfig, deepScrapeConfig — never the internal builtin/
  applyStrategy fields, which are meaningless outside this codebase).
  Import runs EVERY field through the exact same normalizeTemplate()
  rebuild-from-scratch validator real "Save as Template" input already
  goes through — nothing is ever trusted as-is. Malformed JSON,
  non-object/array-at-top-level, a missing name, zero valid columns,
  and an unsupported column attribute are all rejected or silently
  dropped (a template with SOME valid columns and one invalid one still
  imports, minus just the bad column — never the whole thing thrown
  away for one bad field). Export/import go through prompt() (a
  Ctrl+C-able text dialog) rather than a file-save link — the same
  sandboxed-viewer-safe pattern this project's other export paths
  already use where a download can't be triggered.

---- 11. Versioning ----
  schemaVersion 1 is the only version that has ever existed. A record
  with no schemaVersion field at all (impossible today, but the
  intended pre-versioning case) is treated as version 1. A record
  CLAIMING a version number higher than this build supports fails
  import with a clear, specific compatibility message naming both the
  claimed and the supported version — never silently misinterpreted as
  v1 data.

---- 12. Duplicate / collision handling ----
  DECISION: skip-then-rename, matching V1.21's Structured Data
  inspector precedent exactly — a template field whose name collides
  with an existing column is never dropped and never overwrites the
  existing one; it gets a deterministic "(template)" / "(template 2)"
  suffix instead. Documented here as the chosen, consistent behavior
  (spec #12 explicitly asked this decision be made and documented).

---- 13-21. Compatibility ----
  Saved scraper compatibility (#13): a template-applied configuration
  saves through the SAME WSRecipes.saveScraper Saved Scraper path as
  any hand-built one — no `fromTemplate`/`templateId` marker or any
  other special field is ever added to a saved scraper record (verified
  directly). A fully pre-V1.22 legacy saved scraper (zero template-
  related fields anywhere) loads completely unaffected, since Templates
  never touched utils/recipes.js's own schema at all.
  Deep Scraping (#14): a custom template's captured deepScrapeConfig
  runs through utils/recipes.js's OWN normalizeDeepScrape — no second
  Deep Scrape engine, no template-specific extraction path; a template-
  originated Deep Scrape run charges its usual exactly-1 trial credit
  (verified end-to-end).
  Multi-Page (#15) / Pause-Resume-Retry (#16) / Structured Data (#17):
  a template-generated column is byte-for-byte the same shape a
  manually-built one already is, so every one of these engines (which
  are all already generic over whatever columns/rows they're handed)
  needed zero changes and behaves identically — nothing in this version
  touched content/pagination.js, utils/runstate.js, or content/
  scraper.js's DOM/structured extraction paths beyond the two additive
  points named in #2.
  Monitoring (#18) / Snapshots (#19): unaffected — a template-derived
  saved scraper is monitored/snapshotted exactly like any other; no
  Monitoring semantics were touched.
  Export compatibility (#20) / Research Bundle (#21): CSV/XLSX/JSON
  export and Research Bundle manifest generation both already operate
  generically over columns/rows (proven repeatedly since V1.18) — a
  template-sourced mixed DOM+structured dataset passes through both
  completely unmodified, verified directly.

---- 22. Trial / license accounting ----
  Completely unchanged commercial model (10 free completed operations,
  then $5 unlock). Every template action — open, preview, apply, save,
  import, export, rename, duplicate, delete — is a pure chrome.storage.
  local read/write with NO run/message sent to background.js's scraping
  engine at all, so none of them can possibly consume a trial credit;
  verified directly across a full open->preview->apply->save->rename
  sequence (test-popup-v122-templates.js TEST H) staying at the exact
  starting credit count throughout.

---- 23. Security ----
  Every imported field is rebuilt into a fresh plain object with a
  narrow, hardcoded key set — never `obj[untrustedKey] = value` with an
  attacker-controlled key (no prototype-pollution vector exists by
  construction, verified directly with a `"__proto__"` attributeName
  value staying inert plain data). Column attribute is checked against
  a fixed enum; anything else (including literally the string
  `"eval"`) is dropped, never interpreted as a capability or executed.
  No eval()/new Function()/dynamic code path exists anywhere in this
  file, matching the rest of this project (scripts/release-check.js's
  security scan reverified clean). A "script-like"/HTML-like name or
  description survives import purely as an inert string — this project
  already renders all such user-facing text via textContent throughout
  (unchanged, pre-existing convention), so it is never interpreted as
  markup either.

---- 24. UX ----
  "Templates" / "Start with a template" language throughout (button:
  "📋 Templates"; action: "💾 Save Current as Template"); Preview shows
  plain-language notes ("Matched fields below will be ADDED to your
  current columns", "Applying this template will REPLACE your current
  columns and Run Mode settings") rather than internal terms like
  "fieldHints" or "applyStrategy", which never appear in the UI at all.

---- 25. Empty / failure states ----
  No suitable suggestion -> the suggestion note is simply hidden (never
  a fabricated low-confidence guess with nothing behind it). Zero
  matched fields for a built-in template -> Apply is hidden, the
  preview clearly lists every hint as "not found". A Generic Table
  template on a page with no table -> a clean "(no matching structure
  detected on this page)" unmatched note, not a crash. Malformed
  import / incompatible version -> a specific, human-readable error,
  never a partial/corrupt save. A column whose selector no longer
  matches after being applied to a different page behaves exactly like
  any other stale column already does (V1.1's existing "no matching
  elements" Preview message) — nothing new needed there.

---- 26. Performance ----
  Suggestions and built-in-template matching both reuse the SAME
  RUN_AUTO_DETECT / SCAN_STRUCTURED_DATA scan, fetched at most ONCE per
  popup session (cached in `lastTemplateScan`) — opening the Templates
  panel, switching between several template previews, and computing
  suggestions all share that one scan rather than re-scanning the page
  each time.

---- Tests ----
  test-templates-v122.js (72 assertions): the built-in catalog (all 8,
  every spec-required field present), hint-matching (structured-first,
  Auto-Detect-fallback, zero-evidence-never-fabricates), the Generic
  Table/List special strategies, suggestions for every signal type,
  full custom-template CRUD, built-in-template deletion protection,
  export/import round-trips (including mixed DOM+structured+pagination+
  Deep Scrape config), and malformed/hostile import handling (invalid
  JSON, wrong top-level shape, missing name, zero valid columns, a
  partially-bad column silently dropped, a future schema version
  rejected with a clear message, and script-like strings proven inert).
  test-popup-v122-templates.js (28 assertions): the full popup UI —
  list rendering with the suggestion note, built-in preview + apply
  (append + collision suffix), the Auto-Detect-blocked-by-existing-
  container note, Save Current as Template, custom-template direct
  apply (replace), Rename/Duplicate/Delete, built-in templates
  correctly offering ONLY Preview, and trial accounting staying
  untouched across a full template workflow.
  test-v122-crossfeature.js (16 assertions): a template-applied
  configuration saving/reloading as a completely ordinary ​Saved
  Scraper with no special marker, a template-generated column staying
  freely editable, a fully pre-V1.22 legacy scraper loading unaffected,
  Deep Scraping with a template-captured deepScrapeConfig charging
  exactly 1 credit, Research Bundle correctly carrying a mixed DOM+
  structured template dataset, and a hostile/malformed import never
  crashing or corrupting existing data.

  Full regression result: all 70 scratchpad test files (V1.1 through
  V1.22) pass with zero known regressions — 2,076 total assertions,
  reverified stable across two consecutive full-suite runs.

---- Bugs found and fixed ----
  None in production code — V1.22 shipped with zero self-caught defects
  requiring a fix. One test-assumption correction was made during
  development (not a product bug): an early assertion miscounted how
  many E-commerce template field hints should show as "unmatched" with
  zero page evidence (11, not 10) — corrected by re-counting the
  template's own real field list.

---- Known limitations ----
  - Real Estate's structured-data coverage is thin by design — schema.
    org has no canonical, widely-adopted type for property listings, so
    most of its fields rely on Auto Detect (or manual entry) rather
    than a structured match.
  - A built-in template's Auto-Detect-sourced fields are skipped
    (rather than attempted) whenever the scraper already has a
    container/columns set, to avoid ever silently blending two
    unrelated containers' selectors — the user must Reset Columns first
    to use those specific fields. Structured-data-sourced and computed
    (Position) fields are never affected by this restriction.
  - Template export/import goes through a copy/paste dialog (prompt()),
    not a file download — the same sandboxed-artifact-viewer constraint
    every other export path in this project already works within;
    fully functional, just not a native "Save File" dialog.
  - "Position" has no meaning for a Deep Scraping detail page (a single
    page has no "row N of M" concept) and resolves to the constant '1'
    there — documented, not a bug.

============================================================
V1.23 readiness
============================================================
V1.22 leaves utils/templates.js fully isolated from every other
module's own schema — no Saved Scraper, Deep Scrape, Monitoring,
Snapshot, or export code needed a single change to support Templates —
with zero known regressions across the whole V1.1-V1.22 suite. Nothing
about this version blocks V1.23 (Advanced Data Cleaning) — that work is
orthogonal (transforming already-collected values, not how a scraper's
configuration is built or reused) and V1.22 deliberately did not
anticipate or half-build any V1.23 functionality.

============================================================
V1.23 — Advanced Data Cleaning / Transformations
============================================================
Goal: strengthen the raw-data preparation layer so a user can clean and
normalize scraped values before export, monitoring comparison, snapshots,
or a Research Bundle — strictly RAW DATA COLLECTION + PREPARATION (V1.x),
never analysis/visualization/statistics/business interpretation (V2.x
territory, explicitly out of scope and NOT touched here: no averages,
medians, distributions, correlations, charts, recommendations).

AUDIT FIRST (explicit spec instruction, #1): utils/transforms.js
