(WSTransforms) was fully re-read before writing anything. Finding: a
mature, well-designed V1.7 transform engine ALREADY existed — a
deterministic ordered pipeline (trim/collapseWhitespace/removeCurrency/
extractNumber/normalizeNumber/findReplace/regexReplace/regexExtract/
changeCase/prefixSuffix/fillEmpty/normalizeUrl/removeTrackingParams/
substring/stripHtml/split/combine), non-destructive by design (rawRows
are NEVER mutated — the "current" dataset is always re-derived fresh via
applyTransforms(rawRows, columns, transforms, context)), with live
preview-before-apply, Undo Last / Reset, a documented RAW -> TRANSFORMS
-> FILTER -> REMOVE DUPLICATES -> SORT -> display/export pipeline order,
and Saved Scraper persistence with "Apply saved transforms automatically"
already wired through Monitoring's own automated run path
(background.js's fetchOneDetailPage-adjacent monitored-check code already
calls WSTransforms.applyTransforms before diffing — see spec #35 below).
V1.23 is therefore ENTIRELY an EXTENSION of this one existing engine —
no second transformation engine was built anywhere.

---- 1. New transform types added to utils/transforms.js ----
  Text cleaning: removeLineBreaks, normalizeLineBreaks, removeTabs,
  removeInvisibleChars (zero-width space/non-joiner/joiner, word joiner,
  BOM, soft hyphen — conservative, never touches real Unicode text),
  normalizeUnicode (NFC/NFKC compose only — never NFD/NFKD decompose,
  which would visibly split accented characters), decodeHtmlEntities (a
  dedicated regex-only decoder — deliberately NOT DOMParser, so a stray
  literal '<'/'>' is never misread as a tag; covers all numeric/hex
  entities plus a conservative table of ~20 common named ones, an
  unrecognized named entity is left untouched rather than guessed),
  capitalizeFirst (first letter only, rest of the string untouched,
  distinct from Title Case), removePrefix/removeSuffix (dedicated exact-
  match ops, case-sensitivity option, distinct from Find & Replace).
  Number/currency/percentage/date/boolean: normalizeNumber gained an
  explicit `mode` option (auto — unchanged V1.7 behavior reusing
  WSResults.normalizeNumericString's disambiguation / us / eu / custom
  separators); normalizeCurrency (keepText / removeSymbol /
  normalizeValue — NEVER performs exchange-rate conversion, by design,
  out of scope for all of V1.x); normalizePercentage (asNumber / asDecimal
  — always the user's EXPLICIT choice, never guessed); normalizeDate (a
  small DEDICATED parser — deliberately NOT Date.parse()/`new Date(str)`,
  whose ambiguous-format handling is locale/engine-dependent; ISO and
  named-month forms are always unambiguous and resolved automatically; a
  bare numeric D/M/Y-or-M/D/Y form is resolved automatically ONLY when
  one candidate is >12 (so it can only be the day) — a genuinely
  ambiguous numeric date with no explicit dayMonthOrder hint is left
  COMPLETELY UNCHANGED, never guessed; no timezone is ever fabricated
  onto a value that never had one); normalizeBoolean (fully user-
  configurable trueValues/falseValues comma lists and output labels —
  the shipped defaults are a convenience starting point only, never an
  imposed ecommerce-specific meaning, per spec #13).
  URL/domain: extractDomain (hostname/domain-with-www-stripped/origin/
  path/query — a transformation, not analysis); normalizeUrl's existing
  protocol-relative-URL handling (already correct via `new URL(s, base)`)
  documented and covered by a dedicated test.
  Extended existing ops: fillEmpty gained `matchValues` (an extra comma-
  list of "missing-like" tokens — e.g. "-, N/A, NA, null, None" — matched
  case-insensitively IN ADDITION to a truly blank value) and `mode`
  ('custom' fills opts.value, 'toEmpty' clears to '') — spec #14. Find &
  Replace gained an `occurrence` option (first/all). Regex Extract gained
  an `all` mode (every match, joined with a configurable separator,
  reusing one precompiled regex with a defensive `lastIndex` reset before
  each call rather than recompiling per row). Split gained a whitespace
  delimiter mode and, more significantly, four new VALUE-style
  `outputMode`s — firstPart/lastPart/partByIndex/joinParts — alongside
  its original structural 'columns' mode (still the default, unchanged);
  WSTransforms.isStructuralSplit(t) is the one place that distinguishes
  them, used consistently by both the engine and the popup UI.

---- 2. Transform pipeline / ordering / enable-disable (spec #2) ----
  Order already mattered (V1.7); V1.23 adds REORDER (▲/▼ buttons per
  history row, a plain array-index swap — a resulting reference to a
  column a reordered step no longer produces surfaces as the SAME clean,
  already-established error-and-fallback path as any other stale-column
  transform, never a crash) and per-step ENABLE/DISABLE (`t.enabled ===
  false` — and ONLY that exact value — skips a step while leaving it in
  the list/order; a checkbox per history row, a "(disabled)" label
  suffix, a struck-through/dimmed row style). Both are additive fields:
  a transform saved before V1.23 has neither field at all, which means
  "enabled" and "no explicit destination" respectively — fully backward
  compatible by construction, verified directly (test-transforms-v123.js).

---- 3-18. Text/regex/split/merge/number/currency/percentage/date/
     boolean/null/URL/domain/HTML-to-text (spec's own numbering) ----
  All covered by #1 above; HTML to Text is the pre-existing stripHtml
  (DOMParser-based, browser-only — a non-browser context like a pure
  Node test leaves the value untouched rather than fail) now paired with
  decodeHtmlEntities in the new "HTML to Text" preset (see #6).

---- 19-20. Column Derivation / raw-value preservation (spec #19-20) ----
  A NEW generic mechanism, `destination:'newColumn'` + `newColumnName`,
  available on every VALUE-style transform (never combine, never split's
  structural 'columns' mode — both already create their own new
  column(s)): the source column is left COMPLETELY untouched (its raw
  value stays exactly as extracted) and a fresh column
  (`derived_<transformId>`) is inserted right after it, carrying the
  transformed value. Omitting `destination` (every transform that ever
  existed before this — nothing needed to change) is exactly the
  original in-place behavior. This is the SAME mechanism split's four
  new value-style output modes plug into, so "Split -> First Part -> new
  column" and "Normalize Currency -> new column" share one code path
  (applyValueStyle in utils/transforms.js) rather than two.
  Raw-value preservation more broadly: this was already the V1.7
  architecture's central guarantee (rawRows is NEVER mutated, period —
  every "current" view, including a destination:newColumn one, is
  re-derived fresh) and needed no schema rewrite to satisfy spec #20.

---- 21-22. Live preview / pipeline preview (spec #21-22) ----
  The existing sampled-rows before/after preview (WSTransforms.
  previewTransform, unchanged) now also understands the new destination
  mechanism (shows the DERIVED column's value as "after", not the
  now-untouched source) and split's value-style output modes.

---- 23. Bulk Apply (spec #23) ----
  Not built as a separate "apply to multiple columns" flow — Transform
  Presets (#25) already give the equivalent "apply a whole recipe to one
  column in one click" outcome for the concrete cases the spec names,
  without introducing a second application surface; applying an
  incompatible transform to an unrelated column type was always possible
  before V1.23 too (the UI doesn't type-gate columns) and remains a
  user choice, consistent with spec #24's "suggestions only, never
  auto-transform" framing.

---- 24. Type-aware suggestions (spec #24) ----
  Deliberately NOT built as an automatic suggestion engine this version
  — see #27 below for the one, DELIBERATELY MINIMAL, informational-only
  touchpoint that does exist (a plain tip sentence in a built-in
  Template's preview, never applied automatically). A broader type-aware
  suggestion surface remains open for a later version; nothing in V1.23
  blocks adding one.

---- 25. Transform Presets (utils/transforms.js: WSTransforms.PRESETS) ----
  Four ready-made, column-scoped step SEQUENCES — Clean Text (Trim ->
  Collapse Whitespace -> Remove Invisible Characters -> Decode HTML
  Entities), Normalize Price (Trim -> Normalize Currency[normalizeValue]),
  Normalize URL (Trim -> Normalize URL[removeFragment]), HTML to Text
  (Strip HTML -> Decode HTML Entities -> Collapse Whitespace). Applying
  one just pushes ordinary, individually-editable/removable/reorderable
  transform steps into the SAME activeTransforms list as anything built
  by hand — added ATOMICALLY (validated against the full dataset as one
  unit; none are added if any single step would fail) — never a locked
  "preset mode."

---- 26. Saved Scraper persistence (spec #26) ----
  Already fully solved by V1.7's `scraper.transforms`/`autoApplyTransforms`
  fields — a legacy scraper missing either simply gets `[]`/`true`
  respectively (WSRecipes' existing self-heal, unchanged). Every new
  V1.23 transform TYPE is just another string value inside an existing,
  already-generic `options` bag — no schema version bump was needed.

---- 27. Template / Recipe compatibility (V1.22 integration) ----
  A custom Template (utils/templates.js) may now optionally carry a
  `transforms` field, captured by "Save as Template" and restored on
  Apply — mirroring the REPLACE semantics a custom template already uses
  for columns/pagination/Deep Scrape (built-in templates, which APPEND
  columns instead, deliberately do NOT carry or auto-apply transforms —
  spec's explicit "never force transformation without explicit UX
  confirmation"; a short, purely informational tip sentence appears in a
  relevant built-in template's preview instead, e.g. suggesting Normalize
  Price for the E-commerce template's Price field, never applied).
  A REAL bug was found and fixed here (see "Bugs found and fixed" below):
  normalizeTemplateColumn always mints a brand-new column id (a
  deliberate, pre-existing V1.22 security choice — never trust an
  imported id), which orphaned any `transforms[i].column` /
  `deepScrapeConfig.sourceColumnId` reference the INSTANT a template was
  normalized. Fixed by building one old-id -> new-id map while
  normalizing `columns` and rewriting every such reference through it —
  once at SAVE time (utils/templates.js) and again at APPLY time
  (popup.js, since applying mints a SECOND fresh set of ids so the
  template can be re-applied any number of times without collision).
  Both the transform pipeline's `column`/`options.sourceColumns`
  references and Deep Scrape's `sourceColumnId` are now correctly
  remapped at both points.

---- 28. Structured Data compatibility (spec #28) ----
  Needed NO code change at all: a `attribute:'structured'` column (V1.21)
  is just an ordinary column id to the transform engine, which reads/
  writes `row[col.id]` regardless of the column's `attribute` — verified
  directly (test-v123-crossfeature.js TEST 6) rather than assumed.

---- 29-31. Deep Scrape / Multi-Page / Pause-Resume-Retry compatibility
     (spec #29-31) ----
  All three needed NO transform-engine change: transforms are computed
  ONLY in the popup (and background.js's monitoring path), purely as a
  re-derivation from rawRows on every render/diff — never during
  scraping itself, never written back into rawRows. Deep Scrape's merged
  detail-page columns are ordinary row values by the time a transform
  sees them, so row alignment is automatically preserved (verified,
  TEST 4). Pause/Resume/Retry only ever APPEND more raw rows to rawRows
  — since applyTransforms always starts fresh from rawRows and never
  mutates it, there is no mechanism by which a value could be transformed
  twice; re-running the identical pipeline against identical input is
  byte-for-byte idempotent (verified directly, TEST 5).

---- 32-33. Filter / Sort / Remove Duplicates semantics (spec #32-33) ----
  DOCUMENTING, not changing, the existing (V1.7-established) behavior:
  all three operate on the TRANSFORMED dataset (computeTransformedResult()
  in popup.js — Filter/Sort/Dedupe all read from it, never from rawRows/
  state.columns directly), and compareKey/dedupe identity is likewise
  computed against transformed values. This was already the single most
  consistent choice available (it's what "Results" visibly shows) and
  needed no migration.

---- 34. Snapshot behavior (spec #34) ----
  DOCUMENTING the existing behavior: a saved Snapshot stores the
  TRANSFORMED dataset (WSChanges.toNamedRows(transformed.columns,
  transformed.rows) — already established since V1.7, re-verified by
  test-popup-v17.js's own TEST S, unchanged this version).

---- 35. Monitoring behavior — IMPORTANT (spec #35) ----
  The single most safety-critical guarantee in this whole version:
  transform re-evaluation must NEVER cause a false change alert. Audited
  and verified directly (test-v123-crossfeature.js TEST 1/2) against
  background.js's REAL automated monitoring code path (not a mock) —
  every scheduled/manual monitored run already re-applies
  `scraper.transforms` via WSTransforms.applyTransforms before diffing
  against the previous (also transformed) snapshot; since transforms are
  pure, deterministic functions of (rawRows, columns, transforms), two
  runs against IDENTICAL underlying raw data always produce byte-
  identical transformed output — TEST 1 confirms zero false positives
  across a full monitored-run cycle; TEST 2 confirms a REAL underlying
  change is still correctly detected THROUGH the pipeline. If the
  transform CONFIGURATION itself changes between two runs, a resulting
  diff correctly reflects that (expected, not a bug — the visible dataset
  genuinely changed).

---- 36-37. Exports / Research Bundle (spec #36-37) ----
  Both already read from computeTransformedResult()'s output (or the
  popup's own already-established "transformed+filtered+sorted" results
  function) — a destination:newColumn derived column, or any new V1.23
  transform's output, flows into CSV/Excel/JSON/Copy and the Research
  Bundle manifest exactly like any other column, with no separate raw-
  value export mode added (kept the default UX simple, per spec #36's
  own "do not overcomplicate").

---- 38. Large text safeguards (spec #38) ----
  Regex ops retain V1.7's MAX_REGEX_INPUT_LENGTH (5000 chars) defensive
  skip; the 'all matches' extraction mode caps its match-collection loop
  at 10000 iterations as an additional guard against a pathological
  zero-length-match pattern.

---- 39-40. Security / error handling (spec #39-40) ----
  No eval, no new Function, no executable transform field anywhere (re-
  verified by scripts/release-check.js's static scan, 0 findings).
  sanitizeTransformList (new, utils/transforms.js) rebuilds an untrusted
  transform pipeline — from an imported Template's JSON file — into a
  fixed-shape plain object exactly like utils/templates.js's own
  normalizeTemplate/normalizeTemplateColumn already do for columns: an
  unknown `type` is dropped; every `options` value is deep-sanitized
  (only string/number/boolean/array-of-primitives survive, capped in
  both length and array size; a nested object or function is silently
  dropped); a malformed step never partially corrupts the pipeline, it's
  just excluded. Verified end-to-end (test-v123-crossfeature.js TEST 7):
  a hostile imported template's "executable-looking" step is dropped,
  and a surviving step's `${...}`-shaped string is proven to be inserted
  LITERALLY by a regex .replace() — never evaluated, never executed —
  by actually RUNNING it through the real engine, not just inspecting it.
  Invalid regex, invalid date, ambiguous date, invalid number, missing
  source column, unsupported transform type — every one already has a
  clean, established fallback (leave the value unchanged, or a single
  clear thrown Error caught by computeTransformedResult and shown as a
  status message with the untransformed data still displayed) — no run
  is ever crashed by one bad value.

---- 41. Generic requirement ----
  Nothing in V1.23 is ecommerce-specific; normalizeBoolean/normalizeDate/
  normalizeCurrency's defaults are convenience starting points, never
  imposed meanings, and were exercised in tests against product, job,
  article, and generic-table-style sample data.

---- 43. Trial / license ----
  Unchanged: 10 free completed scrape operations, then a one-time
  unlock. Verified directly (test-popup-v123-transforms.js TEST M): a
  full workflow of adding/removing/reordering/enabling/disabling/
  previewing transforms, adding a Preset, and saving+applying a custom
  Template consumes ZERO trial credits — only an actual completed scrape
  run does, exactly as before.

---- 44. Performance ----
  No obvious O(n^2) introduced. Regex ops (Regex Replace/Extract) now
  compile their pattern ONCE per pipeline build and reuse the compiled
  RegExp across every row (previously Regex Extract/Replace recompiled
  per row — a pre-existing inefficiency now also fixed as part of adding
  the 'all matches' mode, since a stateful `lastIndex` reset was needed
  there anyway). Split's parts-computation likewise compiles its regex
  once. No new debouncing was needed — the existing live-preview already
  only samples a small slice of rawRows.

---- Files changed ----
  utils/transforms.js  — ~500 new lines: every new op above, the
    destination/newColumn mechanism, split's value-style output modes,
    enable/disable skip logic, PRESETS, sanitizeTransformList.
  utils/templates.js   — `transforms` field added to the template schema;
    the id-remap fix described in #27.
  popup/popup.html     — ~250 new lines: Presets row, ~15 new operation
    option groups, the generic destination/new-column-name control,
    reorder/enable markup is generated in JS (not raw HTML).
  popup/popup.css      — reorder-button and disabled-row styling.
  popup/popup.js       — ~35 new `els` refs; TRANSFORM_OPTION_GROUPS/
    TRANSFORM_LABELS extended; buildCandidateTransform extended for
    every new op + the destination mechanism; renderTransformHistory
    rewritten for reorder/enable-disable; handleAddPreset (new);
    handleTemplateApply's isDirect branch extended to restore/remap a
    template's transform pipeline; a real ordering bug fixed (activeTransforms
    was being set BEFORE clearResults(), which unconditionally resets it
    to [] — now set strictly after, matching applyLoadedScraper's
    established order); a real wiring bug fixed (the operation-select
    change listener unconditionally called updateSplitFieldVisibility(),
    which now also has Scope/Destination side effects — was clobbering
    those shared controls for every OTHER operation type; fixed by
    letting renderTransformOptionGroups() be the single authority,
    calling updateSplitFieldVisibility() itself only when type==='split').

---- Tests ----
  test-transforms-v123.js (172 assertions) — every new engine op, null-
    safety, destination/newColumn mechanics, split's new output modes,
    enable/disable skip logic, sanitizeTransformList (including hostile/
    prototype-pollution-shaped input), Presets run end-to-end.
  test-popup-v123-transforms.js (38 assertions) — every new operation
    type through the real UI, reorder, enable/disable (including DOM re-
    query after a rebuild — renderTransformHistory replaces every <li>
    on each change), the destination control's visibility wiring, a
    Preset applied through the UI, Save-as-Template capturing the
    pipeline and a custom Template's Apply restoring it, trial accounting
    across the whole workflow.
  test-v123-crossfeature.js (16 assertions) — Monitoring false-positive-
    proof (real background.js code, not a mock) both directions, a
    legacy scraper with no `transforms` field, Deep Scrape row alignment
    with a transformed detail column, Pause/Resume/Retry idempotency,
    a Structured Data column supporting the same pipeline, and the
    hostile-Template-import security proof described in #39-40.
  Pre-existing test-transforms.js (97 assertions, unchanged) and
  test-popup-v17.js (20 assertions, unchanged — basic apply/undo/reset/
  split/persistence/Filter-after-transform/Snapshot-uses-transformed-
  data) both re-verified passing with zero changes, confirming full
  backward compatibility of the V1.7 engine this version extends.

---- Bugs found and fixed (all caught by this version's own tests, none
     shipped) ----
  1. (production) handleTemplateApply's custom-template branch set
     `activeTransforms` from the template's saved pipeline BEFORE calling
     clearResults() — which unconditionally resets activeTransforms to []
     — silently discarding the just-restored pipeline. Fixed by moving
     the restore to strictly after clearResults(), matching
     applyLoadedScraper's already-correct order.
  2. (production) normalizeTemplateColumn's always-mint-a-fresh-id
     behavior (a correct, deliberate V1.22 security choice) orphaned a
     transform pipeline's column references at SAVE time, and again at
     APPLY time. Fixed with an old-id -> new-id remap at both points (see
     #27's fuller writeup) — this also transparently fixed the SAME
     latent issue for Deep Scrape's `sourceColumnId`, a pre-existing
     V1.22 gap this version happened to touch the exact same code path
     for.
  3. (production) the operation-select change listener's unconditional
     call to updateSplitFieldVisibility() clobbered the new Scope/
     Destination controls for every operation type OTHER than split, the
     instant that function grew a Scope/Destination side effect for
     split's own structural-vs-value-style distinction. Fixed by making
     renderTransformOptionGroups() the single authority for those shared
     controls.
  4. (test-only) test-popup-v113-tabs.js's fixed 150ms post-boot settle
     margin (used since V1.13) became genuinely too tight — reproduced a
     ~10% failure rate across 10 isolated re-runs at 150ms — because
     popup.js has grown substantially again with this version's ~600
     new lines. Widened to 400ms throughout that file (matching the
     identical fix already applied to test-popup-v115-trial.js in
     V1.22); 10/10 clean afterward. No production code involved.

---- Known limitations ----
  - normalizeUnicode's NFC/NFKC choice is not exposed in the popup UI
    (always defaults to the safer NFC) — the engine itself accepts an
    `opts.form` for programmatic/future use.
  - normalizeDate's parser recognizes ISO, named-month, and slash/dash/
    dot-separated numeric dates; it does not attempt free-form natural-
    language dates ("next Tuesday", "3 days ago") — out of scope, and
    consistent with spec #12's "do not fabricate."
  - Bulk Apply (spec #23) was deliberately not built as its own UI
    surface — see #23/#24 above for the reasoning and the narrower,
    safer alternative (Presets) that covers the same concrete use cases.
  - Type-aware transform SUGGESTIONS (spec #24) are limited to a single
    informational tip sentence in a built-in Template's preview — no
    column-by-column "this looks like a price, add Normalize Price?"
    prompt exists yet in the main Transform panel itself.

============================================================
V1.24 readiness
============================================================
V1.23 leaves utils/transforms.js as the SOLE transform engine (still
true after this version — no second one was ever introduced), fully
backward compatible with every transform saved before it, with zero
known regressions across the complete V1.1-V1.23 suite (72 standard-
format test files, 2302 assertions). Nothing about this version commits
V1.24 (Export/Destination Improvements) to any particular design — the
export/Research Bundle code this version touched (by reading, never by
restructuring) is already reading from the exact transformed/filtered/
sorted dataset any export-destination improvement would also need.

============================================================
V1.24 — Export / Destination Improvements
============================================================
Goal: make exports and destinations significantly more professional and
useful — selected-column export, CSV/Excel quality improvements
(delimiter, freeze/autofilter/widths, opt-in typed cells), NDJSON, a
Copy format picker, stronger filename generation, optional metadata,
explicit raw/transformed export semantics, and a clean (honestly
unconfigured) destination abstraction for Google Sheets — while never
weakening existing CSV/Excel/JSON/Copy/Images/Files/Research Bundle
functionality, and never drifting into V2.x analysis territory (no
charts/averages/medians/correlations/recommendations/insights).

AUDIT FIRST (explicit spec instruction, #1): utils/csv.js, utils/xlsx.js,
utils/downloads.js, utils/research.js, utils/settings.js, and popup.js's
export/Copy/Bulk-Download/Research-Bundle handlers were fully re-read
before writing anything. Finding: the export pipeline was ALREADY quite
mature — getExportRows()/computeDisplayRows() already fed every export
path from the SAME canonical transformed+filtered+sorted dataset (so
"never export just a preview slice" and "never double-transform" were
already true architecturally); WSDownloads already had deterministic
per-asset filenames, collision handling (name_2, name_3), sanitization,
dedup, and {ColumnName}/{index} filename templates for Bulk Download and
Research Bundle assets; the Research Bundle ZIP pipeline already had
real progress states and a working Stop/Cancel (STOP_ZIP_RUN); dataset-
info.json already existed with solid metadata. V1.24 is therefore an
EXTENSION of this existing infrastructure — no second export engine, no
second downloader, was built anywhere.

---- 1-4. Export options / selected-column export / column order ----
  A new Export Options panel (popup.js/popup.html), collapsed by
  default — clicking CSV/Excel/JSON/Copy without ever opening it exports
  every column with zero configuration (spec #2's explicit beginner
  requirement, verified directly — test-popup-v124-export.js TEST A).
  Opening it shows one checkbox per EFFECTIVE column (Select All/None
  provided); the checked subset narrows every export/Copy/destination
  call via a single shared buildExportData()/buildExportDataForCurrentOptions()
  path — never a second, export-specific column-resolution routine.
  Column ORDER follows Results' own column order (state.columns, already
  reorderable via the existing Columns UI) — no separate custom-order
  control was added; spec #4 explicitly allows this ("Results column
  order by default... optional custom order IF architecture supports it
  cleanly") and a second reorder surface would be redundant with the one
  that already exists.

---- 5. CSV improvements (utils/csv.js) ----
  rowsToCSV(columns, rows, opts) gained opts.delimiter (','|';'|'\t',
  default ',') — quoting logic now checks whichever delimiter is
  ACTIVE, not a hardcoded comma. Unicode/empty-value/long-text handling
  was already correct (untouched). Arrays/object-like values are handled
  the same way they always were: every value is String()-coerced before
  writing — see #18-19 below for why this was already the right,
  deterministic behavior and needed no change. See #38 below for the
  CSV formula-injection protection this version adds, ON BY DEFAULT.

---- 6. Excel improvements (utils/xlsx.js) ----
  buildWorkbook(columns, rows, opts) gained three usability
  improvements, ALL DEFAULT ON (they only affect view-state/formatting,
  never a cell's actual value, so nothing that reads XLSX cell content
  could ever be affected):
    - freezeHeader — a frozen top row (sheetViews/pane), so scrolling a
      large result set keeps the header visible.
    - autoFilter — a basic autoFilter over the whole data range.
    - columnWidths — a simple "widest cell wins" width heuristic per
      column (bounded 8-60 characters so one pathological long value
      can't blow a column out).
  A fourth, OPT-IN-ONLY (default OFF) option, typeCells, writes a value
  as a REAL Excel number (t="n") instead of inline-string text — but
  ONLY when it's unambiguously safe (WSXlsx.isSafeNumericValue: no
  leading zero ahead of another digit, so a SKU like "0501234567" is
  never corrupted; bounded digit count to avoid float precision loss).
  Left OFF by default to preserve this module's original, deliberate
  "never reinterpret scraped text" guarantee (documented in its own
  V1.13.2-era header comment) for every caller that hasn't explicitly
  opted in — the popup's own CSV/Excel export buttons do NOT opt in
  (kept maximally safe as the one-click default); a caller that wants
  typed numeric columns can pass { typeCells: true } directly. Dates are
  deliberately NOT written as native Excel date serials (see Known
  Limitations) — a considered simplification, not an oversight.

---- 7. JSON / NDJSON (utils/results.js) ----
  rowsToJSON is unchanged. A new, clearly SEPARATE rowsToNDJSON produces
  one compact JSON object per line, no enclosing array — wired to its
  own "NDJSON" button (never a hidden mode of the JSON button, so a
  normal user asking for "JSON" is never surprised by unfamiliar output).
  Both share one row-shaping helper (rowToPlainObject) so the two
  formats can never silently drift apart in what a "row" even means.

---- 8. Copy improvements ----
  A small format picker (TSV default | CSV | Semicolon... no — CSV |
  JSON) sits directly beside the Copy button — progressive disclosure
  per spec #8: the one-click default (TSV) is completely unchanged, the
  picker is just there, not a separate flow to open first.

---- 9-10. Filename generation / templates (utils/downloads.js) ----
  A NEW, separate function, buildExportFilename(template, context) —
  deliberately NOT the same function Bulk Download/Research Bundle asset
  filenames already use (buildFilenameFromTemplate, which is per-ROW:
  {index}/{ColumnName}) — since an export FILE's name describes the
  whole dataset, not a row. Token vocabulary: {site} {scraper} {date}
  (YYYY-MM-DD, local time) {time} (HH-mm-ss, hyphens not colons — colons
  are an invalid Windows filename character) {rows} {format}. An unknown
  token resolves to empty rather than throwing (matches
  buildFilenameFromTemplate's own established convention); a template
  producing nothing usable falls back to the literal string "export".
  Reuses the EXISTING sanitizeFilename (no new sanitizer) — verified
  directly that no path-traversal sequence or invalid Windows character
  can survive (test-export-v124.js). Default template is
  "{scraper}_{date}", replacing the old hardcoded
  "web-scraper-<host>-<timestamp>" scheme.

---- 11. Export metadata (utils/research.js) ----
  dataset-info.json (already existed since V1.12) gained 5 PURELY
  ADDITIVE fields: collectionMode, deepScrapeEnabled, pagesVisited
  (currently always null — see Known Limitations), transformStepCount,
  exportedAt. No metadata sheet/section was added to CSV/XLSX manifests
  themselves — spec #11 explicitly allows keeping this JSON-only
  ("...only if clean... do not pollute default exports unnecessarily"),
  and dataset-info.json is already the established metadata surface.

---- 12. Large export safety ----
  No new wasteful copies were introduced: buildExportData() clones a
  row only once, only when column-selection or raw-value-inclusion
  actually applies (both opt-in, off by default) — a plain one-click
  export still reads directly from the SAME cached computeTransformedResult()/
  computeDisplayRows() pipeline every other feature already shares.
  ZIP-based exports (Images/Files/Research Bundle) already ran through
  background.js's existing fetch+ZIP infrastructure with real progress
  and Stop/Cancel (STOP_ZIP_RUN, pre-existing) — untouched, still fully
  functional (re-verified, zero regressions).

---- 13-15. Google Sheets / destination abstraction (utils/destinations.js,
     NEW file) ----
  A real, usable WSDestinations.list()/getDestination()/
  exportToGoogleSheets() abstraction covering Download, Clipboard (both
  fully functional today, simply pointing at existing functionality) and
  Google Sheets — DELIBERATELY LEFT UNCONFIGURED. Per spec #13's own
  explicit instruction ("Do not fake an integration... DO NOT hardcode
  fake credentials... keep the feature disabled until configured"): a
  real Sheets export needs a Google Cloud project, the Sheets API, and
  an OAuth 2.0 Client ID registered against a STABLE, PUBLISHED
  extension ID — none of which exist for this repository, and inventing
  a placeholder client_id would make the extension either silently
  non-functional or (worse) LOOK like it works while doing nothing. The
  file's own header comment documents exactly what's needed to finish
  it (4 concrete provisioning steps) — see that comment for the full
  list. The popup's "📊 Google Sheets" button always calls
  WSDestinations.getDestination('google-sheets') first and shows its
  `reason` as a clear status message; exportToGoogleSheets() itself
  REJECTS with a clear error rather than attempting any network call
  while unconfigured — verified directly that this path never sends
  anything anywhere (test-export-v124.js, test-popup-v124-export.js
  TEST H). No OAuth token handling exists yet at all, so spec #15's
  "never expose OAuth tokens in UI/logs" is trivially satisfied (there
  is no token to expose).

---- 16. Export preview (spec #16) ----
  A lightweight, NON-FORCED preview (row count, column count, filename)
  appears inside the Export Options panel only while it's open — a
  one-click export never computes or shows it.

---- 17. Raw vs transformed export (V1.23 integration) ----
  Default: exported values are the transformed/canonical Results values
  (unchanged from V1.1-V1.23). New, OPT-IN "Also include a Raw <Column>
  column" checkbox: for every column with at least one ENABLED, IN-PLACE
  transform touching it (never a destination:'newColumn' or structural
  split/combine result, which already produce their own separate raw+
  transformed column pair naturally — spec #17's own "do not duplicate
  every raw field automatically"), a sibling "Raw <Name>" column is
  inserted carrying the value read directly from rawRows — reusing the
  EXACT SAME "transformed row position always matches its rawRows index"
  invariant V1.23's own getCurrentFilteredRawIndices already relies on,
  via a fresh object-reference -> rawRows-index Map built per export
  (buildRawRowIndexMap). Verified end-to-end (test-popup-v124-export.js
  TEST E): a transformed column keeps its clean value, its Raw sibling
  keeps the untouched original, and a column with no transform gets NO
  raw sibling at all.

---- 18-19. Array / object serialization ----
  DOCUMENTING the existing, already-deterministic behavior (unchanged):
  every cell value is coerced via String(value) before writing to
  CSV/XLSX/TSV — an array or plain object therefore serializes as
  whatever its own String()/toString() produces (an array joins with
  ","; a plain object would read "[object Object]"). In practice this
  was never actually a live concern: nothing in this codebase's
  extraction/structured-data/transform pipeline ever stores a raw JS
  array or object as a cell value — every V1.21 structured-data path
  already flattens to a leaf string (see structureddata.js's own
  flattenToLeaf/getValueAtPath), and every transform op returns a
  string. JSON/NDJSON export preserves whatever string ended up in the
  cell — since that's already always a flat string, "preserve valid
  structured representation" (spec #19) has nothing further to do.

---- 20-22. Image / file export (utils/downloads.js, unchanged core) ----
  Audited: deterministic filenames, collision handling (name_2, name_3,
  case-insensitive), dedup (collectUniqueUrls), {ColumnName}/{index}
  filename templates, per-item status, and a byType/failure summary all
  already existed (V1.5/V1.13.2) and needed no changes — structured-
  data image columns, DOM image columns, and Deep-Scraped image columns
  are all just ordinary URL-bearing columns to this pipeline, already
  handled uniformly. No return to one-save-dialog-per-image was ever at
  risk (the ZIP-packaged pipeline, V1.13.2, remains the only path).

---- 23. Download Files (unchanged core) ----
  Same audit conclusion as #20-22 — WSDownloads.buildDownloadQueue
  already applies the identical dedup/naming/collision/status logic to
  both images and files via one shared `downloadKind` parameter; nothing
  needed to change.

---- 24. Research Bundle improvements ----
  See #11 above for the metadata additions. manifest.xlsx/manifest.json/
  manifest.csv, dataset-info.json's pre-existing fields, images/,
  files/, asset mappings, and Dataset IDs are all completely unchanged
  (re-verified, test-research.js's 58 assertions still pass unmodified).

---- 25-26. Export progress / cancellation ----
  DOCUMENTING: already fully implemented pre-V1.24 (V1.13.2) — real
  progress states (fetching/zipping/awaiting-manifest/completed/
  cancelled/error) and a working Stop button (STOP_ZIP_RUN) for both
  Bulk Download and Research Bundle. CSV/Excel/JSON/NDJSON/Copy are
  synchronous and instant (no ZIP/network step), so no progress UI is
  needed or was added for them.

---- 27. Export history ----
  NOT built (spec explicitly allows skipping: "Do not add it if it
  creates unnecessary state complexity") — a deliberate scope decision,
  not an oversight, to avoid a new persisted-state surface for a purely
  optional, non-essential feature.

---- 28. Saved export preferences (utils/settings.js) ----
  A new, deliberately SMALL exportPreferences object inside the existing
  `ws_settings` key (same isolated-key, self-healing pattern V1.14
  already established for researchFormatDefaults) — csvDelimiter,
  filenameTemplate, includeRawValues, copyFormat. Every default
  reproduces pre-V1.24 behavior exactly; saving only happens when a user
  actually changes something in the Export Options panel. Loaded once at
  init (reusing loadAppSettings()'s existing single WSSettings.load()
  call — no second, redundant storage read) and restored on every
  future popup open — verified directly (test-popup-v124-export.js
  TEST I: change a preference, reopen the popup, confirm it's restored).

---- 29-33. Compatibility (Recipe/Template, Structured Data, Transform,
     Deep Scrape, Multi-Page) ----
  All five verified DIRECTLY against the real popup code (not merely
  assumed), via test-v124-crossfeature.js: a Recipe/Template-created
  dataset exports through the identical buildExportData() path as a
  manually-built one (no special-casing exists to diverge); a
  structured-data column exports correctly and participates normally in
  column selection (TEST 3); a transform pipeline is applied exactly
  once, with exporting the same dataset twice producing byte-identical
  output (TEST 4 — no re-evaluation drift, no double-transformation);
  a Deep-Scraped detail column keeps exact row alignment through
  selected-column filtering, including a row where the detail fetch
  failed (TEST 1); a large (75-row) merged dataset exports IN FULL, never
  a truncated preview slice (TEST 2, spec #33's explicit requirement).

---- 34-36. Snapshot / Monitoring compatibility ----
  Neither was touched by this version at all — export/destination code
  never reads from or writes to ws_snapshots or a scraper's monitoring
  state. Re-verified via the full regression suite (test-popup-v17.js's
  snapshot tests, test-background-monitoring.js) passing unmodified.

---- 37. Trial / license accounting ----
  Unchanged: 10 free completed scrape operations, then a one-time
  unlock. Verified directly (test-popup-v124-export.js TEST J): a full
  workflow of CSV/Excel/JSON/NDJSON/Copy (3 formats)/Google-Sheets-
  attempt/column-selection/raw-values-toggle/delimiter-change/filename-
  template-change consumes ZERO additional trial credits beyond the one
  single completed scrape (Preview) that started the test.

---- 38. Security — CSV/XLSX formula injection (spec #38, CRITICAL) ----
  CSV: ON BY DEFAULT. CSV carries NO type information — a spreadsheet
  program decides purely from a cell's leading character whether to
  treat it as a formula, so ANY scraped value starting with =, +, -, @,
  a tab, or a carriage return is a real, well-known vulnerability class
  ("CSV injection"), regardless of what actually produced it. Mitigated
  via the industry-standard (OWASP CSV Injection) leading-single-quote
  prefix — the spreadsheet program then displays the value as plain
  text. This DOES mean the raw underlying text differs by one character
  from what was scraped for values matching this pattern; documented as
  the accepted, necessary trade-off (spec #38's own "while preserving
  legitimate data as much as possible" — everything NOT matching the
  trigger pattern is completely untouched). Can be explicitly disabled
  per-call via { sanitizeFormulas: false }.
  XLSX: DELIBERATELY NOT applying the same leading-quote mitigation.
  An OOXML cell explicitly typed t="inlineStr" is NOT reinterpreted as a
  formula on load — Excel/LibreOffice/Sheets trust the file's own
  structural type metadata, unlike CSV which has none. Applying CSV's
  mitigation here would needlessly alter legitimate scraped text in a
  format where the underlying vulnerability doesn't actually exist,
  contradicting this module's own "exact round-trip" design goal. This
  is a reasoned, DOCUMENTED decision (written directly into xlsx.js's
  own header comment), not an inconsistency or an oversight.
  Directory traversal / filename injection: unchanged, already-proven-
  safe sanitizeFilename/sanitizeFolderName (V1.5) continue to gate every
  filename anywhere in this version, including the new
  buildExportFilename — verified directly that no path-traversal
  sequence or invalid Windows character can survive it.
  No eval, no new Function, no executable export-template field anywhere
  (re-verified by scripts/release-check.js's static scan, 0 findings,
  now scanning 32 runtime files).

---- 39. Privacy ----
  Every default export path (CSV/Excel/JSON/NDJSON/Copy/Images/Files/
  Research Bundle) remains entirely local/download-based — nothing is
  ever sent anywhere unless a user explicitly picks a network
  destination, and the only such destination this version defines
  (Google Sheets) is unconfigured and REJECTS before attempting any
  network call at all (see #13-15). Nothing in this version adds any
  new outbound network call.

---- 40. Error handling ----
  Every export handler (CSV/Excel/JSON/NDJSON/Copy/Sheets) is wrapped in
  try/catch with a user-facing status message, never a raw stack trace;
  an empty dataset or zero-selected-columns state is caught by the
  existing getExportRows()/buildExportData() gates ("Add at least one
  column first." / "No rows to export." / "Select at least one column
  to export.") before any serialization is attempted. Image/file
  fetch failures, ZIP creation failures, and download-permission issues
  all continue to use their existing, already-established V1.5/V1.13.2
  error paths — untouched.

---- Files created ----
  utils/destinations.js — the destination abstraction described in #13-15.

---- Files changed ----
  utils/csv.js    — delimiter option, formula-injection protection.
  utils/xlsx.js   — freeze header, autofilter, column widths, opt-in
    typed numeric cells; the "why not CSV's mitigation" security
    rationale documented directly in its header comment.
  utils/results.js — rowsToNDJSON, shared rowToPlainObject helper.
  utils/downloads.js — buildExportFilename (export-file token
    vocabulary, separate from the existing per-asset template function).
  utils/research.js — buildDatasetInfo gained 5 additive metadata fields.
  utils/settings.js — exportPreferences (delimiter/filename/raw-values/
    copy-format), same self-healing pattern as researchFormatDefaults.
  popup/popup.html — Export Options panel, Copy format picker, NDJSON
    and Google Sheets buttons.
  popup/popup.css  — Export Options panel + Copy-split-button styling.
  popup/popup.js   — buildExportData/buildExportDataForCurrentOptions/
    columnsWithInPlaceTransform/buildRawRowIndexMap (the raw-value
    mechanism); currentExportFilenameBase/currentCsvDelimiter; rewired
    handleExportCsv/Xlsx/Json + new handleExportNdjson/handleExportSheets;
    handleCopy rewritten for the format picker; Export Options panel
    render/toggle/preview logic; every existing panel-toggle function
    (Filter/Sort/Dedupe/Transform/Snapshots/Download-setup/Research-
    setup) extended to close the new Export Options panel too, matching
    the established "only one panel open at a time" convention; Research
    Bundle's dataset-info.json call site extended with the new metadata.

---- Tests ----
  test-export-v124.js (51 assertions) — CSV delimiter/formula-injection,
    XLSX freeze/autofilter/widths/typed-cells/isSafeNumericValue, NDJSON,
    buildExportFilename (tokens, empty-template fallback, path-traversal/
    invalid-character safety), and the Google Sheets scaffold's honest
    unavailability.
  test-popup-v124-export.js (31 assertions) — one-click defaults require
    zero configuration, selected-column export, Select All/None, CSV
    delimiter through the real UI, filename template + live preview,
    the Raw-column mechanism end-to-end, the Copy format picker (CSV/
    JSON), NDJSON, the Google Sheets button's clear status message,
    saved-preference persistence across a popup reopen, and trial
    accounting across the entire workflow.
  test-v124-crossfeature.js (25 assertions) — Deep Scrape row alignment
    through column selection, a 75-row dataset exporting in full,
    structured-data column export, transform idempotency/no-double-
    transform, a legacy Saved Scraper (missing every V1.24 field) still
    exporting successfully, extended Research Bundle metadata verified
    via a spy on WSResearch.buildDatasetInfo, and an end-to-end CSV
    formula-injection security proof through the real Export flow.
  Pre-existing test-research.js (58 assertions) and every popup test
  file exercising CSV/Excel/JSON/Copy re-verified passing with zero
  changes, confirming full backward compatibility.

---- Bugs found and fixed ----
  None in production code — the export pipeline's existing architecture
  (canonical shared dataset, established sanitizers, existing ZIP/
  progress infrastructure) turned out to already satisfy most of this
  version's compatibility requirements (#29-36) by construction, and
  every new addition was additive/opt-in, so no existing behavior needed
  correcting. Two test-authoring mistakes were caught and fixed during
  development (a wrong expected value in an isolated buildExportFilename
  test, and a trial-accounting test that incorrectly expected 0 charges
  when the correct, established behavior is exactly 1 charge for the
  test's own single completed Preview run) — both are documented in the
  test files' own git history/edits, neither reflects a real defect.

---- Known limitations ----
  - typeCells (Excel numeric typing) and the freeze/autofilter/width
    improvements have no dedicated popup UI toggle for typeCells
    specifically (it's engine-available, opted into by a future caller,
    but the popup's own Export/Excel button never turns it on — kept
    off to preserve the safest possible default).
  - Excel dates are exported as plain text, never native date serials —
    deliberate, avoids timezone/locale ambiguity in a serial-date
    conversion; a V1.23-normalized date column (e.g. "2026-08-22")
    already sorts/reads correctly as text in practice.
  - dataset-info.json's new `pagesVisited` field is currently always
    null — computing an accurate "pages actually visited" figure would
    require deeper run-state plumbing than this version's scope covers;
    left as an honest null rather than a fabricated number.
  - Export History (spec #27) and a dedicated custom EXPORT column-order
    control (spec #4's optional half) were deliberately not built —
    both are explicitly optional in the spec, and Results' own existing
    column-reorder UI already covers the column-order use case.
  - Google Sheets remains fully unconfigured (by design — see #13-15);
    finishing it is a scoped, well-documented follow-up requiring real
    Google Cloud provisioning that doesn't exist in this repository.

============================================================
V1.25 readiness
============================================================
V1.24 leaves the export pipeline fully backward compatible with every
CSV/Excel/JSON/Copy/Images/Files/Research Bundle workflow that existed
before it, with zero known regressions across the complete V1.1-V1.24
suite (75 standard-format test files, 2409 assertions). Nothing about
this version commits V1.25 (Performance / Large Datasets / Final V1 UX)
to any particular design — if anything, V1.24's buildExportData() gives
V1.25 one single, already-cached choke point to optimize for large
datasets, rather than several independent ones.

============================================================
V1.25 — Performance / Large Datasets / Final V1 UX
============================================================
Goal: take the complete V1.1-V1.24 engine and make it performant,
stable, understandable, and production-ready — NOT another large
feature category. Preserve every existing feature; add no V2.x
analysis/charts/statistics of any kind.

FULL AUDIT FIRST (explicit spec instruction, #1): every area the spec
names — popup rendering, Results rendering, scraper execution, content
scripts, background/service worker, pagination/infinite-scroll/Load
More, Deep Scrape, retry, pause/resume, structured data, templates,
transforms, snapshots, monitoring, exports, image/file downloads,
Research Bundle, chrome.storage usage, message passing, DOM scanning,
dedup, URL dedup, asset handling, event listeners, timers,
MutationObservers, navigation waits, retry waits — was actually read
before any change was made. The headline finding: this codebase already
had REAL, deliberate performance engineering baked in across nearly
every prior version, not something V1.25 had to retrofit. Below is what
was found, version by version of prior work, followed by the ONE
concrete bug this audit actually turned up and fixed, and the few
genuine, low-risk additions made on top.

---- What the audit found ALREADY correct (no change needed) ----
  - Results table rendering: already windowed at PREVIEW_LIMIT (30)
    rendered <tr> elements regardless of dataset size (buildPreviewTable/
    renderResults) — a 10,000-row dataset renders exactly 30 DOM rows.
    Filtering/sorting/transforms/dedup/export all continue to operate on
    the FULL canonical dataset (computeTransformedResult/
    computeDisplayRows/getExportRows) — verified directly with a
    1,500-row and a 10,000-row synthetic dataset (test-v125-
    largedatasets.js).
  - Dedup (WSResults.removeDuplicates): a single O(n) hash-map pass
    (Object.create(null) key lookup) — never O(n^2).
  - Filter (WSResults.applyFilter): a single O(n) .filter() pass.
  - Sort (WSResults.applySort): Array.prototype.sort (O(n log n)) with a
    stable index-tiebreak; the numeric-column check runs ONCE before
    sorting starts, not per comparison.
  - Changes/snapshot comparison (WSChanges.compareDatasets): already
    documented and implemented as "O(n) via key->row maps, never a
    nested-loop O(n^2)" — its own header comment already says so.
  - V1.23 transforms: regex-based ops (Regex Replace/Extract, Split)
    already compile their pattern ONCE per pipeline build and reuse the
    compiled RegExp across every row (fixed during V1.23 itself when the
    'all matches' mode was added).
  - Structured data (V1.21): the page-level JSON-LD/OG/Twitter-Card
    snapshot is computed AT MOST ONCE per extraction
    (maybeGetStructuredSnapshot, called once in runExtraction and once
    in runDetailExtraction, never inside a per-row/per-container loop)
    — never re-parsed per row.
  - Auto-Detect (V1.17): already has explicit, hard caps —
    MAX_SCAN_ELEMENTS (6000), MAX_CANDIDATES (8),
    MAX_FIELDS_PER_STRUCTURE (10), and a MAX_TIME_MS (4000ms) soft
    budget that stops deepening analysis once exceeded — a pathological
    page cannot lock it indefinitely.
  - Deep Scrape + ZIP downloads (Bulk Download/Research Bundle): both
    already share ONE bounded-concurrency queue (runWithConcurrency,
    default concurrency 4, capped 1-8) — never hundreds of simultaneous
    requests/tabs.
  - Snapshot scalability: DEFAULT_RETENTION_PER_GROUP already prunes
    old snapshots per scraper/page group on every save, AND a proactive
    byte-size estimate is checked against chrome.storage's own quota
    BEFORE attempting a write, surfacing a clear "Snapshot storage limit
    reached — delete some old snapshots and try again" error rather than
    silently failing or losing data.
  - Monitoring scalability: monitoring.history is already capped at
    MONITOR_HISTORY_LIMIT (50) entries per scraper, truncated on every
    write.
  - Storage-write failure handling: chrome.storage.local.set() failures
    (including quota-exceeded) are already caught via
    chrome.runtime.lastError and surfaced as {ok:false, error} —
    every persist() path in utils/recipes.js already does this, never
    silently reporting success on a write that didn't actually land.
  - Message passing / storage batching: state.columns/containerSelector
    (small, user-authored config) are what actually get persisted to
    chrome.storage.local per edit — the potentially large scraped
    dataset itself (rawRows) is never round-tripped through
    chrome.storage.local on every UI tweak; it lives in memory
    (chrome.storage.SESSION only, for pause/resume run-state recovery,
    which is exactly what that mechanism is for).
  - Permissions (manifest.json): all 6 (activeTab, storage, scripting,
    downloads, alarms, notifications) are genuinely used — activeTab/
    scripting for content-script injection, storage for chrome.storage.*
    everywhere, downloads for chrome.downloads.download, alarms for
    Monitoring's scheduling, notifications for Monitoring's change
    alerts. optional_host_permissions (*://*/*) is requested on-demand
    per-origin at the point of need (image/file fetch, Deep Scrape),
    never a blanket always-on grant. Nothing was removed — nothing was
    unused.
  - Console logging: every console.log call site (content/pagination.js,
    content/content.js) is consistently LOG_PREFIX-tagged structured
    diagnostic output for an otherwise-invisible background scraping
    process (page-change/stop-reason/retry logging) — low-frequency
    (once per page/pass, never per-row), genuinely useful for debugging
    a failed multi-page run, and none of it logs tokens/secrets/
    credentials. Not the "noisy development log" spec #44 asks to
    remove — left as-is.

---- The ONE real bug this audit found and fixed ----
  renderChangesList() (the Changes/comparison view, popup.js) built one
  full DOM subtree PLUS a click listener per changed row with NO cap at
  all — genuinely unbounded, unlike the Results table it sits right next
  to. On a large comparison (many changed/new/removed rows) this was a
  real, concrete risk of the exact "frozen popup" scenario spec #2
  warns about. Fixed with the SAME windowing pattern the Results table
  already used: a new CHANGES_LIST_LIMIT (300, more generous than
  PREVIEW_LIMIT's 30 since a changes entry is usually the reason someone
  opened this view), a truncation note mirroring previewNote's own
  wording, and — critically — the summary stats and CSV/price-changes
  export (currentChangesExport) both continue to read from the FULL,
  un-windowed lastComparisonResult, never the rendered slice. Verified
  directly with an 800-row (1,600-entry) synthetic comparison
  (test-v125-ux-migration.js).

---- Deliberately NOT changed, and why (spec #1's "do not rewrite
     working architecture merely for theoretical performance") ----
  fetchOneZipItem (background.js) writes the full zip-run state
  (including the whole `items` array) to chrome.storage.session on EVERY
  single item completion, not just periodically — this is a genuine,
  real per-item write, and was evaluated as a throttling candidate for
  spec #19/#20. NOT changed, for four concrete reasons: (1) this write
  happens in the BACKGROUND SERVICE WORKER, not the popup's main thread
  — it cannot directly freeze the popup UI; (2) the popup's own
  onChanged listener only re-renders a lightweight progress summary
  (counts, a status line), never a per-item list, so a high write
  frequency doesn't translate into expensive popup-side DOM work; (3)
  realistic asset-download volumes in this product are bounded by
  curated, deduplicated row counts (dozens to a few hundred images/files
  in practice), not raw scraped-row counts in the thousands; (4) this
  exact code path is shared by Bulk Download, Research Bundle, AND Deep
  Scrape's retry/cancel machinery, all already extensively tested —
  introducing throttling logic here carries real regression risk to a
  proven, working system for a benefit that's marginal at this
  product's actual scale. Documented here as an audited-but-deliberately-
  unchanged finding, not an oversight.

---- Large dataset behavior (spec #2, #50) ----
  1,000 / 5,000 rows: verified directly — Results renders in well under
  a second (a generous multi-second sanity ceiling, not a strict
  benchmark, per spec #50's own explicit "do NOT write flaky tests based
  on extremely strict millisecond thresholds"), the DOM never exceeds 30
  rendered rows, and CSV export contains every single row correctly.
  10,000 rows: verified directly at the engine level — a combined
  transform -> filter -> dedup -> sort pipeline over 10,000 rows (with
  ~1,000 intentional duplicate keys) completes in well under 200ms in
  this test environment, produces exactly correct counts (no row created
  or lost anywhere in the pipeline), zero surviving duplicates, and a
  genuinely monotonic sort order across every row.
  25,000+ rows: no dedicated fixture was added at this size (spec #50
  only requires 1k/5k/10k) — the SAME O(n)/O(n log n) algorithms apply
  without a different code path kicking in, so behavior should degrade
  gracefully/linearly rather than catastrophically; the honest, expected
  limiting factor at that scale is chrome.storage.local's own quota (for
  Saved Scrapers/Snapshots) — already surfaced as a clear, actionable
  error (see above) rather than silent data loss.

---- Accessibility (spec #36-39) ----
  Audited: every interactive control in this codebase already uses
  native semantic HTML (<button>, <input>, <select>) — no custom
  click-only <div> pretending to be a button was found anywhere, so Tab
  navigation and Enter/Space activation already work by browser default
  with no code changes needed (spec #36). Icon-adjacent buttons that
  pair an icon with visible text (⬇ Download Images, 📊 Google Sheets,
  ✕ Close, etc.) were already fine as-is (not icon-ONLY, per spec #37's
  actual concern). The 6 genuinely icon-only controls found (column/
  transform-step reorder ▲/▼, column/field delete ×) already had a
  `.title` tooltip; each also gained an explicit aria-label with the
  identical text, the more reliably-announced screen-reader mechanism.
  No focus-trap or broken-modal-close behavior was found in a targeted
  review of the panel-toggle functions (spec #38) — every panel/dialog
  close action is an ordinary button click, not a focus-management-
  dependent flow. No state in this UI relies on color alone — every
  status (running/completed/failed/disabled) already pairs a text label
  or icon with its color (spec #39), unchanged and re-confirmed.

---- UX consistency / duplicate-click / recovery (spec #29-35, #42-43)
     ----
  Audited broadly rather than rewritten wholesale (spec #29's own "do
  not redesign the entire visual identity — polish it," and spec #52's
  "refactor only where the benefit is clear"): status messages, button
  labels, empty states, and confirmation dialogs were already
  reasonably consistent across the 24 prior versions (each of which
  already had its own UX-consistency requirements baked into its own
  spec — e.g. V1.9's success/status conventions, V1.13's tab-navigation
  polish). No duplicate DOM ids were found across all 395 unique ids in
  popup.html (a real risk after 25 versions of additive HTML — checked
  directly, zero collisions). No double-registered init() or duplicated
  event listener was found. Long-running action buttons (Scrape, Deep
  Scrape, Export, ZIP/Research Bundle runs) already show a "running"
  status state and are already gated by their own existing async-await
  flow (a second click while a promise is in flight re-enters the same
  guarded path rather than double-starting a run) — re-verified via the
  full regression suite rather than reworked. Cancellation (STOP_ZIP_RUN)
  already leaves the UI in a valid, re-usable state (Retry/Done buttons
  appear, status is never stuck at "running") — pre-existing V1.13.2
  behavior, unchanged.

---- Service worker resilience (spec #15) — honest limitation ----
  DOCUMENTING, not "fixing" (a documentable platform constraint, not a
  bug): Chrome MV3 service workers CAN be suspended and later restarted
  by the browser at any time; this codebase already designed around
  that reality starting in V1.20 — utils/runstate.js exists specifically
  so an in-progress multi-page/Deep-Scrape run's state lives in
  chrome.storage.session (survives a service-worker restart) rather than
  only in the worker's own in-memory variables, and background.js's own
  bootstrap logic already resumes a run it finds still marked "running"
  after a restart. The one HONEST, UNAVOIDABLE limitation (already true
  before V1.25 and still true now, since Chrome itself provides no
  stronger guarantee): a ZIP run's in-flight `zipRunContexts` (already-
  fetched asset bytes for a not-yet-finalized bundle) are IN-MEMORY ONLY
  — a service-worker restart mid-ZIP-run loses that specific in-flight
  data and the run must be restarted; this was already an explicitly
  documented KNOWN LIMITATION in background.js's own comments before
  V1.25, not a new discovery, and Chrome provides no API to persist
  raw binary asset bytes across a worker restart cheaply — restarting
  the run is the correct, honest behavior rather than pretending
  otherwise.

---- Migration (spec #47) ----
  Verified directly (test-v125-ux-migration.js TEST 3) against a
  deliberately ANCIENT storage shape: a Saved Scraper record missing
  every field added from V1.3 through V1.24 (mode, nextButtonConfig,
  dedupeKey, limits, downloadColumn, filenameTemplate, folderName,
  skipDuplicates, compareKey, transforms, autoApplyTransforms, research,
  deepScrape, monitoring — ALL absent at once) plus a bare-bones
  pre-V1.15 license record, and NO ws_settings/ws_templates/ws_snapshots
  keys ever having existed in storage at all. The popup boots without
  error, the ancient scraper loads and runs a successful Preview, and
  the Templates panel (a feature layered on top of a storage key that
  simply never existed for this install) opens and lists all 8 built-ins
  correctly. No user data — saved scrapers, snapshots, templates,
  trial/license state — is ever lost or corrupted by an old shape.

---- Trial / license final verification (spec #48) ----
  Verified directly with ONE combined, realistic workflow
  (test-v125-ux-migration.js TEST 4): Preview (the one completed scrape)
  -> apply a Transform -> export CSV, Excel, JSON, NDJSON, and Copy ->
  Save as Template -> Save Snapshot -> Save Scraper. Exactly 1 trial
  credit was charged for the entire sequence, with exactly 1
  chargedRunId recorded — confirming, in one single end-to-end pass
  rather than piecemeal, that transforms/exports/templates/snapshots/
  saving a scraper never charge, and that runId idempotency holds. This
  is in addition to (not a replacement for) every version's own
  dedicated trial-accounting tests (V1.15 through V1.24), all of which
  remain in the suite and still pass unmodified.

---- Security regression audit (spec #45) ----
  Re-ran scripts/release-check.js's static scan (no eval, no new
  Function, no remote-executed script, no <script src="http...) — clean
  across all 33 runtime files including the new content added this
  version. CSV formula-injection protection (V1.24) and every prior
  version's sanitization (filenames, imported Template/transform data,
  structured-data parsing) were re-verified passing via the untouched
  pre-existing tests for each, plus this version's own new large-dataset/
  migration tests exercising many of those same paths under realistic
  load.

---- Files created ----
  None — V1.25 was audit-plus-targeted-fixes, not a new feature surface.

---- Files changed ----
  popup/popup.html — a new #changes-truncated-note element.
  popup/popup.js   — CHANGES_LIST_LIMIT + the windowed renderChangesList
    fix; aria-label added to the 6 icon-only reorder/delete buttons
    (columns, transform steps, Deep Scrape fields) alongside their
    existing title attributes.

---- Tests ----
  test-v125-largedatasets.js (14 assertions) — a 10,000-row pure-engine
    transform/filter/dedup/sort pipeline (correct counts, no duplicates/
    lost rows, generous non-flaky sanity-ceiling timing) and a 1,500-row
    DOM-driven Results render (exactly 30 rendered <tr>s, correct row-
    count text, full-dataset CSV export).
  test-v125-ux-migration.js (14 assertions) — the Changes-list windowing
    fix (both the truncated and the not-truncated case), migration from
    a deliberately ancient/incomplete storage shape across every module
    at once, and the combined-workflow trial/license final verification
    described above.
  Every pre-existing test file across the full V1.1-V1.24 suite
  re-verified passing with zero changes.

---- Bugs found and fixed ----
  1. (production) renderChangesList's unbounded per-row DOM render —
     described in full above. The only production bug this version's
     audit turned up.
  No test-authoring bugs required a note this version beyond the usual
  locale-formatting adjustment (.toLocaleString() produces "1.600" under
  a Turkish locale vs "1,600" elsewhere — a test assertion was written
  to check the underlying number regardless of locale-specific digit-
  grouping punctuation, not a product bug).

---- Known limitations ----
  - The ZIP-run per-item progress-write frequency (see "Deliberately
    NOT changed" above) remains unthrottled — an audited, deliberate
    decision, not an oversight.
  - A service-worker restart mid-ZIP-run loses that run's in-flight,
    in-memory-only fetched asset bytes and requires restarting the run
    — an honest, pre-existing (documented since V1.13.2/V1.20), platform-
    level constraint, not something any client-side code change can
    fully eliminate.
  - No dedicated 25,000+-row fixture exists (spec #50 only requires up
    to 10k); behavior at that scale is expected to degrade linearly
    (same O(n)/O(n log n) algorithms, no different code path), with
    chrome.storage.local's own quota as the realistic ceiling for
    long-term persistence (Saved Scrapers/Snapshots), already surfaced
    with a clear, actionable error rather than silent data loss.
  - UX consistency (spec #29) was audited broadly, not rewritten line-
    by-line across all 25 versions' worth of copy — no glaring
    inconsistency was found beyond what's already fixed above; a full
    exhaustive string-by-string audit of every status/button label in
    this now-large codebase was judged lower value than the concrete
    performance/accessibility/migration work above, per spec's own
    explicit "polish it, do not redesign" framing.

============================================================
V1 FINAL readiness
============================================================
V1.25 leaves the complete V1.1-V1.25 engine fully backward compatible,
with zero known regressions across the full suite (77 standard-format
test files, 2437 assertions), one real performance bug found and fixed
(the Changes-list render), accessibility basics addressed, migration
from the oldest realistic storage shapes verified directly, and trial/
license accounting verified end-to-end across a combined realistic
workflow. Nothing here blocks a V1 FINAL full production audit / real-
site validation pass — see the Final Report's own "must be manually
validated in Chrome" list for exactly what a human tester should check
next (an automated JSDOM suite, however thorough, cannot substitute for
loading the actual .zip in chrome://extensions and scraping a handful
of real, live websites).

============================================================
V1 FINAL — Full Production Audit / Real-Site Validation
============================================================
Audit date: 2026-08-23 (session-continuous with V1.25, same day).
NOT a feature version — no new capability was added. This is a read-
the-whole-project, test-everything-locally-testable, fix-what's-safely-
fixable production audit before the extension would be considered ready
for a human to run its own manual real-Chrome validation pass and,
beyond that, a Chrome Web Store submission.

every popup/*, background/*, content/*, utils/*, scripts/* file, and

prior documentation against the ACTUAL code at every claim verified
below — no discrepancy between documented and implemented behavior was
found (V1.17 through V1.25's own sections were each written turn-by-turn
against the real code at the time, and this audit re-confirms none of
them drifted).
