---- 3. Documentation vs code audit ----
No discrepancies found. Each version's own projeler.txt section was
written against the real code at the time it was built, and re-reading
the full implementation against those sections this audit turned up no
documented-but-missing feature, no undocumented-but-implemented feature,
and no outdated limitation claim. The one thing worth calling out
explicitly (not a discrepancy, a clarification): "NDJSON if implemented"
(this spec's own phrasing) — NDJSON IS implemented (V1.24), confirmed
working, not merely scaffolded.

---- 4. Manifest audit ----
manifest_version: 3 (correct). version: bumped 1.25.0 -> 1.25.1 this
audit (see #10 below for why). name/description: present, accurate,
under the Chrome Web Store's practical description length. permissions:
activeTab, storage, scripting, downloads, alarms, notifications — all
6 re-confirmed genuinely used (grep-verified against real call sites for
each this session). host_permissions: none unconditional;
optional_host_permissions (*://*/*) requested on-demand per-origin only
— never a blanket always-on grant. background.service_worker: exists on
disk (background/background.js). action.default_popup: exists
(popup/popup.html). content scripts: not statically declared in
manifest.json at all — every content script is DYNAMICALLY injected via
chrome.scripting (CONTENT_FILES array), each of the 9 files confirmed to
exist on disk by scripts/release-check.js. icons: all 3 sizes present
and referenced correctly. CSP: no explicit content_security_policy key
is set — Chrome's MV3 DEFAULT policy applies (script-src 'self';
object-src 'self'), which is the STRICTEST available baseline and
already forbids unsafe-eval/remote code; nothing in this codebase
weakens it. commands: none declared (no keyboard-shortcut feature
exists — consistent, no gap).

---- 5-6. Static code / dead code audit ----
Zero TODO/FIXME/HACK/XXX markers anywhere in the codebase (grep-
verified, whole project). Zero eval()/new Function() anywhere (grep-
verified; release-check.js's own scan independently confirms this on
every run). Every innerHTML assignment in the entire codebase is `= ''`
(clearing content before an explicit createElement/textContent rebuild)
— never assigned untrusted or dynamic content; confirmed by direct grep
across all 4 directories, zero exceptions. Exactly 1 setInterval exists
(content/domwait.js, a 100ms URL-change poll) — paired 1:1 with its own
clearInterval inside a single `finish()` cleanup path also used for the
MutationObserver disconnect and the abort-signal listener removal — no
leaked timer. Exactly 2 MutationObserver instances exist, exactly 2
disconnect() calls — 1:1 paired, no leaked observer. No localhost/dev-
server/ngrok reference anywhere. No API key/secret/password/token
pattern found anywhere (grep-verified). The only hardcoded
https://www.googleapis.com/... string in the entire codebase is inside
a documentation COMMENT in utils/destinations.js explaining what a real
Google Sheets integration would eventually need — never executable code,
never fetched. Console logging: all content/pagination.js and
content/content.js console.log calls are consistently LOG_PREFIX-tagged
structured diagnostic output for an otherwise-invisible background
scraping process — reviewed and judged genuinely useful (not noisy dev
artifacts), left as-is; none logs sensitive data. No dead/orphan module
was found — every utils/*.js file is loaded and used by at least one of
popup.html's script tags or background.js's importScripts list (cross-
checked directly).

---- 7-8. Message flow / run-state machine audit ----
Every long-running operation (pagination, Deep Scrape, ZIP downloads)
carries its own runId, generated once at start and threaded through
every subsequent message/storage write for that run — confirmed by code
reading across content/pagination.js, background.js's Deep Scrape/ZIP
code, and utils/runstate.js. Status transitions are gated by an explicit
enum (WSRunState.STATUSES) with single-assignment setStatus() calls
(setStatus(runState, 'running'/'paused'/'stopped'/'completed'/'failed')
— never two independent boolean flags that could disagree) — an
"impossible combination" like completed+running cannot occur because
there is only ever ONE status field, and nothing in the codebase reads
or infers status from anything else. Retry state (attempts/backoff) is
tracked per-item, cleared on that item's own success/exhaustion, never
globally. chrome.runtime.lastError / promise rejections are checked at
every storage write path that matters (recipes.js/snapshots.js already
surface write failures rather than assuming success; the popup's own
sendToContent/sendToBackground wrappers propagate a rejected promise up
to the calling handler's own try/catch).

---- 9. Double-click / duplicate-run audit — REAL BUG FOUND AND FIXED
     ----
This audit's most significant finding. handlePreview and handleStartRun
had NO re-entrancy guard: neither the Preview button nor the Start Run
button was ever disabled while its own async chain (a content-script
round-trip for Preview; a permission prompt + content-script
registration + content-script round-trip for Start Run — potentially
SECONDS if a native permission dialog appears) was in flight. A
genuine rapid double-click during that window would start a second,
fully independent extraction/run — for Preview specifically, each
independent success path generates and charges its OWN unique runId
(WSLicense.consumeRunCredit's idempotency only prevents the SAME run
being charged twice, not two genuinely distinct accidental runs), so
BOTH could successfully consume a trial credit for what the user
experienced as one click. Fixed with a minimal, purely-additive
re-entrancy flag (runTriggerInFlight) shared by both functions (renamed
their original bodies to handlePreviewInner/handleStartRunInner, wrapped
in a guarded outer function) — no change to either function's actual
logic, ordering, or error handling. Verified directly: three rapid
Preview clicks while an artificially-delayed extraction is in flight
now result in exactly 1 RUN_EXTRACTION call and exactly 1 trial credit
charged; a genuine SECOND click AFTER the first has fully completed
still works normally and correctly charges its own credit (the guard
only blocks in-flight re-entry, never legitimate sequential use).
Export buttons (CSV/XLSX/JSON/NDJSON/Copy) were evaluated too and
deliberately left unguarded — they don't charge credits and a double-
click there just produces two harmless downloads/clipboard writes, not
a state-corruption or business-model risk, so adding a guard there
would be unnecessary surface area per this audit's own "do not perform
risky rewrites merely for code cleanliness" instruction.

---- 10. Versioning decision ----
manifest.json bumped 1.25.0 -> 1.25.1 (a PATCH release, per this
project's own semver-style convention: patch = bug fix, no feature
change). Justified specifically by item #9 above — a real, user-facing
production defect (a rapid double-click could double-charge a trial
credit) was found and fixed. No other version-justifying change was
made; 2.0.0 was never considered (V1 FINAL is explicitly not V2.0, per
spec's own absolute instruction).

---- 11-17. Normal scrape / selector failure / Auto-Detect / pagination
     / Deep Scrape / retry / pause-resume audits ----
All re-confirmed via the full regression suite (2,470 assertions across
78 files) plus this audit's own new tests — 0/1/multiple rows, missing/
partially-missing fields, duplicate rows, long text, Unicode/emoji/HTML
entities, relative/absolute URLs are all exercised across the
accumulated suite (Deep Scrape row-alignment tests specifically cover a
row where the detail fetch failed, keeping alignment correct with an
empty value rather than shifting). Malformed selectors: content/
scraper.js's extraction wraps each column read in its own try/catch —
one bad selector never aborts the whole row, and the popup surfaces the
resulting empty/anomalous cell rather than crashing (pre-existing,
re-verified). Pagination stop conditions (no next page, disabled next,
repeated/looping page signature, origin-changed, page-not-changed,
max-pages) are each an explicit, named stopReason in content/
pagination.js — re-read and confirmed no "accidental infinite loop"
path exists; every loop mode has its own max-pages/max-rows/max-scrolls
guard checked every pass.

---- 18-20. Structured data / template / transform engine audits ----
JSON-LD parsing wraps each block in its own try/catch (one malformed
block never aborts the others), never executes script content, handles
single objects/arrays/@graph. Template import (utils/templates.js) and
transform-pipeline sanitization (utils/transforms.js's
sanitizeTransformList) both rebuild untrusted input into a fixed-key
plain object — never `obj[untrustedKey] = value` — confirmed (again,
this audit) that a hostile __proto__/constructor/prototype-bearing
import cannot pollute Object.prototype (re-verified directly: importing
such a payload and then checking `Object.getPrototypeOf({}).polluted`
is undefined afterward). Every implemented transform is null/undefined/
empty-string-safe (re-confirmed by re-running the existing null-safety
test sweep across every op). Raw-value preservation (V1.23's own
architecture: rawRows is never mutated, only re-derived from) re-
confirmed intact.

---- 21-22. Results / large-dataset stress audit ----
Re-confirmed this session (originally established in V1.25, re-verified
here): 1,000/5,000-row datasets render with the DOM capped at
PREVIEW_LIMIT (30) rows regardless of dataset size, row-count text and
CSV export both reflect the full dataset. A 10,000-row synthetic
dataset run through a combined transform -> filter -> dedup -> sort
pipeline completes in well under a generous, non-flaky sanity ceiling
with exactly correct counts, zero duplicates, zero lost rows, and a
genuinely monotonic sort order. No 25,000+-row fixture exists (see
Known Limitations) — the same O(n)/O(n log n) algorithms apply
regardless of scale, no different/slower code path exists for a larger N.

---- 23-24. Storage / storage-quota-failure audit ----
Storage namespaces confirmed cleanly separated by key: ws_settings,
ws_state::<hostname> (working scraper config), ws_saved_scrapers,
ws_templates, ws_snapshots, ws_license, chrome.storage.SESSION for
ephemeral run state (ws_state::<hostname>-run/ws_zip_run/etc, never
chrome.storage.local) — a missing/corrupt key in any one of these
cannot affect any other, confirmed directly this audit by seeding 5
simultaneously-corrupted (wrong-typed) keys at once and confirming the
popup still boots and every panel still self-heals independently.
Storage-quota failure: snapshots.js proactively estimates byte size
against chrome.storage.local.QUOTA_BYTES BEFORE attempting a write,
returning a clear "Snapshot storage limit reached — delete some old
snapshots and try again" error rather than attempting and silently
losing data; recipes.js's persist() catches chrome.runtime.lastError on
every write and surfaces it as {ok:false, error} rather than reporting
false success. In-memory Results data remains fully usable (and
exportable) even if a save/snapshot write fails — a storage failure
never clears rawRows.

---- 25. Service-worker restart audit — honest limitation, re-confirmed
     ----
utils/runstate.js already persists in-progress pagination/Deep-Scrape
run state to chrome.storage.session specifically so a service-worker
restart can be recovered from — re-confirmed by code reading, unchanged
this audit. The ONE honest, unavoidable limitation (documented since
V1.13.2/V1.20, re-confirmed, not newly discovered): a ZIP run's already-
fetched-but-not-yet-finalized asset bytes (zipRunContexts) are in-memory
only and are lost if the service worker restarts mid-run — Chrome
provides no API to cheaply persist raw binary bytes across a worker
restart, so restarting that specific run is the correct, honest
behavior rather than a false guarantee.

---- 26-27. Snapshot / monitoring audit ----
Re-confirmed: snapshot retention (DEFAULT_RETENTION_PER_GROUP, pruned
per group on every save) and quota-checking are both already correct
and unchanged; monitoring.history is capped at MONITOR_HISTORY_LIMIT
(50 entries) per scraper, truncated on every write — bounded state
confirmed, no unlimited growth path exists. Actual chrome.notifications
DELIVERY (does a real OS notification actually appear) remains MANUAL
— this cannot be verified in JSDOM.

---- 28-31. CSV / XLSX / JSON / NDJSON export audits ----
CSV: commas/quotes/newlines/Unicode/empty cells re-confirmed correctly
escaped (RFC 4180); formula-injection mitigation (leading single-quote
for a value starting with =/+/-/@/tab/CR) re-confirmed via a fresh
end-to-end test through the real Export button this audit (not just the
isolated V1.24 unit test) — ordinary values, including negative numbers,
are confirmed completely untouched. XLSX: row/column count correctness,
Unicode, empty-value handling, and structurally-valid OOXML (direct XML
inspection of the generated sheet1.xml, confirmed well-formed with
correct freeze-pane/autoFilter/cols elements in schema-correct order)
all re-confirmed; actually opening the file in Excel/LibreOffice/Sheets
remains MANUAL. JSON: valid, parseable, correct data re-confirmed.
NDJSON: one valid JSON object per line, no enclosing array, re-confirmed
— genuinely implemented, not a stub.

---- 32-35. Image/file download / ZIP / Research Bundle audits ----
Re-confirmed: bounded concurrency (runWithConcurrency, default 4,
shared by Bulk Download/Research Bundle/Deep Scrape), collision-safe
filenames (name_2, name_3, case-insensitive), dedup (URL-normalized),
absolute/relative/invalid/duplicate URL handling, progress + failure-
summary reporting. ZIP writer (utils/zip.js) writes STORED (never
compressed) entries with correct local-file-header/central-directory
structure — verified this session by direct byte-level inspection of a
generated .xlsx (itself a ZIP) and finding a valid EOCD record with zero
trailing bytes. Research Bundle's manifest.csv/xlsx/json + dataset-
info.json (V1.24-extended metadata) all confirmed present, no silent
omission, partial-asset-download failure summary confirmed present.
Actual downloaded-.zip-file inspection in a real Downloads folder
remains MANUAL.

---- 36. Google Sheets audit ----
Re-confirmed (already true since V1.24, re-verified this session): no
fake credentials anywhere in the codebase; WSDestinations.
isGoogleSheetsConfigured() correctly returns false (no client ID is
hardcoded); the popup's own Sheets button checks availability BEFORE
attempting anything and shows a clear "not configured" status;
exportToGoogleSheets() itself would reject before any network call even
if called directly, bypassing the UI gate. Real integration remains
MANUAL / EXTERNAL CONFIGURATION REQUIRED — see utils/destinations.js's
own header comment for the exact remaining steps (a Google Cloud
project, the Sheets API, an OAuth Client ID registered against a
published extension ID, and manifest.json's own oauth2 key — none of
which can be added truthfully without real provisioning).

---- 37. Privacy audit — factual list of what CAN leave the browser ----
  1. The page the user is actively viewing: content scripts read its
     DOM (never navigate it elsewhere); nothing about this "leaves" the
     browser beyond what loading the page already did.
  2. Deep Scrape: a validate-only fetch() (method GET, credentials:
     'omit', body immediately discarded — status/content-type/final-URL
     only) followed, only on success, by a real (inactive) background
     tab loading ONE detail-page URL the user's OWN scrape configuration
     already produced (never a URL discovered by crawling further) —
     confirmed via direct code reading of background.js's own
     documented "two network round-trips per URL, deliberately" design.
  3. Bulk Download / Research Bundle assets: fetch() (credentials:
     'omit') for each image/file URL the user's OWN scrape produced.
  4. Monitoring: a real (inactive) background tab loads the ONE URL a
     Saved Scraper with Monitoring enabled was configured against, on
     the user-set schedule.
  5. chrome.notifications: a LOCAL OS notification only — no network
     call.
  6. Google Sheets: NEVER attempts a network call while unconfigured
     (verified directly, #36 above) — this build ships permanently in
     that state.
No analytics, telemetry, crash reporting, or tracking of any kind exists
anywhere in this codebase (grep-verified — no hardcoded external
endpoint besides the 4 legitimate ones above, no analytics SDK
reference, no beacon/pixel pattern). Every default operation (scraping,
transforming, exporting, saving, templating) is 100% local.

---- 38. Network audit ----
Exactly 2 real fetch() call sites in the entire codebase (background.js
lines ~897 and ~1298, both credentials:'omit', both serving the
legitimate product purposes in #37 above) — grep-verified, no other
fetch()/XMLHttpRequest call exists anywhere. No hardcoded third-party
API, no test/development endpoint, no localhost reference (grep-
verified project-wide).

---- 39. Secret audit ----
No API key, token, password, OAuth secret, or private key of any kind
found anywhere in the codebase (grep-verified). The single "credentials"
string match found while inspecting the packaged release ZIP is the
SAFE fetch() option `credentials: 'omit'` (explicitly OMITTING cookies/
session credentials from every outbound asset fetch — a positive
security property, not a leak) plus destinations.js's own documentation
prose explaining why no fake credential is hardcoded.

---- 40-41. XSS / prototype-pollution audits — re-verified via the REAL
     running popup this session ----
Scraped values containing <img src=x onerror=...>, <script>...</script>,
<svg onload=...>, and a bare javascript: URL string were all fed through
a real Preview run this audit: none executed (verified via a global
flag the payload would have set if it ran), all rendered as literal
text in the Results table (via textContent, confirmed — see #5-6 static
audit above), and all survived correctly into a real CSV export as
literal text rather than being silently stripped. A hostile Template
import containing __proto__/constructor/prototype keys was re-verified
to leave Object.prototype completely unpolluted afterward.

---- 42. Regex safety audit ----
An unterminated/invalid regex pattern typed into a live Regex Replace
transform, applied through the real Transform panel, produces a clean,
visible error message (never an uncaught exception) — verified directly
this audit. JavaScript's own regex engine has no built-in catastrophic-
backtracking guard; this is an inherent language-level limitation, not
something client-side code can fully eliminate — documented honestly
rather than claiming otherwise (utils/transforms.js's own
MAX_REGEX_INPUT_LENGTH cap bounds the WORST-CASE input size a regex op
will attempt against, which is the practical mitigation available).

---- 43. URL safety audit ----
javascript:, mailto:, tel:, blob:, and vbscript: schemes are all
correctly REJECTED by WSDownloads.validateDownloadUrl (re-verified
directly this audit); http/https/well-formed data: URLs are correctly
accepted. Nothing in this codebase ever navigates a tab or fetches a
resource using a scheme this validator would reject — every download/
fetch path routes through it first.

---- 44. Download filename safety ----
Re-confirmed (V1.24's own dedicated test, plus this audit's own fresh
check): "../../etc/passwd"-style path-traversal input, reserved
Windows characters, and empty-string filenames are all neutralized by
the existing sanitizeFilename/sanitizeFolderName (unchanged since V1.5)
— no path separator of any kind can survive into a final filename.

---- 45. Import security audit ----
Every import mechanism in the codebase (Template JSON import, the
transform pipeline riding inside it) rebuilds the untrusted input into
a fixed-key plain object field-by-field — never eval, never a dynamic
Function constructor, never innerHTML of imported content, never a raw
filesystem path from imported data. Re-confirmed this audit (#41 above).

---- 46. CSP audit ----
See #4 above — no custom CSP is declared, so Chrome's strictest MV3
default applies. No unsafe-eval anywhere.

---- 47-48. Trial / license audit — the business model, verified exactly
     as specified ----
10 free completed scrape operations, then a one-time unlock — UNCHANGED,
re-confirmed. Verified directly, end-to-end, this audit: a normal
completed scrape = exactly 1 credit; pagination/Auto-Scroll/Load-More
= one run = one credit regardless of page count (charged once on
completion, never per page — confirmed by code reading of
maybeChargeForCompletedRun, called once per terminal run state, and by
the pre-existing V1.19/V1.20 pagination tests which already verify
this); Deep Scrape = no separate/additional charge (it merges into the
SAME scrape run that's already being charged once); retry = no
duplicate charge (WSLicense.consumeRunCredit's chargedRunIds ledger,
serialized against races); pause/resume = no duplicate charge (same
mechanism); export/ZIP/template/snapshot = never charge anything
(verified end-to-end this session across CSV/XLSX/JSON/NDJSON/Copy/
Save-as-Template/Save-Snapshot/Save-Scraper in one combined workflow,
exactly 1 credit charged total). runId idempotency re-confirmed
directly (calling consumeRunCredit twice with the identical runId only
charges once). The one GENUINE gap this audit found and fixed: a rapid
UI double-click could generate two DISTINCT runIds and charge twice
(see #9 above) — fixed. No UI-only check was found gating a background
charge (the charge always happens in the popup's own JS after a real
extraction/run genuinely completes, backed by the serialized,
idempotent utils/license.js — there is no separate "UI thinks it's
unlocked" state that a background check could disagree with, since
there is no background license check at all in this V1.15/V1.16
architecture by design). No invasive DRM was added or considered.

---- 49. Migration matrix ----
7 distinct representative storage-shape generations were constructed
and verified this audit (test-v1final-audit.js TEST 2): early V1
(V1.1-V1.2, bare minimum shape), mid V1 (V1.5-V1.9, has downloads/
dedupe, no run-mode/compare/transform), pre-V1.20 (has mode/pagination,
no compareKey/transforms/deepScrape/monitoring), V1.20+ (has
compareKey/transforms/deepScrape), V1.23+ (transforms populated),
V1.24 (research/export prefs present), and V1.25 (fully current shape)
— every one loads without error and successfully completes a Preview
run.

A completely empty chrome.storage (true fresh install) boots with no

WRONG-TYPED storage values (a string where an object was expected, null

ws_license/ws_templates/ws_snapshots all at once) do not crash the boot

lists all 8 built-ins despite a non-array ws_templates.templates value)
— verified directly this audit.

---- 52-58. UI final walkthrough / empty states / error+success messages

---- 59-63. Browser API error / event listener / timer / MutationObserver

All re-confirmed via #5-8 above and direct code reading: chrome.runtime.

the code level.

---- Known limitations (single authoritative list — supersedes any
     older, narrower "known limitations" note in an earlier version's
     own section above) ----
  Browser/platform limitations (not fixable client-side):
    - A Chrome MV3 service worker can be suspended/restarted at any
      time; an in-progress ZIP run's already-fetched-but-not-yet-
      finalized asset bytes are in-memory only and are lost on a
      restart mid-run (the run must be restarted) — Chrome provides no
      cheap way to persist raw binary bytes across a worker restart.
    - JavaScript's regex engine has no built-in catastrophic-
      backtracking guard; MAX_REGEX_INPUT_LENGTH bounds the practical
      blast radius but cannot eliminate the underlying language
      limitation.
    - Real Excel/LibreOffice/Google Sheets file-opening behavior,
      real chrome.notifications delivery, and real chrome.downloads
      file-system output cannot be verified without a real Chrome
      browser — see the Manual Test Plan below.
  Site-specific limitations (inherent to scraping, not a defect):
    - Extraction accuracy depends entirely on each target site's own
      DOM structure/selectors — no feature in this product works
      identically on "all websites" (see next section).
    - A site that blocks non-interactive/background tab loads, uses
      aggressive anti-bot measures, or requires authentication behind a
      login wall is outside this product's scope (no proxy/anti-bot
      bypass exists, by design — V2.0/out-of-scope territory).
  Intentional product limitations (by design, documented, not a gap):
    - No recursive crawler — Deep Scrape follows exactly one additional
      link depth, only URLs the list scrape itself already produced.
    - No XPath — CSS selectors only.
    - No V2.x analysis (charts/averages/medians/correlations/AI
      insights/recommendations) — V1.x is raw data collection only.
  External configuration requirements (feature exists, needs setup this
  repository doesn't have):
    - Google Sheets destination — see utils/destinations.js's own header
      comment for the exact remaining steps (Google Cloud project,
      Sheets API, OAuth Client ID against a published extension ID).
    - Real payment/checkout — see V1.16's own commercial release
      checklist; the trial/license STATE MODEL is real and complete,
      the payment PROVIDER integration is not connected.
  No dedicated 25,000+-row fixture exists (1k/5k/10k are covered) —
  expected to degrade linearly, chrome.storage.local's own quota is the
  realistic ceiling for long-term persistence, already surfaced clearly
  rather than silently losing data.

---- Do-not-over-promise note (spec #74) ----
This product's own documentation (this file, the manifest description,
and its in-UI copy) deliberately never claims universal "works on all
websites" behavior. Scraping is inherently site-dependent — Auto Detect,
pagination detection, and structured-data extraction all describe their
own bounded, conservative behavior (explicit caps, graceful fallbacks,
"unmatched"/"skipped" states) rather than a blanket guarantee.

See the Final Report delivered alongside this documentation update for
the complete, ordered manual Chrome test checklist, PASS criteria per
test, and what failure evidence to capture — reproduced here only in
summary to keep this file from duplicating it verbatim: (A) e-commerce
product listing, (B) search results, (C) article/news list, (D)
directory/listing, (E) table page, (F) pagination site, (G) infinite-
scroll site, (H) Load More site, (I) list->detail Deep Scrape site, (J)
a structured-data-rich product/article page; plus a dedicated service-
worker-restart procedure and a dedicated downloaded-file /
external-app-compatibility (Excel/LibreOffice/Sheets) checklist.

V1 FINAL — REAL CHROME BUG FIX + INTERNATIONALIZATION + TRIAL/PAYWALL QA

---- V1 FINAL — Manual Chrome Validation: BUG #1 ----
SYMPTOM (reported from a real Chrome install, not simulated): the popup
header showed "10 runs left" at the exact same moment the "Free trial
complete" modal was open ("You've used your 10 free scraping runs.") —
logically impossible, since the header and the paywall must always
agree. Separately, clicking "Maybe Later" did nothing: the modal stayed
open and blocked the popup.

ROOT CAUSE (two independent, provable defects, not patched around):
1. Trial-state race / disagreeing fallbacks. popup.js's
   `currentLicenseState` starts as `null` and is only populated once
   init()'s own `loadLicenseState()` call resolves. If a gated action
   (Preview / Start Run / Deep Scrape Start) was ever evaluated before
   that resolved, the OLD code took two DIFFERENT, WRONG readings of the
   same "not yet loaded" (null) state: `applyLicenseUI()` had
   `if (!currentLicenseState) return;` — leaving the header's STATIC
   HTML PLACEHOLDER text ("10 runs left") untouched — while
   `WSLicense.hasRunsRemaining(null)` evaluates to `false`, incorrectly
   treating "not yet loaded" as "zero remaining" and opening the
   paywall. Two different fallback behaviors for the identical
   missing-state case is exactly how a stale "10 runs left" header and
   an open "trial complete" modal could coexist.
2. CSS cascade bug behind "Maybe Later does nothing". popup.css's
   `.ws-modal-overlay { display: flex; ... }` is an author-origin class
   rule, which ALWAYS overrides the browser's native
   `[hidden] { display: none; }` user-agent rule per CSS cascade origin
   ordering (author beats UA regardless of selector specificity, absent
   `!important`). `hideTrialModal()`'s `el.hidden = true` was always
   setting the HTML attribute correctly but never actually hiding the
   modal visually in any real browser — this bug has existed since the
   modal was first built (V1.15) and only surfaced now because this was
   the first real-Chrome manual test of trial exhaustion in the
   project's history (every prior verification was jsdom-based, and
   every other jsdom test file in this project strips CSS entirely, so
   none of them could have caught a pure-cascade defect like this).

THE FIX (utils/license.js untouched — both defects were in popup.js and
popup.css):
1. `trialAllowsNewRun()` (popup/popup.js) is now async and
   self-healing: if `currentLicenseState` is missing, it loads it fresh
   from storage AND refreshes the header via `applyLicenseUI()` in that
   exact same step, before ever returning a gating decision. There is no
   longer any code path where the gating decision and the header can be
   based on two different snapshots of state.
2. popup.css gets a companion rule, `.ws-modal-overlay[hidden] {
   display: none; }`, mirroring the pre-existing
   `.ws-tab-panel[hidden] { display: none; }` convention already used
   elsewhere in this file for exactly this class of author-rule-vs-UA-
   default conflict.
3. Defense in depth: the trial modal's "Maybe Later" and overlay-click
   dismiss listeners are now wired at the very TOP of init(), before any
   `await`, so they are structurally guaranteed to attach even if some
   later, unrelated step in init()'s long sequential body throws.

FILES CHANGED: popup/popup.js (async `trialAllowsNewRun()`, its 3 call
sites, dismiss-listener wiring moved to the top of init()),
popup/popup.css (`.ws-modal-overlay[hidden]` rule).

TESTS: test-v1final-bug1-trial-contradiction.js (NEW) — the only test
file in this project that injects the REAL popup.css inline so
getComputedStyle reflects the genuine cascade, not just DOM/JS state.
46 assertions, 0 failures: the CSS fix in isolation; the header/paywall
NEVER disagree across completedRuns 0-12 (including while the modal is
open, the header simultaneously shows the correct number, never a
stale "10 runs left"); the header stays correct even when Preview is
clicked immediately after boot (racing init()'s own state load); 4
repeated open/dismiss/open/dismiss cycles all work with no listener
multiplication; at trialRunsUsed=10, Maybe Later closes the modal
without resetting the counter, and the very next scrape attempt shows
the paywall again.

STATUS: CODE-FIXED / AWAITING REAL CHROME RETEST. Not marked as
manually verified — see the Manual QA Procedure in the accompanying
Final Report for the exact real-Chrome retest steps.

---- V1 Internationalization ----
ARCHITECTURE: a centralized internal translation module
(utils/i18n.js + utils/i18n-data.js), NOT Chrome's native
`_locales/messages.json` + chrome.i18n API. Reason: chrome.i18n is
locked to the browser's own UI language for the life of the extension
context — there is no supported way to let a user pick a language
inside the popup and see it change immediately, which the spec
explicitly requires. utils/i18n.js is the small engine (lookup,
interpolation, simple singular/plural key pairs, DOM re-render,
coverage audit); utils/i18n-data.js holds six plain, static JS object
literals (en/tr/de/fr/zh-CN/ru) — no eval, no new Function, no fetch,
no remote dependency.

SUPPORTED LOCALES: English (canonical fallback), Turkish, German,
French, Simplified Chinese, Russian. Native names are always shown
un-translated in the language picker (English/Türkçe/Deutsch/
Français/简体中文/Русский).

BROWSER DETECTION & PERSISTENCE: on a session where no explicit choice
has ever been made, `chrome.i18n.getUILanguage()` is read once
(synchronous, permission-free) and normalized down to one of the 6
supported codes (unrecognized locales, e.g. Traditional Chinese, safely
fall back to English). The moment a user picks a language from the
Settings dropdown, that explicit choice is persisted and ALWAYS wins
over browser detection from then on, until they change it again or
explicitly select "Auto (follow browser)". Persistence reuses the
EXISTING `ws_settings` storage key/schema (utils/settings.js) — a new
`language` field alongside the pre-existing researchFormatDefaults/
exportPreferences, not a new storage key. Missing/unrecognized values
self-heal to `'auto'` on load, matching this project's established
defensive-load convention everywhere else.

FALLBACK: any lookup for a key missing from the active locale falls
back to English; a key missing from EVERY locale (a typo) falls back to
returning the raw key itself — the UI can never show `undefined`,
`null`, or a blank string.

COVERAGE (V1 scope): 181 translation keys, 100% translated in all 6
locales (utils/i18n.js's `coverageReport()`, wired into
scripts/release-check.js, fails the release if this ever drops below
100%). This covers the complete primary user journey: all 4 tabs, the
Scrape workflow (Saved Scrapers/Columns/Run Mode/Run Status/Preview/
Reset), Results (top-level Data/Export/Assets/Change-Tracking/Deep-
Scraping toolbars and their primary buttons), Monitor, Research, the
complete Settings screen, and the complete trial paywall (exact given
strings for English/Turkish; professional, natural — not literal —
translations for German/French/Simplified Chinese/Russian). Pricing
("$5") is never translated or currency-converted in any locale.
Widely-recognized technical terms (CSS, XPath, JSON, JSON-LD, CSV,
XLSX, URL, Deep Scrape) are preserved rather than awkwardly translated.

INTENTIONAL EXCEPTIONS (documented, not oversights — spec #45/#46
explicitly allow this): the individual Transform operation names/option
lists (Trim, Regex Replace, Normalize Date, ...) and their detailed
sub-field forms, the Auto Detect/Structured Data/Deep Scrape field-
configuration sub-panels, and the Export/Download options-panel
internals (filename token hints, advanced-panel `<summary>` labels)
ship English-only for V1 — these are secondary, advanced configuration
surfaces reached only after the primary flow, they lean heavily on
already-preserved technical terms, and they fall back to English
cleanly and safely exactly as designed. Dev/QA-only UI (the isDevelop-
mentInstall()-gated switcher covered below) is deliberately never
translated — it is not user-facing production copy.

NEVER TRANSLATED: scraped page content, product names/descriptions/
prices/URLs, column values, HTML attributes, structured-data values,
exported data, user-created scraper/template names, or user-entered
column names — i18n applies ONLY to extension-owned interface text.

INTERPOLATION/PLURALS: template strings use `{count}`/`{remaining}`/
`{total}`-style placeholders (e.g. "{count} runs left" /
"Vous avez utilisé vos {count} extractions gratuites.") — no translated
sentence is ever built by concatenating English fragments. A simple
`_one`/`_other` two-form convention covers V1's small pluralization
surface (run counts, row counts) across all 6 locales.

ACCESSIBILITY & SECURITY: `document.documentElement`'s `lang` attribute
is kept in sync with the active language; button titles/aria-labels
translate alongside their visible text. Translation values are static
local data only — no eval, no innerHTML injection, no remote translation
service.

PERFORMANCE: switching languages calls `WSI18n.applyToDom()` — a pure
DOM relabel of `[data-i18n]`/`[data-i18n-title]`/`[data-i18n-aria-
label]`/`[data-i18n-placeholder]` nodes plus a couple of JS-driven
strings (header badge, Settings license summary, the paywall) — it
never reloads scrape datasets, restarts a run, mutates results, or
touches trial/license state.

MIGRATION: an existing V1.25 user's `ws_settings` record has no
`language` field; `WSSettings.load()` defaults it to `'auto'` (follow
browser) without touching researchFormatDefaults/exportPreferences —
existing users are never reset to any particular language, and adding
localization never touches trial history, scraper configs, templates,
results, or license state.

UNICODE: all six catalog files are UTF-8; Turkish (ç ğ ı İ ö ş ü),
German (ä ö ü ß), French (é è ê ë à ç œ), Simplified Chinese
characters, and Cyrillic all render correctly with no mojibake
(verified via the automated test suite below, which asserts exact
translated strings byte-for-byte).

TESTS: test-v1final-partbc-i18n-qa.js (NEW, shared with the QA harness
tests below) covers: fresh install with no browser-language signal
defaults to English; fresh install correctly detects a supported
browser language (Turkish); an explicit language selection always wins
over browser detection AND survives a simulated popup reopen even
though the browser still reports a different language; the full
en→tr→de→fr→zh-CN→ru→en switch matrix (spot-checking one translated
string per language) with trial state and scraper column config
provably untouched throughout; a totally unknown key falls back to
itself, never blank/undefined; a key deliberately deleted from one
locale's table falls back to English; the trial+localization
interaction (French: correct singular "1 run left" phrasing at 1
remaining, no premature paywall, full French paywall text — title/body
with correctly-interpolated count/Maybe-Later label — at 0 remaining,
Maybe Later closing it without touching trialRunsUsed, and the paywall
correctly reappearing on the next attempt).

---- V1 QA Trial Testing ----
GOAL: let a developer manually exercise the real 10-free-runs → $5-
paywall flow repeatedly in real Chrome, in seconds, without corrupting
real trial state and — critically — without exposing any reset/unlock
capability to ordinary production users.

HOW IT WORKS: extends the EXISTING V1.16 "dev license switcher"
pattern rather than inventing a second, parallel mechanism.
utils/license.js already had 3 DEV ONLY functions
(`activateDevLicense`/`resetToTrialDev`/`simulateRevokedDev`), each
explicitly documented as requiring its caller to gate access behind
`isDevelopmentInstall()` — this pass adds exactly one more,
`setTrialRunsUsedDev(n)`, following the identical contract (clamps `n`
into [0, 10], forces `licenseStatus` back to `'trial'`, clears the
charged-run ledger so a stale entry can never suppress the next gated
click). popup.html's pre-existing `#settings-dev-switcher` container —
`hidden` by default, only ever unhidden by popup.js after an async
`WSLicense.isDevelopmentInstall()` check (`chrome.management.getSelf()`
reporting `installType === 'development'`) resolves true — now also
contains 6 small QA buttons: STATE A (0 used/10 left), STATE B (1
used/9 left), STATE C (9 used/1 left), STATE D (10 used/0 left), STATE
E (simulated PAYMENT_SUCCESS → unlocked, reusing the existing
`activateDevLicense()` exactly), and "Reset QA Trial State" (reusing
the existing `resetToTrialDev()` exactly).

ISOLATION FROM PRODUCTION: `installType` is `'development'` ONLY for
an unpacked/sideloaded extension loaded via Chrome's Developer Mode —
it can never be `'development'` for a real Chrome Web Store install,
regardless of anything shipped inside the package. No ordinary
production Settings control, keyboard shortcut, or menu exposes
trial-reset/force-unlock — the ONLY path to any of these functions is
through this one gated container. scripts/release-check.js now has a
dedicated static check ("QA trial-state/force-unlock controls are not
reachable outside the isDevelopmentInstall()-gated container") that
FAILS the release if any of the 6 QA control ids, the pre-existing
3-way dev-license radio group, or any of their handler functions
(`handleQaSetTrialRunsUsed`/`handleQaSimulateUnlock`/
`handleQaResetTrialState`/`handleDevLicenseSwitch`) is ever found
outside that gated container, or if any DEV ONLY `utils/license.js`
function is ever referenced from background.js or a content script.
test-v1final-partbc-i18n-qa.js additionally verifies at runtime: with
no `chrome.management` at all, the switcher stays hidden; with
`chrome.management` present but `installType: 'normal'` (a real
install), it stays hidden; only with `installType: 'development'` does
it appear, and each of the 6 QA buttons then does exactly what it
says (verified against the header badge and the raw storage record).

HOW TO SIMULATE PAYMENT SUCCESS FOR QA: click "E: Unlocked (QA)" — it
calls the exact same `activateDevLicense()` the pre-existing Licensed
(DEV) radio already used, just exposed as a second, more discoverable
entry point next to the other QA buttons. This is explicitly NOT the
real payment system (see below) and is only ever reachable through the
same isDevelopmentInstall()-gated UI as everything else on this list.

WHAT "UNLOCK UNLIMITED — $5" ACTUALLY DOES TODAY (production build):
`WSLicense.activateLicense()` is a documented stub —
`CHECKOUT_URL`/`LICENSE_API_BASE_URL` are both `null` — that always
resolves `{ ok: false, reason: 'not-configured' }`. Clicking the button
in a real production install shows an honest status message
("Purchase is not available yet in this build.") and does NOT mark the
user unlocked. PAYMENT INTEGRATION IS NOT IMPLEMENTED. This was true
before this pass and remains true after it — nothing in this pass
changes that, and nothing in this pass pretends otherwise.

============================================================
V1 WORKFLOW / UX REORGANIZATION
(reorganize the EXISTING functionality around a clear workflow — not a
rewrite; no engine changes, no removed features, no new version line)
============================================================

---- Problem discovered during real manual testing ----
The extension wasn't confusing because it lacked features — it has all
of them. It was confusing because features were exposed in the wrong
order, and the relationship between Scrape / Results / Monitor /
Research / Auto Detect / Preview / Run / Export was never made
explicit. A first-time user had no way to know "what do I click first,
and what happens after."

---- Old workflow ----
Scrape tab opened directly into the full Manual toolset (Saved
Scrapers, Columns, Auto Detect, Structured Data, Templates, Run Mode,
Preview, Reset) with no entry-level path for a user who just wants
data. Monitor and Research were two more top-level tabs, presented as
co-equal destinations with no visible connection to "the results I just
got."

---- New workflow ----
    1. COLLECT DATA   (Scrape tab: AUTO | MANUAL)
    2. REVIEW RESULTS (Results tab: table + Data tools, unchanged)
    3. NEXT ACTION     (Results tab: "What would you like to do next?"
                         -> Export Data / Monitor Changes / Create
                         Research Package)
AUTO is the new default entry point: Scan Page -> review detected
fields -> Extract Data -> Results, with zero selector/attribute/
Preview/Run vocabulary exposed. MANUAL is the complete pre-existing
toolset, one tap away, fully unchanged.

---- AUTO vs MANUAL architecture ----
Both modes are a PRESENTATION SWITCH over the exact same pipeline —
there is no second scraping engine:
  - "Scan Page" calls the SAME RUN_AUTO_DETECT content-script message
    Manual Mode's own Auto Detect panel uses, preferring a genuine
    repeating DOM structure (itemCount >= 2 — the common listing/
    search-results/table/directory case) and falling back to the SAME
    SCAN_STRUCTURED_DATA message (single-record product/article detail
    pages) only when no strong structure was found. A field is
    unchecked by default only when the detector's OWN metadata already
    flags it unreliable (Fragile quality, or <50% coverage) — never a
    hardcoded field-name list, so this works for e-commerce, search
    results, directories, article lists, and tables alike.
  - "Extract Data" applies the checked fields as an ordinary
    state.columns configuration (identical shape to what "Use Selected
    Fields"/"Add Selected Fields" already produce) and then calls the
    EXACT SAME handlePreview() Manual Mode's Preview button calls — same
    trial gate (trialAllowsNewRun()), same idempotent
    chargeRunCredit(), same RUN_EXTRACTION content-script call, same
    results pipeline, same double-click protection
    (runTriggerInFlight). This is what guarantees AUTO and MANUAL share
    ONE trial counter and cannot diverge.
  - Scanning/detecting NEVER calls chargeRunCredit — only a completed
    Extract Data (or Manual Preview/Start Run) does, exactly matching
    the pre-existing, unchanged business rule.
  - The mode itself is a small persisted preference (WSSettings'
    existing `ws_settings` key, new `scrapeMode` field — 'auto' by
    default) that an explicit switch always overrides going forward,
    same pattern as the V1 FINAL language selector. If the current
    host already has a loaded saved scraper or existing manual columns,
    the popup opens directly into MANUAL for that session (spec #11)
    without overwriting the stored default.

---- Reused components (nothing duplicated) ----
RUN_AUTO_DETECT / SCAN_STRUCTURED_DATA / PREVIEW_STRUCTURE / RUN_EXTRACTION
content-script messages; handlePreview()/trialAllowsNewRun()/
chargeRunCredit(); WSStorage.makeColumnId()/persistState()/renderColumns();
switchTab(); toggleExportOptionsPanel() (Export Data); handleOpenResearchPanel()
(Create Research Package — already operates on the CURRENT rawRows, no
saved-scraper requirement); handleSaveScraper() (Monitor Changes,
save-if-not-already-saved step, same name-prompt UX as the existing
"💾 Save as New Scraper" button); renderMonitoringSection() (Monitor
Changes card lookup, via a new `data-scraper-id` attribute on each
existing card).

---- Files changed ----
popup/popup.html (AUTO/MANUAL mode switch + #auto-mode-panel; existing
Scrape-tab content wrapped, unmodified, in #manual-mode-content; Results
"Next Actions" block, reusing the existing results-go-research-btn/
results-go-monitor-btn ids plus a new results-export-data-btn; "Back to
Results" links on Monitor/Research); popup/popup.js (scrapeMode state +
decideInitialScrapeMode()/setScrapeModeUi(); handleScanPage()/
handleAutoExtract() and their rendering helpers; handleResultsGoMonitor()/
handleResultsGoResearch()/handleResultsExportData()/focusMonitoringCard();
data-scraper-id added to monitoring cards); popup/popup.css (.ws-mode-switch/
.ws-mode-btn/.ws-next-actions/.ws-scraper-row-highlight); utils/settings.js
(new `scrapeMode` field, same self-healing pattern as `language`);
utils/i18n-data.js (16 new keys x 6 locales — mode.auto/mode.manual/
autoMode.*/results.nextActionsTitle/results.exportData/
results.monitorChanges/results.createResearchPackage/
results.backToResults — 197 total keys, still 100% coverage in all 6
languages).

---- Tests added ----
test-v1-workflow-reorg.js (NEW) — 65 assertions, 0 failures (verified
stable across repeated runs): AUTO is the default for a fresh install;
MANUAL exposes every pre-existing advanced control unchanged, still
inside manual-mode-content; Scan Page never charges a trial credit;
detected fields are listed in full with low-confidence ones unchecked
by DETECTOR metadata (never a hardcoded name); Extract Data charges
exactly 1 credit and auto-routes to Results with the extracted row
visible; the structured-data fallback path works and correctly shows
the "review before extracting" note; a page where NEITHER signal finds
anything never fabricates a result; an existing saved scraper still
loads its columns correctly and opens directly into MANUAL; existing
Manual Preview/extraction still works and still charges correctly;
Monitor Changes saves an ad-hoc AUTO result first (existing Save
Scraper prompt) then switches tabs and highlights the right card;
Create Research Package opens the research panel pre-populated from
current results; Export Data reveals the existing Export Options panel;
all 6 languages render the new workflow strings correctly (no mixed-
language UI); the 10th run (Manual) and an attempted 11th run (AUTO)
share exactly one trial counter, correctly allowed/blocked; QA
trial-only controls remain hidden with no chrome.management, unchanged
by the reorg.

---- Total tests / assertions / failures ----
Full accumulated suite: 81 files, ~2,475-2,520 assertions (varies run-
to-run — see Known Limitations), 0 assertion failures on the cleanest
runs. release-check.js: 16/16 checks pass (includes the pre-existing
QA-bypass and 100%-translation-coverage checks — the reorg introduces
no new production QA/dev bypass and the new translation keys are fully
covered).

---- Bugs discovered/fixed ----
None in the pre-existing engine/trial/i18n code — this was a
presentation-layer reorganization. One self-caused issue was found and
fixed DURING implementation, not shipped: an early draft sequenced
WSI18n.init() before chrome.tabs.query() in init(), adding real
(if small) latency to every popup boot and destabilizing several
already-flush()-budget-marginal older test files; fixed by running them
concurrently (Promise, not sequential await) — see V1 FINAL's own i18n
section for why this ordering matters at all.

---- Known limitations ----
- AUTO mode is scoped to the "Current Page" extraction case (the
  overwhelming majority of listing/search/directory/table/detail
  pages). It does not attempt to auto-configure pagination, Load More,
  infinite scroll, or Deep Scraping — those remain Manual-only,
  reachable one tap away via "Need more control? Switch to Manual".
  Auto-detecting and safely auto-configuring multi-page navigation
  without user confirmation was judged too risky for this pass (spec's
  own "smallest safe change" instruction) and is a reasonable candidate
  for a future, dedicated pass — not silently promised here.
- "Monitor Changes" from an unsaved AUTO result triggers the EXISTING
  save-scraper name prompt (a native window.prompt) rather than a
  custom in-UI dialog — reused deliberately rather than building a
  second save-a-scraper UI.
- The pre-existing, previously-documented jsdom test-suite timing
  flakiness (fixed-millisecond flush() budgets in ~5-6 older test
  files, sensitive to system scheduling load when 80+ Node processes
  run back-to-back) persists and is unrelated to this reorg — verified
  by hardcoding scrapeMode to 'manual' and reproducing the identical
  flakiness rate, proving it is not caused by the new AUTO/MANUAL
  panel-hiding logic. This is an environmental test-harness
  characteristic, not a product defect; every individual assertion
  passes reliably when isolated with adequate flush time.
- Auto Detect's underlying detection QUALITY (confidence scoring,
  field-name inference, coverage thresholds) is unchanged by this pass
  — "Scan Page" is a new entry point onto the EXISTING detector, not a
  detection-quality improvement. Real-Chrome validation of detection
  accuracy on live sites is still required (see the checklist below and
  V1 FINAL's own "AWAITING REAL CHROME RETEST" status, still unresolved).

---- Manual Chrome tests still required ----
See the Final Report delivered alongside this update for the complete
checklist. Nothing in this section — nor anywhere else in this pass —
is marked as manually verified in real Chrome.

============================================================
V1 AUTO DETECTION IMPROVEMENT
(detection QUALITY only — the AUTO/MANUAL workflow itself, built and
approved in the previous pass, is untouched)
============================================================

---- Real Chrome failure observed ----
On an eBay product listing page, AUTO -> Scan Page technically worked
(no crash, no timeout) but surfaced poor/secondary fields — Brand,
Type, Color, Features, Connectivity, Text 6, Text 7, Text 8 — instead
of the actual listing a human would want: Product title, Price,
Product URL, Image, Rating, Review count.

---- Root cause ----
Two independent, provable defects in content/autodetect.js's candidate
scoring, both generic (neither is eBay-specific):
1. The page ALSO contains an "item specifics" panel — a repeated
   label:value specification table (Brand/Type/Color/Connectivity/
   Features/Model), structurally a repeating group of short, link-free,
   image-free rows. The scorer's dominant signal was "card-to-card text
   consistency" (12 of ~70 max points, no counterweight) — and a
   label:value spec table is EXTREMELY consistent in text length
   (that's what makes it look tidy to a human), scoring it competitively
   against, or above, the real product cards, whose titles genuinely
   vary in length card to card. There was no signal at all measuring
   "does this repeated group actually look like a list of items" (price-
   like content, heading-like titles, link-target diversity) and no
   penalty for "this looks like a spec/attribute table" (no links, no
   images, tight consistency).
2. A pre-existing case-sensitivity bug in popup.js: the "confidence is
   low, review before extracting" note compared
   `candidate.confidence === 'low'` against content/autodetect.js's own
   `scoreToConfidence()`, which actually returns 'High'/'Medium'/'Low'
   (capitalized) — the comparison could never match a real DOM
   structure's confidence, silently defeating spec #10's "never pretend
   certainty" requirement for the one case it was supposed to catch.
   Fixed alongside the ranking work (case-insensitive comparison).
Separately (not a root cause of the reported bug, but discovered while
building the regression fixtures for it): a common real card pattern —
the thumbnail nested INSIDE the same `<a>` as the title
(`<a><img/><h3>Title</h3></a>`) — was invisible to field detection
entirely, because anchors are deliberately never descended into (an
anchor is treated as one text/href unit, so real cards structured this
way never got an Image field proposed at all, regardless of ranking).

---- Candidate dataset ranking approach ----
content/autodetect.js's scoreCandidate() (used identically for every
repeating group AND every table row-group — no separate code path) now
also measures, per candidate:
  - price-like content ratio (+) — spec #2's strongest positive
    e-commerce/listing signal, generic regex across 7 currency symbols,
    no per-site logic.
  - heading-tag ("h1"-"h6") ratio (+) — spec's "repeated titles/
    headings" signal.
  - link-target DIVERSITY (+, only counted once links already exist) —
    spec #3's "avoid... all links point to the same page" negative
    signal, implemented as its positive inverse.
  - an explicit "looks like an attribute/spec list" penalty (-35): no
    links AND no images AND unusually tight text consistency AND a
    modest item count, ALL together — this is the direct fix for the
    reported failure. A real product-card candidate is unaffected as
    long as it has EITHER links or images, which real item cards from
    any site essentially always do.
  - the pure text-consistency weight was reduced (was the single
    largest signal) so it can no longer, alone, outrank genuine
    link/image/price-bearing candidates.
Every signal above is measured from the DOM/text itself — nothing is
keyed off class names, ids, or any eBay-specific marker.

---- Semantic field naming ----
- The old generic-text fallback name ("Text", producing "Text 6"/
  "Text 7"/"Text 8" for multiple unclassifiable fields on the same
  structure) is now "Field" ("Field 6"/"Field 7"/"Field 8") — spec #4's
  own suggested neutral name; still never invents meaning, just reads
  as an honest "we don't know what this is" instead of looking broken.
- "Review Count" is now distinguished from the generic "Count" guess
  when the sample text itself contains "review"/"rating" wording (e.g.
  "128 reviews", "(1,234 ratings)") — still entirely evidence-driven,
  never guessed from a field's position.
- Fixed a regex bug (open/close parenthesis position) that had
  previously prevented "(N reviews)"-style parenthesized counts from
  matching the count pattern at all.

---- Repeated value quality (spec #5) ----
Each field candidate now also carries a `uniqueness` measurement —
distinct non-empty sample values / total non-empty samples, computed
across the full sampled row set (not just the handful kept for
display). A field whose value is essentially identical on every row
(a repeated badge/boilerplate label, not real per-item data) now: (a)
ranks below an equal-coverage field that actually varies, and (b) is
excluded from AUTO mode's default-checked preselection when uniqueness
is very low (<0.2) across 3+ sampled rows — spec #12's "do not
preselect junk fields merely to show more data," now driven by
evidence instead of only coverage/selector-fragility.

---- Row-root alignment (spec #6) ----
Architecturally unaffected and was never actually the bug: every field
proposed for a structure is, and always was, extracted relative to that
ONE structure's own container instances — cross-region mixing (e.g. a
product title paired with a sidebar's Brand/Type values) is structurally
impossible in this pipeline. The reported failure was the ranking
choosing the WRONG single structure, not two structures' fields being
combined. Verified directly: TEST 20 re-extracts through the real,
unmodified scraper engine and confirms every row of a chosen structure
comes from that structure alone.

---- Structured data (spec #7) — unchanged, confirmed correct ----
The AUTO workflow built in the previous pass already only falls back to
SCAN_STRUCTURED_DATA when RUN_AUTO_DETECT finds no strong repeating DOM
structure (itemCount < 2) — page-level structured data was already
never allowed to "hijack" a genuine listing dataset. No change needed
here; re-verified by TEST 17.B (product detail page).

---- Confidence / candidate-choice behavior (spec #3/#10/#11) ----
- A DOM structure only counts as viable at all when it both repeats
  (itemCount >= 2) AND clears a low score floor (>=20) — a low-score
  fluke match is now named honestly ("we couldn't confidently detect a
  useful dataset") instead of being silently offered as real data.
- When the top TWO candidates are a genuinely close call (score gap
  <=15), AUTO mode's Scan Page now shows a small "Detected data:"
  picker (defaulting to the strongest candidate) instead of silently
  guessing — new popup.html/popup.js addition, hidden entirely on an
  ordinary, unambiguous page (the ordinary "one click" case gets nothing
  extra to read).

---- Files changed ----
content/autodetect.js (scoreCandidate() new signals + attribute-list
penalty; guessSemanticName()/COUNT_RE Review-Count fix; assignFieldName()
"Field" fallback; buildFieldCandidate()/detectFields() uniqueness
measurement + ranking; image-inside-anchor detection); popup/popup.js
(fixed the confidence-case-sensitivity bug; viableAutoDetectStructures()
score floor; renderAutoScanCandidatePicker() for close-call datasets;
uniqueness folded into default-checked preselection); popup/popup.html
(#auto-scan-candidates picker markup); utils/i18n-data.js (1 new key —
autoMode.multipleDatasets — x6 locales; autoMode.noneDetected reworded
to spec #10's exact phrasing in all 6 languages; 198 total keys, still
100% coverage).

---- Tests added ----
test-v1-autodetect-quality.js (NEW) — 31 assertions, 0 failures,
deterministic (pure synchronous DOM analysis, no timing sensitivity):
TEST 18 is the EXACT regression fixture for the reported real-Chrome
failure (product cards + an item-specifics spec panel elsewhere) and
directly confirms the fix — product cards score 100, the spec panel
scores 0, and none of its labels leak into the winning structure's own
fields. TEST 17.A (listing + sidebar filters/brand facets/pagination),
17.B (product detail page + spec table + related products), 17.D
(article/news list), 17.E (directory listing), 17.G (nav-heavy page
with no real dataset — confirms a low/no-confidence result rather than
a fabricated one), 17.H (two competing datasets close in strength).
TEST 19/19b verify semantic naming (Title/Price/Link/Image/Review
Count all correctly named; an unclassifiable field falls back to
neutral "Field", never the old "Text N", never invented meaning). TEST
20 verifies row-root alignment by re-extracting through the real
scraper engine and confirming zero cross-contamination with the nearby
spec panel. content/autodetect.js's own PRE-EXISTING baseline suite
(test-autodetect.js, 36 assertions) still passes unchanged — confirms
the new scoring signals didn't regress any previously-correct case
(product cards, sidebar-vs-main, HTML tables, missing-field coverage,
duplicate-nested-field dedup, multi-structure ranking, multi-currency
price recognition, generic lists, no-repetition graceful failure,
shadow DOM).

---- Bugs discovered/fixed ----
1. The eBay-reported ranking failure itself (see Root Cause above).
2. The confidence-case-sensitivity bug in popup.js's low-confidence
   note (existed since the AUTO/MANUAL workflow pass; never triggered
   for a real DOM structure until now).
3. Images nested inside a card's primary link were invisible to field
   detection entirely (pre-existing, discovered while building TEST 18/
   19's fixtures — both initially failed to detect Image until fixed).
4. A parenthesis-placement regex bug prevented "(N reviews)"-style
   counts from ever matching the count-recognition pattern.

---- Known limitations (honest — AUTO is NOT claimed universally reliable) ----
- This is heuristic, classical DOM/text-pattern analysis, not AI/ML —
  it will still misdetect on sufficiently unusual page layouts. The
  fixtures here are representative, hand-built HTML, not live captures
  of real e-commerce/news/directory sites; REAL Chrome validation
  against real, currently-live pages (which change their markup over
  time) is the only true test and has NOT been performed by the
  assistant.
- The "attribute/spec-list" penalty is a structural heuristic (no
  links + no images + tight consistency + modest count) — a genuine,
  unusual product-listing pattern that happens to have neither links
  nor images (e.g. a pure text price-list with no clickable rows) could
  still be under-scored. This trade-off was deliberate: the reported
  failure mode (a spec table outscoring real product cards) was judged
  more common and more damaging than this narrower edge case.
- The candidate-picker (spec #3/#11) only ever offers the TOP TWO
  candidates, and only when genuinely close in score — matches the
  spec's own "keep UX simple," but a page with 3+ legitimately
  competing datasets still only surfaces the strongest two.
- "Review Count"/"Location" and other richer semantic names remain
  evidence-gated and conservative by design — many genuinely
  meaningful fields will still correctly fall back to neutral "Field"
  rather than a guessed label, exactly as spec #4 requires ("never
  invent meaning").

---- Manual Chrome retest required ----
See the Final Report delivered alongside this update for the exact
checklist. Nothing in this section — nor anywhere else in this pass —
is marked as manually verified in real Chrome.

============================================================
V1 AUTO DETECTION — CANDIDATE GROUP FORMATION FIX
(follow-up to "V1 AUTO DETECTION IMPROVEMENT" above — that pass fixed
RANKING of an already-formed candidate; the real eBay retest still
failed because the main product cards were never even forming as a
candidate group in the first place, upstream of ranking entirely)
============================================================

---- Why the previous pass didn't fix the real eBay retest ----
The previous pass's fix (scoreCandidate's new signals + the attribute-
list penalty) only matters once a repeating group of elements has
already been identified as ONE candidate. On the real eBay retest, the
product cards were apparently never being grouped into one candidate at
all — a different, upstream bug in candidate GENERATION
(findCandidateGroups/scanRootForGroups), not candidate scoring.

---- Debugging note: no live access to the real page ----
This sandbox's network egress to eBay is blocked (bot-detection 403,
confirmed — general internet access otherwise works). The real page's
exact DOM could not be fetched or inspected directly. Root cause below
was established by code audit of the grouping algorithm plus general,
well-documented knowledge of how real e-commerce search-result markup
behaves, and then CONFIRMED empirically by building fixtures that
reproduce that class of real-world DOM shape and running them against
the unmodified code (see "Root cause CONFIRMED via reproduction
fixtures" below) — not by inspecting eBay's literal current markup.

---- Root cause ----
content/autodetect.js's scanRootForGroups() grouped sibling elements by
an EXACT match of their full combined stable-class SET
(`tag + ':' + allStableClasses.sort().join('.')`). Real product-card
grids very commonly vary a handful of BEM-style modifier/badge classes
across only SOME cards on the same page — sponsored/promoted, watched,
best-match, price-drop, free-returns, top-rated, and similar per-item
state badges are near-universal on real e-commerce search results. Any
such difference between two otherwise-identical cards produced a
DIFFERENT full-set signature string, so the exact-match grouping
fragmented what is structurally ONE repeating dataset into many small,
weaker sub-groups — in the worst case, small enough that none of them
individually cleared the size->=2 grouping threshold, meaning the real
product listing never appeared as a candidate AT ALL.

---- Root cause CONFIRMED via reproduction fixtures ----
test-v1-autodetect-realworld-classvariance.js (NEW) builds fixtures with
per-card class variability instead of the identical-class-list shape
every prior fixture in this project used, and runs them against the
UNMODIFIED (pre-fix) code:
  FIXTURE 1 (moderate/realistic — ~15% of cards carry one badge class):
    largest group found = 32 of 40 real cards (20% silently lost).
  FIXTURE 2 (severe — every card carries a different realistic badge
    COMBINATION, still well within real-world plausibility):
    largest group found = 12 of 40 (70% lost) — fragmented into 8+
    separate weak sub-candidates (12/5/5/4/4/3/2/2).
  FIXTURE 3 (worst case — every card carries one unique per-item class
    token): largest group found = 0 — the real dataset did not appear
    as a candidate at all, reproducing the reported symptom exactly.
  FIXTURE 4 (control — identical classes, the shape of every pre-
    existing fixture in this project): unaffected, 40/40 as expected —
    confirms the harness itself is sound and this is specifically about
    class-list variability, not a generic regression.

---- The fix ----
1. content/autodetect.js — scanRootForGroups() no longer requires an
   exact full-class-set match. Each child is now bucketed by
   tag + EACH INDIVIDUAL stable class it carries (a card with classes
   {s-item, s-item--ads} joins BOTH the "s-item" bucket and the
   "s-item--ads" bucket), plus a tag-only fallback bucket for elements
   with zero stable classes. This is a pure GENERALIZATION, not a
   narrower replacement: when every sibling shares an identical full
   class set (the old common case, and every pre-existing fixture),
   each shared class's own bucket already reconstructs the exact same
   membership as before — nothing that used to group together can stop
   grouping together. Trade-off accepted deliberately: a generic
   utility class shared by unrelated siblings of the SAME parent can
   now also form a (weak) candidate group; it still has to survive
   scoring like any other candidate, so it simply loses rather than
   being pre-filtered at this stage. Bounded cost: still governed by
   the same MAX_SCAN_ELEMENTS/MAX_TIME_MS budgets; per-node cost rises
   by a small constant factor (a card's own stable-class count, usually
   1-4), not a complexity-class change.
   content/autodetect.js's OTHER use of the exact-match childSignature()
   (table row-grouping, detectTableStructures) is UNCHANGED — table rows
   are structurally much more uniform in practice and this is a
   deliberately narrow, surgical fix scoped to the repeating-group scan
   where the real failure was confirmed.
2. content/selector.js — isLikelyDynamicClass() gets one additional,
   narrow check: a flat (no hyphen/underscore/digit), all-lowercase-
   letter class of 5+ characters with NO vowels at all (a/e/i/o/u/y) is
   now treated as likely-dynamic. Real, human-chosen English class names
   ("grid"/"card"/"nav"/"wrap"/"list"/"item"/"price"/"title" and
   virtually every other short real word) always contain at least one
   vowel; this specific shape is the signature of a per-instance CSS-
   module/component-library class that happens to draw only letters
   (not caught by the existing hex/digit-ratio checks). Kept narrow
   (flat tokens only) specifically to avoid false-positiving on
   legitimate short class names — every existing selector.js/
   autodetect.js test (152 assertions across 6 files) still passes
   unchanged, confirming no real class name was affected. This is
   defense-in-depth for the case where a card's ENTIRE class list is
   noise (falls back to the tag-only bucket in scanRootForGroups above)
   — fix #1 above is what resolves the reported failure on its own; #2
   is a genuine complementary hardening, not the decisive fix (the
   reproduction fixtures' own tokens happened to contain vowels by
   construction, so fix #1 alone is what flipped Fixture 3 to passing —
   documented honestly rather than overstating #2's contribution).

---- Result: same 4 fixtures, now against the FIXED code ----
Per the explicit instruction to not modify the reproduction fixtures —
they were re-run completely unchanged. All 4 now report the FULL,
correct 40-item count and the product listing (not the spec panel)
winning: FIXTURE 1: 40/40. FIXTURE 2: 40/40. FIXTURE 3: 40/40 (was 0).
FIXTURE 4 (control): 40/40, unaffected. 8/8 assertions pass (was 5/8).

---- Files changed ----
content/autodetect.js (scanRootForGroups() grouping algorithm — see
fix #1 above; childSignature() itself and its other call site in
detectTableStructures are unchanged). content/selector.js
(isLikelyDynamicClass() — see fix #2 above).
test-v1-autodetect-realworld-classvariance.js is a NEW test file added
in the prior debugging step and was NOT modified by this fix (per
instruction) — it now passes because the production code changed under
it, exactly as intended for a regression test written before its fix.

---- Tests run ----
- test-v1-autodetect-realworld-classvariance.js: 8/8 (was 5/8 against
  the pre-fix code — see above).
- content/autodetect.js's full existing suite (test-autodetect.js): 36/36
  unchanged — no regression to any previously-correct case (product

============================================================
V1 AUTO DETECTION — NAV-PENALTY / MAIN-BONUS COUPLING FIX
(root cause PROVEN by the real Chrome diagnostic output — the third
and, per the evidence, actual root cause of the ongoing eBay failure)
============================================================

---- What the real Chrome diagnostic showed ----
Candidate 125: the real 60-item eBay product dataset. 100% link
presence, 100% image presence, 100% price-like presence — as strong a
"this is the dataset" signal as exists. Scored only 66 because
navigationPenaltyApplied=true AND mainContentBonusApplied=false.
Candidate 77: a 9-item Brand/Item-Specific FILTER ACCORDION — no
images, no prices, no links. Scored 79 and won.

---- Root cause ----
Two compounding bugs in content/autodetect.js's scoring, both
GENERIC (not eBay-specific):
1. `var mainBonus = !navPenalty && isInMainLikeAncestor(repEl);` — the
   main-content bonus was structurally IMPOSSIBLE to receive whenever
   any nav penalty applied, for ANY reason. One ancestor-keyword
   coincidence therefore cost a candidate BOTH the -30 penalty AND the
   +10 bonus it would otherwise have earned — a 40-point swing from a
   single signal, on any site, not just eBay.
2. `isInNavLikeAncestor()`'s keyword list included a bare "ad" — a
   2-letter standalone token climbed up to 8 ancestor levels. On a
   real, large, deeply-nested page, SOME ancestor within that climb
   coincidentally containing "ad" as an isolated id/class token (for
   reasons unrelated to navigation or advertising) is common, not
   rare — this is what most likely tipped candidate 125's real
   ancestor chain into a false penalty. Whichever exact ancestor
   triggered it, the underlying design flaw (a single short, generic
   keyword match anywhere in an 8-level climb carrying the SAME -30
   weight as being inside an actual `<nav>` element) is real
   regardless of the literal class name involved.

---- The fix (algorithmic, not eBay-specific) ----
1. mainBonus is now computed FULLY INDEPENDENTLY of navPenalty — a
   candidate can show both signals at once; one no longer silently
   cancels the other.
2. The nav-ancestor match is now GRADED: 'semantic' (a real <nav>/
   <header>/<footer>/<aside>/ARIA-landmark ancestor — high confidence,
   kept at the full -30) vs. 'keyword' (an id/class text coincidence —
   materially less reliable, now only -12). The bare "ad" token was
   removed from the keyword list entirely (too generic; "advert"/
   "promo"/"banner"/"sponsor" — all real, specific words — remain).
   The keyword-climb depth was reduced from 8 to 4 ancestor levels — a
   genuine nav/sidebar/footer wrapper is almost always a close
   ancestor; the further up the tree a coincidence is found, the less
   likely it describes the candidate's own role.
3. NEW explicit "strong product signature" counter-bonus (+20): when
   link presence, image presence, AND price-like presence are ALL
   >=80% across >=10 repeated items, this fires regardless of any
   ancestor-keyword coincidence — directly implements "a repeating
   dataset with strong product-like signals must strongly outrank
   filter/navigation/specification groups with no images/prices."
   Purely signal-based (link/image/price ratios + item count already
   computed for every candidate) — no site/class name involved.
A genuine `<nav>`/`<aside>`-wrapped weak group (no real content
signals) still receives the full -30 and still correctly loses — this
fix narrows the false-positive surface, it does not remove nav-penalty
capability (verified by a dedicated regression test — see below).

---- Files changed ----
content/autodetect.js only: NAV_KEYWORD_RE ("ad" removed),
NAV_KEYWORD_CLIMB_DEPTH (8->4, new constant), isInNavLikeAncestor()
replaced by navAncestorMatchKind() (graded return value), and
computeCandidateSignals() (mainBonus decoupled, graded nav-penalty
weight, new strongProductSignature bonus). The dev-only diagnostic
report gained two new fields (navigationPenaltyKind,
strongProductSignatureBonusApplied) purely by exposing values already
computed. No changes to popup.js, popup.html, Manual Mode, or any
other file. MAX_SCAN_ELEMENTS unchanged. No eBay-specific selectors,
class names, or hostname checks anywhere in this fix.

---- Tests added ----
test-v1-autodetect-navpenalty-coupling.js (NEW) — 15 assertions, 0
failures: TEST 1 reproduces the exact failure shape generically (a
60-item, 100%-link/image/price product grid wrapped by an ancestor
whose class merely contains an "ad"-like token unrelated to
navigation, competing against a 9-item, zero-signal filter accordion)
and confirms the product grid now wins outright (score 100 in the
fixture, up from what would have been 66). TEST 2 inspects the
diagnostic's own signal breakdown for the winner, directly confirming
WHY it wins now (100% link/image/price ratios recorded,
navigationPenaltyKind is NOT 'semantic', strongProductSignatureBonus-
Applied=true, mainContentBonusApplied=true — no longer suppressed).
TEST 3 is the regression guard: a genuine `<aside>`-wrapped weak group
is still correctly flagged navigationPenaltyKind='semantic' and still
correctly loses to a real `<main>`-wrapped product listing — proves
the fix narrows the false positive without gutting real nav-penalty
detection.

---- Tests run ----
Every existing autodetect fixture file re-run and unchanged: 36+31+8
+26+15 = 116 assertions across test-autodetect.js/test-v1-autodetect-
quality.js/test-v1-autodetect-realworld-classvariance.js/
test-v1-autodetect-diagnostic.js/test-v1-autodetect-diagnostic-popup.js
— including the original eBay item-specifics regression (spec panel
must still lose) and the class-variance fixtures (badge/modifier
class tolerance must still work) — both still hold correctly after
this pass. Full accumulated suite: 86 files, 2,627 assertions total —
0 failures outside the same already-documented, pre-existing flaky
popup test files (unrelated: grep-confirmed none of them load
content/autodetect.js). release-check.js: 17/17.

Manifest 1.27.4.
