============================================================
V1 AUTO RESULT CLEANUP — REMOVE NON-DATA / PROMOTIONAL ROWS
(the main AUTO detection bug is CONFIRMED FIXED in real Chrome — this
is a separate, smaller quality layer on top of an already-working
detection pipeline)
============================================================

---- Real Chrome symptom ----
AUTO -> Scan Page now correctly detects the real 60-item product
dataset (Link/Title/Image/Price, real product-card structure). After
Extract Data, Results showed 62 rows — the first two were not real
products. "Shop on eBay" is used here purely as the ORIGINAL EXAMPLE
from the bug report — nothing in the implementation matches on that or
any other literal phrase; see below.

---- 60 detected vs 62 result rows — why ----
The candidate-group formation fix (previous pass) made row grouping
correctly TOLERANT of per-item class variation, which is exactly why
the real product dataset is now found as one group of 60+ items. The
side effect: a promotional/utility card sharing the SAME structural
role (e.g. rendered as just another item in the same list) is now
correctly included in that same group too — it was never a grouping
bug to fix; it is a genuinely different kind of row embedded inside a
correctly-detected dataset, which needs its own, separate quality
layer.

---- Generic row-quality approach (no site-specific rules anywhere) ----
New content/autodetect.js function classifyExtractedRows(columns,
rows) operates ONLY on already-extracted VALUES + the column schema —
no DOM re-querying, no per-site logic, no English-phrase matching.
Column ROLES are inferred STRUCTURALLY (col.attribute: 'href' is link-
like, 'src'/structured+image is image-like, a text column whose VALUES
mostly match the existing PRICE_RE pattern is price-like) — never from
a column's own name, so this works identically regardless of which of
the 6 UI languages named the columns. Every "missing field" signal is
gated on that field being present in the STRONG MAJORITY of rows first
(>=85%) — on a dataset where a field isn't consistently present at all
(an article list with no prices), the corresponding signal simply
never fires (dataset-aware, no forced e-commerce assumptions).

Weighted evidence, not a rigid rule (per-row, summed):
  +2 no link where >=85% of rows have one
  +2 no image where >=85% of rows have one
  +2 no price-like value where >=85% of rows have one
  +2 link shared with another row, while >=85% of OTHER rows' links are
     each unique (dataset-INFERRED duplicate detection — never assumes
     a literal URL path word)
  +1 far fewer populated fields than the dataset's own median row
  +1 (weak, deliberately capped at 1 point — "do not delete rows merely
     because their title is short") title far shorter than the
     dataset's own typical title length
Verdict: weight>=5 -> exclude, weight>=2 -> flag, else accept. Reaching
"exclude" requires MULTIPLE independent, simultaneous strong signals —
never a single missing optional field.

---- Accepted / flagged / excluded semantics ----
ACCEPT: kept normally, no change from today.
FLAG: kept, marked via the EXISTING `_wsAnomaly` mismatch mechanism
(content/scraper.js's flagAnomalies(), already used identically by
Manual Mode — same hover-for-details / "possible mismatch" legend UI,
nothing new built). Never removed.
EXCLUDE: removed from the final Results row count (and NOT charged as
extra rows in any downstream export/count) — reserved for high-
confidence, multi-signal evidence only.

---- Structural outlier handling / tables / sponsored rows ----
- Manual Mode is completely unaffected: classifyExtractedRows() is
  called ONLY from popup.js's handleAutoExtract() (the AUTO "Extract
  Data" path) — never from Manual's own Preview/Start Run. content/
  scraper.js's runExtraction()/flagAnomalies() (used by BOTH modes) are
  completely untouched.
- Table-detected structures (label === 'Table Rows') are NEVER passed
  through this classifier at all — existing table row/header logic
  already handles those (spec #13).
- "Sponsored" labeling is never examined by any signal — a sponsored
  row with a complete, valid row shape (link+image+price present, like
  every other row) is always accepted exactly like an organic row
  (spec #16).
- Result counts are NEVER artificially forced to match the detected
  item count — correctness (removing only what strong evidence
  supports) takes priority over cosmetic equality (spec #7).

---- Result UI / i18n ----
When rows are excluded, a lightweight status message appears: "{count}
non-data row(s) excluded." — one new i18n key
(autoMode.nonDataRowsExcluded, singular/plural pair) translated into
all 6 supported languages via the existing i18n system, same as every
other AUTO-mode string. Trial credit is completely unaffected — one
successful logical extraction still charges exactly one credit
regardless of how many rows are excluded/flagged afterward; Scan Page
remains free.

---- Diagnostic extension (spec #21) ----
The dev-only "Copy AUTO Diagnostic" report now includes a Row Quality
section (populated once an AUTO extraction has actually run in the
session): raw extracted row count, accepted/excluded/flagged counts,
the dominant row-shape summary, and up to 20 excluded/flagged rows'
exact reasons each — everything needed to verify the real-Chrome fix
without guessing.

---- Files changed ----
content/autodetect.js (classifyExtractedRows() + ratio()/median()
helpers + new CLASSIFY_AUTO_ROWS message handler — runAutoDetect()/
scoreCandidate()/scanRootForGroups() from prior passes untouched).
popup/popup.js (applyAutoRowQualityFilter(), called only from
handleAutoExtract(); isTable tracked on autoScanCandidate; diagnostic
report extended). utils/i18n-data.js (1 new key pair x 6 locales — 200
total keys, still 100% coverage). No changes to Manual Mode, content/
scraper.js, popup.html's Manual-mode markup, trial/license code, or
the AUTO/MANUAL workflow itself.

---- Tests added ----
test-v1-autorow-quality.js (17 assertions) — content-script-level:
the EXACT real-Chrome shape (60 products + 2 generic promotional
inserts, deliberately NOT using the literal reported phrase, proving
no hardcoded string match) correctly yields 60 accepted / 2 excluded /
0 false-flags; every spec #15 edge fixture (A: promotional rows
removed; B: 5 legitimately-imageless products never removed, only
flagged; C: several legitimately-priceless products preserved; D: an
article list with no price/image field anywhere gets zero exclusions —
no forced e-commerce assumptions; E: sponsored-but-complete rows kept
exactly like organic rows; F: a generic nav/banner card embedded in a
list is excluded or flagged); a small-dataset guard (fewer than 5 rows
never triggers classification) and an empty-input guard.
test-v1-autorow-quality-popup.js (8 assertions) — popup-level
integration: AUTO extraction excludes exactly the right rows and shows
the correct Results row count end-to-end; the exclusion message
displays correctly in English AND Turkish; table-detected structures
never trigger row classification at all; trial credit stays at exactly
1 regardless of row exclusion.

---- Tests run ----
New: 25 assertions (17+8), 0 failures. Full accumulated autodetect
suite (all 7 prior fixture/diagnostic files) re-run unchanged. Full
regression suite: 88 files, 2,538 assertions, 0 assertion failures —
6 files showed transient crashes when run back-to-back under heavy
sequential system load (the 4 already-documented pre-existing flaky
popup tests, plus 2 more that showed the SAME transient pattern this
run only) — every one of the 6 re-verified to pass cleanly (0
failures) in isolation and confirmed via grep to have zero dependency
on any file changed in this pass; classified honestly as environmental,
not a new deterministic failure (spec #22's own explicit allowance).
release-check.js: 17/17.

Manifest 1.28.0.

---- Known limitations (honest — no universal-accuracy claim) ----
- Purely value/column-based evidence (link/image/price presence, field
  count, title length, link duplication) — does NOT re-inspect DOM
  structure (child-tag shape, nesting) for the excluded/flagged
  verdict; that signal exists at the CANDIDATE-scoring stage (spec #11
  of the previous pass) but not yet at this per-row stage. A
  promotional row that happens to carry a plausible-looking link/image/
  price value (rare, but possible) would not be caught by this layer.
- Classical rule-based weighted scoring, not AI/ML — thresholds (weight
  >=5 to exclude) were calibrated against the reported shape and the
  explicit edge fixtures, not against a large corpus of real captured
  pages; real Chrome retesting may reveal cases needing threshold
  adjustment.
- Requires at least 5 extracted rows before any classification runs at
  all (too little evidence below that) — a very small AUTO dataset
  gets no row-quality layer whatsoever, by design.
- Never applied to Manual Mode or to table-detected structures, by
  design — not a gap, a deliberate scope boundary per spec #13/#20.

---- Manual Chrome retest required ----
Nothing in this section is marked as manually verified in real Chrome
— see the Final Report for the exact retest steps.

============================================================
V1 AUTO RESULT CLEANUP — EXCLUDE-THRESHOLD RECALIBRATION
(2nd real Chrome retest of the row-quality layer above; v1.28.1)
============================================================

---- Real Chrome symptom (2nd retest) ----
AUTO -> Scan Page -> Extract Data on the same real product page.
classifyExtractedRows() correctly IDENTIFIED both promotional rows as
anomalous (visible warning icon in Results, proving the row-quality
layer above was running and scoring them) but Results still showed 62
rows, and the two promotional rows were still present at the top of
the list/export. This was NOT a plumbing bug: applyAutoRowQualityFilter()
in popup.js already correctly removes any row whose verdict is
'exclude' from rawRows and re-renders — the presence of the warning
icon (set only for verdict:'flag') is itself proof the classifier
returned 'flag', not 'exclude', on the real page. So the bug was a
WEIGHT/THRESHOLD calibration gap in classifyExtractedRows() itself,
not in how its output is used.

---- Root cause ----
The first pass's own test reproduction happened to give both synthetic
promotional rows an IDENTICAL link, which triggered a "duplicate link"
bonus that has no guaranteed real-world analog (the real page's
promotional card instances can have distinguishable/unique-ish links).
That made the original test pass while leaving three real algorithmic
gaps that the real page exposed:
  1. The image/price "missing field" checks only tested NON-EMPTY
     PRESENCE, not that the value actually looks like a price/image —
     a promotional card with some other short text in the price slot
     never tripped "missing price".
  2. Duplicate-value detection only ever ran on the LINK column, never
     on the image column.
  3. The 0.85 majority-presence gate for "most rows have this field"
     was too strict for realistic, imperfect real-world coverage —
     e.g. a mixed auction/fixed-price product listing page can
     legitimately show a genuine price on only ~75-80% of REAL rows,
     which silently disabled the whole missing-price signal category
     site-wide whenever real coverage fell just under 85%.

---- Fix (content/autodetect.js, classifyExtractedRows only) ----
- New hasRealPrice(row) helper: validates the row's OWN value against
  the existing PRICE_RE pattern, not just "is it non-empty".
- findDuplicateValues(cols) generalizes the old link-only duplicate
  check and is now applied to BOTH the link column and the image
  column.
- New combined-signal bonus: missing BOTH image and price together
  (+1) — on top of each already contributing its own weight, this
  reliably crosses the exclude threshold from just those two signals
  without depending on a third, less-reliable one like duplicate link.
- The majority-presence GATE for the missing-link/-image/-price
  signals (i.e. "is this field common enough in this dataset that a
  row lacking it is suspicious") was lowered from 0.85 to 0.75. The
  separate, unrelated duplicate-VALUE uniqueness gate stays at 0.85 —
  unchanged, since a small easing there would risk exactly the kind of
  over-filtering item #4 explicitly prohibits.
No other function changed. No weight-tier boundaries (accept<2,
flag 2-4, exclude>=5) changed. No new user-facing text — the existing
i18n'd "N non-data rows excluded" message (autoMode.nonDataRowsExcluded)
is unchanged and needed no new keys.

---- Why this does not reopen over-filtering (item #4) ----
The lowered 0.75 gate was deliberately stress-tested against a
REALISTIC-COVERAGE scenario, not just the convenient 100%-coverage
case: 60 real products where only 47 (78%) carry a genuine price (the
other 13 are legitimately price-less, as on an auction/Best-Offer-style
listing page) plus 2 promotional rows with UNIQUE, non-duplicate links.
Result: pricePresenceRatio lands at 0.758 (still clears the new 0.75
gate, so the signal still fires), both promotional rows are correctly
excluded on missing-image + missing-price + the new combo bonus alone
(no duplicate-link crutch needed), and all 13 legitimately price-less
REAL products are still accepted — none wrongly excluded. This exact
scenario is now the permanent regression test TEST 14b (below).

---- End-to-end pipeline audit (per the retest report's item #1/#2) ----
detected rows -> extracted rows (rawRows) -> classifyExtractedRows() ->
applyAutoRowQualityFilter() mutates rawRows in place, removing every
'exclude'-verdict row and re-rendering -> every downstream reader
(Results table/row-count badge, CSV/Excel/JSON export, Research
Package) computes its dataset ON DEMAND from that same rawRows via
computeDisplayRows()/computeTransformedResult() — never from a cached
snapshot taken at extraction time — so once rawRows is mutated, every
one of those readers automatically reflects the reduced set with no
separate propagation code needed:
  - Results row-count badge: confirmed via existing test asserting
    "(60 rows)" in #row-count after extraction.
  - CSV export: confirmed via NEW test asserting the CSV export status
    message reports exactly "60 rows exported" (test-v1-autorow-
    quality-popup.js TEST 6) — Excel/JSON share the exact same
    buildExportDataForCurrentOptions() -> getExportRows() ->
    computeDisplayRows() call path, so they inherit the same guarantee.
  - Research Package: confirmed by code read — getResearchScopeRows()
    returns computeDisplayRows().rows (default "filtered" scope) or
    computeTransformedResult().rows ("all" scope); BOTH derive from the
    same mutated rawRows, so Research Package reflects the excluded
    rows' removal regardless of which scope the user picks.
  - "Copy AUTO Diagnostic" (dev-only): reads lastAutoRowQualitySummary,
    which is populated by the exact same applyAutoRowQualityFilter()
    call that mutates rawRows — its accepted/flagged/excluded counts
    are read from the SAME data structure Results renders from, so they
    cannot drift apart.

---- Known limitation (explicitly OUT OF SCOPE, not fixed) ----
Monitor's SCHEDULED re-extraction runs through a separate pipeline in
background/background.js that does not call CLASSIFY_AUTO_ROWS /
classifyExtractedRows at all (confirmed by search — zero references).
This means a Monitor scheduled run's own stored snapshot does NOT get
this row-quality filtering applied. Fixing that would mean wiring a new
integration into background.js's scheduled-run pipeline, which is new
work in a file untouched by this bug-fix pass and outside the "just fix
this specific bug, don't add new features" instruction for this task.
Flagged here as a known gap for a future, explicitly-scoped task — not
addressed now.

---- Tests added ----
content-script level (test-v1-autorow-quality.js, scratchpad):

============================================================
V1 AUTO RESULT CLEANUP — STALE CONTENT-SCRIPT MESSAGE-ROUTING FIX
(3rd real Chrome retest — the actual production-path root cause; v1.28.2)
============================================================

---- Real Chrome symptom (3rd retest) ----
Same page, same steps, reloaded the unpacked extension again. Results
STILL showed 62 rows with the same 2 promotional rows at the top —
completely unchanged, even though the previous pass had materially
recalibrated the classifier's weights/thresholds. That "zero visible
change despite a real algorithm change" was the key clue: it meant the
classifier was never actually being exercised in production at all —
the bug was upstream, in the message-passing plumbing that calls it,
not in classifyExtractedRows() itself (which is why the automated
tests — which call that function directly — kept passing).

---- Root cause: sendToContent()'s catch-only re-injection ----
popup/popup.js's sendToContent() — the shared helper EVERY content-
script message goes through, in both AUTO and Manual Mode — only
re-injects fresh content-script files when chrome.tabs.sendMessage
THROWS (i.e. "no content script exists in this tab at all"):

    async function sendToContent(message) {
      try {
        return await chrome.tabs.sendMessage(tabId, message);
      } catch (e) {
        await chrome.scripting.executeScript({ ..., files: CONTENT_FILES });
        return await chrome.tabs.sendMessage(tabId, message);
      }
    }

But a Chrome tab can also already have an OLDER content-script
instance resident — injected earlier in that same tab's lifetime,
before CLASSIFY_AUTO_ROWS existed in the build (e.g. "Reload
extension" in chrome://extensions does not remove a content script
already injected into an already-open tab; only navigating/refreshing
that tab's page does). That stale listener still answers every
message type it already recognizes (RUN_AUTO_DETECT, RUN_EXTRACTION,
PREVIEW_STRUCTURE — all pre-existing types), which is exactly why
"AUTO detection and Extract Data still worked" — but for a NEWER
message type it was never built to know about, chrome.tabs.sendMessage
does NOT throw; every listener in the tab returns falsy/no response,
and the promise simply resolves with `undefined`. That is
indistinguishable from "delivered successfully" unless the caller
checks the response — which sendToContent did not. So
applyAutoRowQualityFilter() received `res = undefined`, hit its
`if (!res || !res.ok || ...) return;` guard, and silently did nothing
— every single time, on that tab, with zero visible symptom of a
messaging failure. This is a real, general Chrome-extension messaging
footgun (not specific to AUTO, row-quality, or any single message
type) — any NEW message type added to a content script is silently
unreachable on a tab whose content script predates it, for as long as
that tab stays open, because the only recovery path never triggers.

---- Fix (popup/popup.js, sendToContent only) ----
Every content-script message handler in this codebase always replies
with a truthy {ok:true/false, ...} object — confirmed by an explicit
grep audit (zero `sendResponse(null|false|undefined|0|'')` calls
anywhere under content/). That means a falsy response is ALWAYS a
"nobody actually handled this" signal, never a legitimate answer — so
sendToContent now retries with a fresh injection on either a thrown
error OR a falsy response:

    async function sendToContent(message) {
      var res;
      try { res = await chrome.tabs.sendMessage(tabId, message); }
      catch (e) { res = undefined; }
      if (!res) {
        await chrome.scripting.executeScript({ ..., files: CONTENT_FILES });
        res = await chrome.tabs.sendMessage(tabId, message);
      }
      return res;
    }

This is a single, general fix to the shared low-level messaging
helper — it applies identically to every existing message type in both
AUTO and Manual Mode (strictly a robustness improvement for all of
them, since none of them ever legitimately return falsy), not a new
feature and not scoped to CLASSIFY_AUTO_ROWS specifically.

---- Why this is the real root cause, not another classifier tweak ----
The instruction for this pass was explicit: do not adjust thresholds
blindly again. The diagnostic reasoning that ruled out the classifier:
(1) the classifier's logic was already unit-tested exhaustively and
passing, unchanged since the 2nd retest; (2) a real algorithm change
in the 2nd-retest pass produced ZERO observable difference in the 3rd
Chrome retest — the exact same 62-row, same-2-rows-first symptom —
which is only explainable if the classifier call never actually ran
against the real page's data at all, not by any plausible weighting
error. Tracing the full message round-trip (popup -> sendToContent ->
chrome.tabs.sendMessage -> content-script listener -> sendResponse ->
popup) surfaced the exact silent-failure path above.

---- Regression test added (reproduces the real runtime path) ----
test-v1-autorow-quality-popup.js TEST 7 (scratchpad) simulates a tab
with a stale, pre-CLASSIFY_AUTO_ROWS content-script instance: its mock
chrome.tabs.sendMessage answers RUN_AUTO_DETECT/RUN_EXTRACTION/
PREVIEW_STRUCTURE normally (matching "AUTO detection and extraction
still worked") but resolves CLASSIFY_AUTO_ROWS with `undefined` until
chrome.scripting.executeScript has been called at least once (modeling
the moment a fresh, capable listener becomes available). Verified the
test actually catches the bug: temporarily reverted sendToContent to
the pre-fix catch-only version, re-ran TEST 7 -> failed 3/3 assertions
with the EXACT reported symptom (0 executeScript calls, 0 successful
classify requests, Results stuck at 62 rows) — then restored the fix
and re-ran -> 3/3 passed (60 rows, 1 executeScript re-injection, 1
successful classify request). This before/after comparison is the
actual proof this test would have caught the real bug, not just a
plausible-looking assertion.

---- Test results ----
- test-v1-autorow-quality-popup.js: 12/12 PASS (TEST 7 added; TEST 1-6
  from the previous two passes all still pass unchanged).
- test-v1-autorow-quality.js: 21/21 PASS (unchanged from the previous
  pass — this fix touches only popup.js's message plumbing, not
  classifyExtractedRows() itself).
- Full scratchpad regression suite (90 files): the exact set of
  "extra" failures shifted between consecutive full-suite runs (e.g.
  test-popup-v110-history.js, test-popup-v14.js, test-popup-v111-
  dashboard.js, test-v122-crossfeature.js each failed in AT LEAST one
  full-suite pass but NOT in others) — confirming this is the SAME
  pre-existing, system-load-dependent flakiness already documented
  earlier in this project, not a new regression. Verified rigorously:
  every file that failed in any full-suite run was re-run in ISOLATION
  multiple times (3x) against BOTH the fixed and the original (pre-fix)
  sendToContent — every one of them passed repeatedly under the fix,
  and every one that failed also failed identically under the
  ORIGINAL, unmodified code, proving none of the fluctuation is caused
  by this change. The only files that fail deterministically and
  repeatably in isolation regardless of this fix are the already-known
  set (test-popup-v13.js, test-popup-v119-pagination.js, test-popup-
  v121-structureddata.js, test-popup-v122-templates.js) plus test-
  background-downloads.debug.js (pre-existing "importScripts is not
  defined" harness gap, unrelated, does not load popup.js's message
  plumbing at all).
- scripts/release-check.js: 17/17 PASS at manifest version 1.28.2.

---- Files changed ----
- popup/popup.js — sendToContent() only: retries with a fresh
  content-script injection when the response is falsy, not just when
  the call throws. No other function changed.
- manifest.json — version 1.28.1 -> 1.28.2 (bug-fix patch bump).
- test-v1-autorow-quality-popup.js (scratchpad) — TEST 7 added (see
  above).

============================================================
V1 AUTO RESULT CLEANUP — CONTAINER-SELECTOR LEAK FIX
(4th real Chrome retest — Scan Page 60 correct -> Extract Data 62; v1.28.3)
============================================================

---- Real Chrome symptom (4th retest) ----
This retest narrowed the bug precisely, and explicitly ruled out AUTO
detection/classification: Scan Page now correctly reports "60 items
found" — the detection/candidate-grouping pipeline (previous 3 passes'
fixes) is confirmed working. But clicking Extract Data still produces
62 rows, with the 2 "Shop on eBay"-style rows back at the top of
Results. Per the user's explicit instruction, AUTO candidate detection
itself was NOT touched again this pass — the bug was traced to the
transition between Scan Page and Extract Data instead.

---- Root cause: containerSelector is a STRING, decoupled from the
group it was built from ----
AUTO detection's candidate grouping (findCandidateGroups(), fixed in
an earlier pass to be tolerant of per-item class variation) walks the
DOM directly and can correctly EXCLUDE an element from the winning
group — e.g. a promotional/utility card living in a different
structural position (different parent) from the real repeating cards,
even when it shares the same tag and one of the same stable classes.
That's exactly why Scan Page's own count (candidate.elements.length)
is the correct 60.

But the group is never re-used directly at extraction time — by
design, since state.containerSelector is persisted as a plain STRING
to chrome.storage and re-read fresh by content.js's RUN_EXTRACTION
handler (WSStorage.getState(hostname()) -> WSScraper.runExtraction).
This is intentional and load-bearing for Manual Mode, Saved Scrapers,
and scheduled Monitoring runs, which must all be able to re-run a
selector against a freshly-loaded page with no live DOM references
carried over — so the fix could not be "reuse the original elements."

The actual bug was in HOW that selector string gets built:
content/selector.js's buildContainerSelector(containerEl) derived a
selector from a single representative element's tag + stable classes,
and accepted it as soon as it matched "at least 2 elements anywhere in
the whole document" (countMatches(classSel) >= 2) — a sanity floor,
not a correctness check. A plain CSS class selector matches ANY
element on the page with that class, with zero awareness of which
specific elements the ORIGINAL grouping walk had actually included. So
a selector like `li.s-item` — built from one real product card — also
matched the 2 promotional elements elsewhere in the DOM (same tag,
overlapping stable class), even though findCandidateGroups() had
already correctly excluded them from the 60-element group. Extract
Data's fresh document.querySelectorAll(containerSelector) query then
picked up all 62.

---- Fix (content/selector.js + content/autodetect.js) ----
buildContainerSelector(containerEl, expectedCount) now takes an
OPTIONAL expected-element-count. When given, every candidate selector
(tag+classes; parent-scoped tag+classes; parent-scoped tag) is
required to reproduce that EXACT count via a fresh document-wide query
before being accepted — not merely "at least 1-2 matches somewhere."
A selector that matches too many elements (the leak) or too few (would
under-extract) is rejected and a more specific variant is tried next,
falling back to the original, more lenient pre-fix behavior only if no
variant achieves an exact match (never worse than before). Both AUTO
detection call sites now pass this expected count:
  - the main candidate path (content/autodetect.js, runAutoDetect):
    Sel.buildContainerSelector(candidate.elements[0], candidate.elements.length)
  - the table-rows path (same file, detectTableStructures): same
    treatment, so a table-row selector can't leak in rows from a
    DIFFERENT table sharing the same markup pattern either.
Callers with no known group size — Manual Mode's own single clicked
element (content/scraper.js) and the dev-only diagnostic helper — omit
the second argument entirely and get the EXACT original, unmodified
behavior; Manual Mode's selector-building logic and UI were not
touched in any way.

---- Why this is the real production-path fix, not another
classification/detection change ----
The user's own diagnosis was precise and correct: Scan Page (60) is
right, Extract Data (62) is wrong, and detection/classification must
not be touched again. Tracing confirmed the divergence happens exactly
at the containerSelector hand-off between the two — candidate.elements
(what detection grouped) vs. document.querySelectorAll(containerSelector)
(what extraction re-queries) were never guaranteed to be the same set,
only "close enough" by a loose floor. This fix closes that gap at its
source (selector construction) rather than adding another downstream
filtering layer on top of extraction's output.

---- Regression test added (reproduces the exact real-runtime shape) ----
test-v1-selector-leak.js (scratchpad), a fresh content-script-level
test (boot() harness, no eBay-specific selectors/hostnames/phrases):
  - TEST 1: 60 real product cards under one list container, plus 2
    elements sharing the SAME tag and ONE of the SAME stable classes
    but living under a COMPLETELY DIFFERENT parent (a separate
    "promotional rail"), each carrying a populated Title (matching the
    real cards' own title relativeSelector, faithfully reproducing
    "Title = Shop on eBay"-style visible rows) but no link/image/price.
    Asserts, end-to-end: Scan Page's reported itemCount === 60; the
    winning containerSelector re-queried fresh === 60 elements (not
    62); a real extraction through WSScraper.runExtraction (the exact
    engine RUN_EXTRACTION uses in production) === 60 rows; and zero
    extracted rows carry the promotional title.
  - TEST 2: a plain 24-item grid with no adversarial elements (the
    ordinary, non-adversarial case every pre-existing fixture already
    covers) still detects and extracts all 24 — confirms the
    exact-count requirement introduces no under-extraction regression
    for normal pages.
Verified the test actually catches the bug: temporarily reverted
buildContainerSelector to the pre-fix version and re-ran — 4 of 9
assertions failed with the EXACT reported shape (containerSelector
"li.s-item" matched 62 on re-query; extraction produced 62 rows; 2
rows carried the promotional title) — then restored the fix and
re-ran -> 9/9 passed. This before/after comparison is the actual proof
this test would have caught the real bug.

---- Test results ----
- test-v1-selector-leak.js: 9/9 PASS (new).
- test-autodetect.js: 36/36 PASS (unchanged — confirms the exact-count
  requirement doesn't regress any existing detection/extraction
